**Movers App — Master Backlog + Decisions Log**

**Updated 2026-10-08 v2, pushed 2026-10-09 (THE SENDING IDENTITY + ENQUIRY ROUTES SESSION. Chat spanned 10-08 morning to 10-09 evening, parallel to the Sid walkthrough chat. Opened on Cornwall's sending domain stuck Pending: it had been entered as `www.cornwallmovers.co.uk` and its records added at IONOS while the live nameservers are Cloudflare. Step 0 then found `sending_domain` and the from-address were independent inputs and that Postmark domains were looked up by name across the whole account, so one tenant could reuse another's verified domain. **S478 DONE** [`b536b08`, migration `20261008123300_s478_sending_domain_unique`: one sending domain per tenant (partial unique index on lower(sending_domain)), an unowned leftover Postmark domain is deleted and recreated so a fresh DKIM key must be published, the tenant's own domain is never recreated; verified on prod, S146 refused `richardstowell.com`]. **F75 DONE** [PR #170 squash `72cfd65`, no migration: the sending domain is derived from the Business Info contact email (no domain box), a Business Info email change to another domain warns and never clears the identity; nameserver lookup names the DNS host ("Your DNS is managed at Cloudflare, even if you bought your domain somewhere else", grey-cloud callout for Cloudflare, `domaincontrol.com` shown as "GoDaddy or 123-reg", unknown hosts show the raw nameserver), guide picker pre-selects it and says DNS not registrar; phone-only record fields with ellipsis + 44px Copy; "Show me how in <host>" button in context; honest pending copy; background re-check cron every 15 min (48h every run, then hourly to 7 days, then stop; first `verified_at` kept); Domain Connect state carries the domain. **Cornwall verified end to end on 10-08 16:15: records added in Cloudflare, nobody pressed Check, the cron flipped it green.**] **F78 DONE** [PR #171 squash `fcc2213`, migrations `20261009120000`, `20261009160000`, `20261009180000`: DEC64 enquiry routes. Onboarding step "Enquiries" asks Q1 "Do you have a business email address?" (defaults Yes when a website was entered) then Q2 "Where do you want to manage enquiries?" (In the app, Recommended, tinted; In your email inbox greyed when Q1 is No); Agents Setup shows the same until answered, then a compact toggle + Business email row with Change opening the explainer; a healthy mailbox is read in BOTH modes and decides the door (reverses DEC40); the Movers App address is the door only with no mailbox; setup checklist gains "Choose how enquiries reach you" and "Connect your inbox", the test enquiry targets the address customers will use and only ticks when it arrived that way, "Set up email sending" is optional without a business email; BCC of sent mail off by default with an opt-in "Email me a copy of everything the app sends" + optional different address (confirm link, POST confirm page, 7-day token), never the generic sender (all 11 generic-sender replies in Resend had been bouncing on their own BCC); every app email carries a tag header and tagged mail is dropped on the way in; booking confirmations now land in the in-app Sent tab IN FULL (new `booking_confirmations_sent` table) and in the connected Sent folder; rail labels "Receiving enquiries" / "Sending replies". Forwarding route built in the morning and removed in the rework at the operator's call.] **S485 DONE** [`2e1ebbf` + `6af9959` + `1522d9b` + `0ccd7c0`: booking confirmation template cannot be saved until it carries at least one placeholder other than `customer_name`, client and server, with Convert beside the message; no double greeting when pasting an already-converted email]. **DEC64 LOCKED** (enquiry routes). **DEC40 REVERSED** by DEC64. **Provider onboarding corrected:** a merged Domain Connect template is not picked up by anyone; IONOS, Cloudflare and GoDaddy each need an onboarding email, sent 10-08 from `admin@moversapp.app` (Gmail send-as over Postmark SMTP, stopgap). **New open:** S486, S487, F79. Full bodies in movers_app_backlog_archive.md (ARCHIVE APPEND 2026-10-08 v2). Session summary: sessions/movers_app_session_summary_2026-10-08_v2.md.)**

**Updated 2026-09-09 (S191 + F33 + F34 ALL SHIPPED & VERIFIED LIVE. The reliability arc: pipeline errors now surface instead of vanishing, failed runs get an in-app home with automatic recovery, and escalated enquiries can be replied to from inside the app. main via PR #99 [F33] + PR #100 [F34]; S191 direct-to-main bfc86c9. Three migrations live via MCP [f33_support_reports_and_error_bucket, f33_retry_count, f34_manual_reply], reconcile w/ S130. Codex hit its usage cap mid-F34; finished on Claude Code, same branch.)** **(1) S191 DONE (bfc86c9, direct to main).** Pipeline status-writes no longer swallow their PostgREST error. A checked-update helper throws on a failed write, so the existing outer catch converts it to status='error' + readable text instead of stranding the run in 'received' (the S257 silent-swallow class, now generalised). Outer catch made non-throwing (console fallback if even the error-write fails); missing-run + fallback-write-failure both logged. NO migration (the `error` column and `'error'` status already existed; DB-confirmed before build). Verified live (a real escalation) + a unit suite covering every branch. Root gap was NOT storage (send-path failures already persisted), it was in-flight status writes ignoring their result. **(2) F33 DONE (PR #99, delivers S194).** Failed pipeline runs get a Review Queue home. An error run renders a red card with a TRANSLATED safe cause (bucketed by WHO CAN FIX: transient / tenant-config / our-end), the customer's email shown, the RAW error hidden. Actions per bucket: Retry (transient/config), Go-to-setup link (tenant-config: missing agent/template), and Report-to-support (any). OUR-END errors (missing API key, check-violation, unknown) auto-write a `support_reports` row + email admin@moversapp.app (both non-fatal); manual Report upserts the same row (idempotent via UNIQUE(agent_run_id)). [2026-09-29: `support_reports` stays machine-raised and untouched; human tickets live in the separate `support_tickets` table, F64.] Recovery: an auto-replay CRON (every 10 min, cap 5 auto-retries via `auto_retry_count`) re-drives error runs through the pipeline, self-healing transient/token_cap/config/our-end once the fault clears, and is what makes the card's "this will update once resolved" promise true. Migrations: `support_reports` (service-role-only RLS, UNIQUE on agent_run_id) + `agent_runs.error_bucket` + `agent_runs.auto_retry_count`. Verified live end-to-end: our-end card + row + real admin email, retry recovery (error→drafted/escalated, count 0→1), cap held at 5, null-body run skipped, unauth cron rejected (401). **(3) F34 DONE (PR #100).** Reply to the customer from the Escalated tab (previously read-only, Mark-as-done only, a genuine launch hole). New run-based `sendEscalatedReply` action reuses the existing transport with NO queue row or draft; a small composer (subject prefilled "Re:", plain-text body). On success the run STAYS status='escalated' with `escalation_resolved_at/by` set (mirrors Mark-as-done), and stores `manual_reply_body_html` + `manual_reply_sent_at` + real `outbound_email_id`. An atomic compare-and-set claim on `outbound_email_id` (pending token before send, real id after) prevents concurrent double-send; the claim is RELEASED on a send failure (retry possible) but RETAINED on send-ok/save-fail (never double-email a delivered reply). Mailbox writeback fixed to search the ESCALATED folder (the inbound was moved there on escalation, so the old watch-folder search missed it). Migration: `manual_reply_body_html` + `manual_reply_sent_at`. Verified live: a real reply delivered + correctly threaded (In-Reply-To), DB persisted, claim swapped to the real Resend id. **(4) NEW ITEMS:** F35 (Sent tab, the correct long-term home for replied escalations), S267 (escalated-reply blocked-send EXIT ROUTE, the send-gate UX gap F34 exposed), S268 (retry-atomicity hardening, the agent_review_queue unique-constraint deferred from F33). **(5) METHOD:** every Codex / Claude-Code claim was DB/live-verified via MCP, which caught the send-gate block, a wrong-branch preview (main not F34), and the header-drop redirect on the manual cron curl. em-dash preference moved into userPreferences this session. Full narrative in the 2026-09-09 session summary.**

**HOW THE OUTBOUND FROM RESOLVES — established this session, corrects the slice-A assumption.** Source of truth at send time is **tenant_config.contact_email**, via lib/agents/resolveSendFrom.ts, falling back to RESEND_FROM_EMAIL. **tenant_sending_identity.from_email is a DEAD column** — written and displayed by the card, never read at send time (see S208). “Verified” for send purposes is NOT status alone: lib/agents/sendingIdentity.ts requires fromDomain === sendingDomain AND status === “verified”, so a verified domain with a mismatched/empty contact_email falls to generic or blocks. **agent_runs.from_address is the INBOUND sender, not the outbound from** — outbound from is passed to Resend at send time and persisted nowhere; ground truth for what a reply was sent as is the received header, not the DB. Codex investigation report: f19b-send-from-investigation-2026-08-25.md.

**S202 IS SHIPPED — THE INBOUND HALF. F15 thread-detection, PR #63, merge 2f9c1f4, feature commit 538e244, branch deleted.** Detection lives once, pre-classify, in computeDraft.ts (after the own-domain skip, before classify), so one place covers both inbound paths (Resend webhook and IMAP mailbox). A mid-thread reply is caught by ANY of: an In-Reply-To header, a References header, or a Re:/Fwd:/Fw:/Aw:/Sv: subject prefix. On a match it returns a new result kind **thread_reply** — modelled on skipped_own_domain, NOT the escalated kind, because pipeline’s escalated branch requires a classificationPayload a pre-classify exit does not have — and pipeline writes status=escalated with classification={escalation_reason:“thread_reply”} (minimal), no queue row, no classify call. Two new nullable columns on agent_runs, **in_reply_to** and **references_header**, are captured on both ingest paths (Resend headers were fetched-then-discarded, now persisted; the mailbox fetch was widened, mailparser already parses both). escalationReason.ts maps thread_reply to plain red-pill copy; the new reason auto-counts in every badge. **VERIFIED ON REAL PRODUCTION EMAIL** — a real Gmail reply into a Cornwall thread, via INBOX.Shadow on S199 Mover (row 74f49250): status=escalated, escalation_reason=thread_reply, in_reply_to populated, references_header carrying the full two-id chain, source=mailbox, **0 queue rows, no draft**. Regression check passed in the same batch: a fresh enquiry with no headers drafted normally, so detection is not over-firing. Badge + Mark-as-done confirmed by the operator.

**S202 — TWO THREAD-CHECKS, NOT ONE. ONLY THE FIRST IS DONE.** The backlog’s “F15 thread-check” was really two guards. **Problem A (inbound is mid-thread) is SHIPPED** — detectable from the incoming message alone, no Sent-folder sync needed; this is the shadow-data defect. **Problem B (the tenant already replied — the race) is STILL OPEN** — the mover answers from their phone and the app also replies, so the customer gets two answers. Problem B needs the Sent folder synced and a thread lookup, and it is the guard that must land before mailbox auto-send can come on. Own slice. **A KNOWN RESIDUAL GAP survives even Problem A: a customer sending a genuinely fresh email about an existing job (****“********about my quote last week********”****) carries no headers and no Re:, so detection misses it and the classifier drafts. Smaller population; the classifier’s own is_initial_enquiry is the intended catch. Tune later; do not widen detection for it.**

**DEC31 AMENDED 2026-08-24 —**** ****“********UNTIL THREAD-CHECK SHIPS********”**** MEANS THE RACE GUARD, NOT THE INBOUND CHECK.** DEC31 held mailbox auto-send OFF “until the thread-check ships”. That phrase now resolves to Problem B, the Sent-folder race guard, NOT the inbound thread-detection shipped this session (Problem A, PR #63). **Mailbox auto-send stays OFF until the race guard lands, even though inbound thread-detection is now live.** Do not read “thread-check shipped” as licence to enable mailbox auto-send. This is the exact double-reply-to-a-real-customer failure DEC31’s catch exists to prevent, and shipping only Problem A does not remove it.

**S203 (escalation noise) IS SMALLER THAN FEARED, POST-S202.** Trivial acknowledgements (“thanks 👍”, “that’s perfect”) that are replies now escalate as thread_reply rather than as generic classifier escalations, and clear with one Mark-as-done tap — the badge returns to meaning “unlooked-at”. S203 remains open only for the fresh-email-no-headers residual above and any acks the classifier still mis-routes; tuning, low priority.

**PROCESS — THE STEP-0 INVESTIGATE GATE EARNED ITS KEEP AGAIN, AND ONE UNDECLARED FILE CHANGE WAS CAUGHT BEFORE MERGE.** The gate established the own-domain return shape to copy, found that pipeline’s escalated branch requires a classificationPayload (which forced the “own kind” design rather than a synthetic payload), and confirmed mailparser already parses the headers — building straight through would have crashed the escalated path on a null payload. Separately, actions.ts changed unexpectedly (+25 -11) and was read before merge: benign, one hunk in mapTestEmailResult (the F12 panel mapper) needed to map the new kind into TestEmailDraftResult; it does not touch any send path. **The F12 panel cannot test this feature at all — body-only, no subject, no headers, no persistence — so it triggers none of the three detection paths and cannot show the no-queue-row property; a panel run confirmed this (escalated as classifier_requested, not thread_reply). Real email was the only path.**

**NUMBERING.** Next free number is **S205**. Next free decision is **DEC32** (DEC31 locked this session — Candidate A). **Check this document before assigning a number; do not infer the next one from the last one seen in a chat.** S178 and S185 remain retired; do not reuse those numbers. New items raised this session: S202 (thread-detection / F15 thread-check, launch-blocking), S203 (escalation noise, tuning), S204 (mailbox polling schedule — auto-run shipped, interval-at-scale open).

**DEC31 LOCKED — CANDIDATE A. Customer replies land in the tenant’s own inbox at launch.** Revisited when F15 thread-check is live. Two-inboxes accepted as temporary, not structural. Inbound at launch = website contact forms, superseded by IMAP for any mailbox-connected tenant. Auto-send eligibility is per-source: Resend runs eligible, mailbox runs held for review until thread-check ships — this governs which runs are eligible, NOT a tenant’s on/off starting state, which is off by default and opted into after setup. **The original Candidate A volume figure is void** — a connected mailbox sees all inbound, not just form submissions, so this lock does not settle F18 sizing. Shadow data settled that instead (see below).

**F18 RECLASSIFIED BY REAL DATA, AND ONLY THE SILENT HALF WAS EVER THE EMERGENCY.** Five days of shadow traffic showed ~6-7 real enquiries/day, ~120/month — decisively the Candidate A low-volume picture, NOT the 20/day → 7,000/year figure the original F18 entry argued from. **So the pagination/payload half of F18 is confirmed NOT launch-blocking and is deferred.** The launch-blocking half was always the Escalated list’s silent .limit(50) truncation — row 51+ invisible with no indication. **That half is now SHIPPED (09686de): cap raised to 200, visible count, at-cap warning; the cap lived in two fetch paths plus a client-side merge cap, all three patched via a shared constant in escalatedList.ts.** Review Queue payload/pagination remains the open, deferred remainder of F18. Note the earlier F18 entry’s arithmetic was sound reasoning from a volume assumption that real data has now overturned — the lesson stands: test-tenant row counts hid the true shape, real traffic revealed it.

**SHADOW MODE IS DONE — AND THE WRITTEN PLAN WAS WRONG, IN A GOOD WAY.** The old Candidate-F plan pointed the WordPress contact form at both the mailbox and the app’s inbound address. The operator’s instinct in-session was better: connect the real mailbox read-only instead. A Hostinger server-side rule copies ALL inbound to INBOX.Shadow (copy, not move — Make’s supply stays intact), and the app watches INBOX.Shadow. This sees every enquiry, not just form submissions; auto-send is forced off for mailbox-sourced runs regardless; and because the app’s OWN classifier does draft-vs-escalate, a single folder tests both drafting and escalation. Set up on a throwaway shadow tenant (8f31127f-50da-4d6b-9513-a983183bac57, “S199 Mover”), leaving the manager’s eventual cold-onboarding test uncontaminated for later. **This unblocked the data F18, D13 and D14 were all waiting on.** Mailbox sync also moved from manual-curl-only to auto-run every minute via Vercel Cron this session (2ed5f58).

**THREE FINDINGS FROM REAL TRAFFIC, TWO STILL OPEN.** (1) Survey-misfire on form enquiries — SHIPPED (ad624fe), was systemic not occasional. (2) **S202 — thread-detection [INBOUND HALF SHIPPED 2026-08-24 evening, PR #63 — see current header; race guard still open].** The app drafts fresh “thanks for your enquiry” replies to mid-conversation emails — customers accepting quotes, confirming dates — because it cannot tell a new enquiry from message 4 in a thread. Confirmed across many shadow rows. This is the F15 thread-check made real, and it is the single most dangerous open item: on any auto-send path it sends a first-contact template mid-negotiation. Harmless in shadow (nothing sends); reputational damage live. **This is exactly the class DEC31 / Candidate A’s two-inbox compromise exists to avoid — the shadow data confirmed that reasoning was sound.** Own session, F15 docs open. (3) **S203 — escalation noise, OPEN, minor.** Trivial acknowledgements (“thanks 👍”, “that’s perfect”) escalate rather than close; tuning, low priority, inflates the escalated count toward the (now 200) cap faster than real escalations do.

**HEALTH AFTER 5 DAYS UNATTENDED.** Cron ran continuously — 200s across all five days, 4 errors (likely maxDuration timeouts on heavy batches; confirm, not urgent). Copy rule held, Shadow folder still filling. Sync cursor sets to the folder high-water mark on connect, so pre-existing mail is correctly skipped. **One email arrived during setup before auto-sync was wired and was missed — cursor-forward-only means a later sync won’t retroactively catch it; re-send into the folder to recover. Logged as a known behaviour, not a bug.**

**Movers App — Master Backlog + Decisions Log**

Updated 2026-08-17. main at 820a0b4 (PR #62, squash-merged). **Three items shipped and verified on real production inbound email: S195, S196 part 1 and S196 part 2.** S195 and part 1 direct to main; part 2 via PR #62, branch deleted. One migration, applied by hand because S130 still blocks db push — **orphan sixteen**. **AN AUTO-RESPONDER CAN NO LONGER OFFER A CUSTOMER A FREE HOME SURVEY WITHOUT KNOWING HOW FAR AWAY THEY ARE, AND DISTANCE NOW RESOLVES FOR 84% OF ENQUIRIES THAT PREVIOUSLY SKIPPED IT ENTIRELY.**

**NUMBERING.** Next free number is **S202**. Next free decision is **DEC31** — see the collision note under decision candidates; DEC30 was already claimed by the nav-badge candidate before this session, and the DEC28 amendment therefore takes DEC31 if locked. **Check this document before assigning a number; do not infer the next one from the last one seen in a chat.** S178 and S185 remain retired; do not reuse those numbers.

**S173 IS NO LONGER LAUNCH-BLOCKING, AND THE OPERATOR’S INSTINCT IS WHY.** A fully scoped design session for S173 was stopped mid-flight with “I’m not actually sure this is the case anymore — shall we do some testing to see if this is even still an issue or if we’re just wasting time and tokens?” It was largely right. One read-only investigation and two SQL queries established that six of nine live agents are single-route and cannot hit the fault at all, one is covered by S171’s stopgap, and of the two three-route tenants only one was broken — because of a missing seed value, not because a flat list cannot express an “or”. **A two-session restructure became a one-line fix (S195).** See the amended S173 entry.

**TWO ENTRIES IN THIS DOCUMENT CONTRADICTED EACH OTHER ABOUT A COLUMN VALUE AND NEITHER WAS FULLY RIGHT.** S64 phase 1 recorded that route_key was backfilled including item_list → list. S171, written three days later, recorded that item_list was route_key: null and that this was why ten hardcoded lines were needed. **S171 was right about the state; S64’s backfill claim was overstated** — 93 of 103 essential-field rows carry NULL, and the seed in lib/agent-defaults/movers.ts was never updated at all. Settling this cost a full investigation. **Worth remembering when writing an entry that asserts database state: say when it was observed, and against which tenants.**

**DEC28’S CORE ASYMMETRY ARGUMENT WAS TRUE ONLY UNDER HUMAN REVIEW.** “Over-offering is visible and correctable, under-offering is invisible” holds when a mover reads every draft. Under auto-send an over-offer has already reached the customer, and a retracted free survey is reputational damage to the tenant — the class this project treats as unrecoverable, not the recoverable “failed to help” class DEC28 assumed. **The premise of a locked decision was false in the one mode that matters most, and nothing in this document flagged it when auto-send shipped.** The general lesson is worth more than the fix: **a locked decision whose reasoning depends on a human being in the loop needs re-examining the moment any path becomes unattended.**

**INBOUND WAS BROKEN TWICE IN ONE SESSION AND NEITHER FAILURE ANNOUNCED ITSELF.** First a malformed RESEND_WEBHOOK_SECRET — 401 at signature verification, which Resend’s dashboard displays as “Attempting” with retries, reading like a slow endpoint rather than a rejected one, and that misdirection produced a wrong timeout hypothesis. Then a broken RESEND_API_KEY, disturbed while fixing the first — and **that one is launch-blocking in its own right (S198)**. Resend’s email.received event is an envelope, not a payload: 531 characters, no text key, no html key. The body is fetched separately. With that fetch returning 400, **the pipeline classified an empty email, drafted a reply, and queued it with Approve + Send live beside**** ****“****No message body****”**, with error NULL on the run. The app produced a customer-facing reply from a subject line and nothing stopped it.

**A SQUASH-MERGED BRANCH STAYS CHECKED OUT LOCALLY AND THE NEXT COMMIT LANDS ON A DEAD BRANCH.** S195 was committed and pushed to codex-system-health and built as a Vercel preview. Local main was also two commits behind, which is why that branch was still checked out — the S61 session’s work existed nowhere else locally. **S118’s rules are necessary but not sufficient:** “confirm the hash on origin/main” catches an unpushed commit but not a commit pushed to the wrong place. **Add git branch –show-current to every brief.** Separately, a Vercel redeploy labelled “Redeploy of” produced a wrong diagnosis that production was on a stale commit — the deployment ID and the commit hash are two names for the same thing. **Read the hash, not the message.**

**THE INVESTIGATE-BEFORE-BUILD GATE MOVED SCOPE ON BOTH BUILT ITEMS AND HAS STILL NEVER FAILED TO EARN ITS COST.** On S196 part 1 it killed a proposed coarse-geocoding design by establishing that town names never reach the system — STRICT extraction excludes them, so there is no coarse location to geocode against a margin. On part 2 it found two auto-send gates where the brief assumed one, and found the resolved route set was computed and thrown away — which turned the fix from a condition into a schema change. It also corrected the **previous** investigation’s claim about which gate checks template markers. **Six recommendations were made and withdrawn across the session, every one caught by checking rather than by reasoning further.**

**THE DISCOVERY GATE MOVED FIVE OF THE SIX THINGS IN SCOPE, BEFORE ANY DESIGN WORK.** A read-only investigation ran first and is in the repo as system-health-error-state-investigation-2026-08-14.md. It found S181 free rather than a build, S180 misdescribed in this document, bounced-email capture entirely absent, S188 not a wall at all, and a silent failure nobody had ever recorded — manual send failures were being written nowhere. **Every one of those would otherwise have been discovered mid-build and cost a rewrite.** The gate cost one Codex run.

**A WIDGET CAN BE BUILT CORRECTLY AGAINST THE WRONG SHAPE, AND ONLY LOOKING AT IT WILL TELL YOU.** The first build enumerated individual enquiry failures, because four of the five error sources are enquiry-level. It matched the brief exactly and was wrong: System Health reports whether the machinery works, not which enquiries broke. Reshaped into subsystem indicator lines, and the row-card work moved into a new Inbox widget rather than being discarded. **The brief specified the sources correctly and the shape wrongly.**

**GREEN MEANT**** ****“****NO FAILURES RECORDED****”****, WHICH IS NOT THE SAME AS**** ****“****WORKING****”****.** Tenant Newco Removals has its agent enabled with auto-send on and no inbound address configured — nothing can ever arrive — and the widget said “Everything’s running.” **The surface built to prevent silent failure was itself reassuring a tenant whose agent could not receive a single enquiry.** Fixed with a third state, grey “Not set up”, which never counts as a failure and never breaks the all-green summary, plus a new “Receiving enquiries” line. **Grey lines are always shown, never hidden: absence tells a mover nothing.**

**A PIPELINE-ERROR RUN HAS NO HOME ANYWHERE IN THE APP.** Two real enquiries on tenant 99c73f1b, four days apart, arrived and produced no draft, no queue row and no escalation — and no surface would ever have said so. The emails themselves are safe (agent_runs captures body at insert time, and inbound is forwarding-based so the tenant’s own mailbox has them), so this is “no draft was written” rather than “an enquiry vanished”. **Both rows say only**** ****“****Unknown pipeline error****”****, because the catch block discarded the real exception.** See S191 and S194; S191 first, or the recovery surface will not be able to explain what it is recovering from.

**THE WHOLE IMAP SYNC PATH FAILED OPEN, AT THREE INDEPENDENT LAYERS, AND ONLY ONE OF THEM WAS IN S129’S ENTRY.** The entry asked what a null cursor does. The answer was worse than the question: lib/mailbox/imap.ts collapsed “folder is empty” and “could not read the highest UID” into the same null; the connect action saved that as status: “ok” with a null cursor; and on sync, a null cursor produced range “1:*” with a filter of (cursor === null || message.uid > cursor), so every message passed. **There was no message cap anywhere — the only bound in the entire path was maxDuration = 60 on the route, which is a time limit, not a bound on work.** Pointed at Cornwall’s real inbox that is the entire mail history through the classifier until the route times out, then again on the next sync.

**THE FIRST TWO FIXES FOR IT WERE BOTH WRONG, AND BOTH WERE CAUGHT BY THINKING ABOUT WHAT THE CAP ACTUALLY BOUNDS.** A cap of 50 messages was specified, then found to be unreachable inside a 60-second budget at two LLM calls per message; the answer was not a smaller number but advancing the cursor per message so a timeout stops being destructive. Then the cap was found to bound *processing* while fetchSinceUid still pulled the whole mailbox into memory first. Then the fetch cap was implemented as a UID-number window (cursor+1 : cursor+50), which **stalls permanently** when deletions leave a gap wider than the window — zero messages, no cursor advance, identical window next sync, reported as fetched 0 / status ok. The shipped answer is a UID SEARCH above the cursor, discard, sort, slice, then fetch only those. **A cap on a sparse, gap-riddled ID space is not a cap on work.**

**THE CONNECT TIMEOUT WAS 90 SECONDS AND IT WAS SHARED WITH SYNC.** imapflow’s default, never overridden, sitting on a form a tenant fills in during onboarding. Found only because the split between verify and sync was questioned after the fix landed. Now 10s on verify, 90s on sync — they want different values and a single shared constant cannot serve both. **Nobody was looking for this; it was found by asking where a change applied, not whether it worked.**

**THE WATCH FOLDER NAME IS NOT GUESSABLE AND FREE TEXT COULD NOT FIX IT.** Cornwall’s Hostinger mailbox uses a dot delimiter and prefixes every subfolder: a folder created as “Testing” is addressable only as INBOX.Testing. Neither “Testing” nor “TESTING” works. **INBOX is the sole case-insensitive folder name in the IMAP spec, which is exactly why assuming the convention generalises is wrong.** The mailbox also contains both INBOX and INBOX.INBOX — two folders whose display names are identical — so the picker must render full paths, not display names.

**A REALTIME SUBSCRIPTION WAS ADDED TO A TABLE THE CLIENT CANNOT READ, AND IT WAS REPORTED AS WORKING.** The S186 banner shipped with a tenant_mailboxes subscription so it would self-clear. It can never fire: the table is not in the supabase_realtime publication, and it has zero RLS policies because DEC24/DEC25 make it service-role only. Realtime authorises as the logged-in user. **Making it work would mean granting the browser SELECT on a table of encrypted mailbox credentials, for a banner.** Removed, with a comment naming the constraint. See S188.

**S90’S RULE WAS RECORDED AS LOCKED AND HAD NEVER BEEN IMPLEMENTED.** The entry says the drafted reply’s greeting uses the signoff name and “must continue to”. GREETING_SYSTEM_PROMPT has always instructed the extractor to read the From display name FIRST. In the common case both values are the same, so the distinction existed only on paper and nothing surfaced it until a test email arrived whose display name and signature disagreed. **A locked decision is not the same as an implemented one, and this document cannot tell the difference.**

**A PROMPT RULE THE MODEL IGNORES IS WORSE THAN NO RULE.** The forwarding guard added to that prompt was tested twice and disregarded both times, including once where it explicitly called for the forwarder and the model returned the original customer. It was also wrong on the merits — **forwarding is the app’s primary inbound path**, so the customer inside the forwarded content is usually the person to greet. Deleted rather than sharpened. The guards that were verified working (mid-sentence third-party names, quoted reply history) stay.

**TWO PREDICTED DEFECTS DID NOT EXIST, AND THE PATTERN IS WORTH RECORDING.** A UIDVALIDITY guard was raised as missing (S178) and is already implemented and correct, on the unmodified side of the diff. A cursor-carried-over-on-folder-change landmine was flagged and does not exist — mailboxIdentityChanged explicitly includes the watch_folder comparison and takes the fresh-cursor branch. **Both were raised as findings rather than questions.** The careful cases in this codebase are covered more often than not; ask, but do not assert.

**CODEX COMMITS AND REPORTS A HASH WITHOUT PUSHING.** Twice this session on direct-to-main items, both times discovered because Vercel did not build. The earlier PR briefs carried an explicit “commit AND push, confirm the hash on origin/main” line and the smaller ones dropped it. **Put it in every brief.** Codex also spiralled for several minutes trying to start a local dev server it does not need and cannot start — the “no local dev server, verification is on the branch preview” line belongs in every brief too.

**A TEST THAT CANNOT FAIL PROVES NOTHING, AND TWO WERE WRITTEN TODAY.** The forwarded-enquiry check pasted a body whose only name appeared in both the forward header and the signature, so “guard fired” and “guard did not fire” produced the same answer. **The F12(a) panel takes body text only — any From: line in a paste is prose the model reads, not a header.** Construct panel tests with a competing name in forwarder position or do not construct them at all.

**SHADOW MODE’S DESIGN IN THIS DOCUMENT IS OBSOLETE.** It describes pointing the website contact form at both the Hostinger mailbox and the app’s inbound address. F15a slice 1 replaced that: connect Cornwall’s mailbox read-only and the app sees everything arriving at the address, not only form submissions. Auto-send is already forced off for mailbox-sourced runs, writeback does not exist so nothing can be moved or deleted, and no form change is needed. **S127 (quote engine parity) does not gate this** — it gates the non-email cutover. Shadow is email-only and read-only.

**CORNWALL’S LIVE MAILBOX CONTAINS MAKE’S WRITEBACK TARGETS.** The folder list shows INBOX.Charlie Replied and INBOX.Harlene’s feed, which are the existing Make scenario’s destinations, alongside three years of INBOX.Archive.2024/2025/2026. **When F15’s writeback slice is built, the app must not move mail into folders Make is also managing** or two systems will fight over the same messages.

**THE FEATURE WAS ALREADY THREE-QUARTERS BUILT AND THE BACKLOG SAID OTHERWISE.** F10’s entry stated flatly that there is no geocoding anywhere in the repo, and the session opened by planning a session around a 32.7 MB offline postcode dataset, a 500 MB free-tier database ceiling and Google’s 30-day caching terms. A single grep ended it: /api/routes/distance already existed, already read and wrote route_cache, already had the server key wired, and the Routes API geocodes an address string internally. **No geocoding needed building, no dataset decision was ever real, and the whole dilemma was an artefact of a stale backlog line.** The grep took under a minute and should have been the first move, not the fourth.

**THE MEASURE CHANGED BECAUSE THE FINDING CHANGED, AND THAT WAS THE RIGHT ORDER.** F10 specified a straight-line radius dragged on a map. Straight-line needs coordinates for both ends, which needs geocoding, which is the thing not worth building. Road miles were already available for free from the existing endpoint. Road is also what the Cornwall quote engine prices on, so routing and pricing now share one measure and cannot contradict each other. **Locked as DEC28 only after establishing that no radius UI existed anywhere**, so there were no saved thresholds to break and no tenant expectations to migrate — free that day, breaking a week later.

**RULE ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING AND ALMOST SHIPPED WRONG.** resolveQuoteRoutes returns on the first matching rules[] entry, and every tenant already carried a single always entry from S64 phase 1. Appending drive_miles after it would have made the new rule dead on arrival — silently, with tests passing and the entry visibly present in the database. Caught in the Step 0 report for 2a, not in code review. **always is a catch-all and is always the terminal entry.**

**THE SEMANTICS CHANGED MID-FEATURE FROM FIRST-MATCH-WINS TO SUBTRACTION, AND THE OPERATOR CALLED IT.** The obvious implementation — a matching drive_miles rule returns its own routes — would mean a nearby customer is offered a survey and nothing else. What movers actually mean is “free survey if you’re close, otherwise photos or video work fine”: the full set inside the radius, minus survey outside it. Then the operator raised the opposite case, a mover who surveys everything they can reach, which is one boolean rather than a second rule system. Both shipped. **exclusive only governs behaviour inside the radius; beyond it, both modes are identical.**

**THE INVESTIGATE-BEFORE-BUILD GATE PAID FOR ITSELF ON EVERY SLICE AND KILLED TWO BRIEFS OUTRIGHT.** 2a’s brief said to stop writing agent_quote_route_policy; Step 0 found .routes live in computeDraft’s fallback path for six tenants, so that line would have shipped a regression. 2b’s brief assumed route resolution could read the pickup postcode; Step 0 found resolveQuoteRoutes was called *before* extraction ran, which is why the call had to move. 2e’s brief was abandoned entirely after Step 0 established that route_key doubles as a merge marker. **Two of those would have been silent failures.**

**A create or replace WITH A NEW DEFAULT PARAMETER CREATES AN OVERLOAD, NOT A REPLACEMENT.** Both RPC migrations this session had to drop the exact prior signature first and reproduce a 300-line body verbatim. Verified afterwards with select count(*) from pg_proc — two rows means ambiguous calls, not a successful migration. Also: drop function discards grants, and this function is security definer. Codex restored the service_role grant explicitly without being asked, which was correct and was not in the brief.

**S130 IS WORSE THAN THE ENTRY SAYS, AND THE ENTRY’S PREMISE IS WRONG.** There is no Supabase CLI installed on the operator’s machine — not globally, not in package.json. So supabase db push is not a capability that was lost to twelve orphaned migrations; it is one that has never existed locally. Three more migrations were added to the orphan pile this session (20260812161117, 20260813130000, 20260813170000) with no way to repair them. **S130’s fix is not**** ****“****repair twelve rows and resume normal pushes****”**** ****— there is no normal to resume until a CLI is installed and linked. Re-scope that item before starting it.**

**THE STOPGAP RULE, EARNED THIS SESSION AND WORTH KEEPING.** S171 was scoped as a subtractive schema column, then rescoped to ten hardcoded lines once it became clear the proper fix (S173) would delete it. The operator’s argument was that a small throwaway now beats blocking launch on a large restructure, and that is right — but the corollary is that a throwaway should be built as a throwaway. **A migration, a backfill and three read sites is not a stopgap. Ten lines with a comment naming the item that deletes them is.**

**EVERY FIX EXPOSED THE NEXT ONE, AND THAT IS THE REAL FINDING OF THE DAY.** Survey-only drafts asked the customer for an inventory a surveyor would take in person. Suppressing that revealed the lead-in still said “to price it accurately we’ll need” for a scheduling ask. Fixing the survey case revealed that list+photos has the same fault — the draft offers a choice of routes and then demands the union of what every route needs. **Routes are an**** ****“****or****”****; the missing-info block is an**** ****“****and****”****. One flat list cannot express that, and no amount of patching will make it.** That is S173, and it absorbs S171, S176 and part of S64’s original brief.

**S137 CHANGED SHAPE TWICE DURING THE SESSION AND BOTH CHANGES WERE RIGHT.** It was briefed as toggle plus wording editing, then wording editing was removed on correctness grounds (the label column carries an invisible noun/clause grammar contract that the missing-info assembly depends on), then the whole grouped layout was replaced by a flat list of per-category pills. The shipped surface is smaller than the first brief and does more.

**THE INVESTIGATE-BEFORE-BUILD GATE PAID FOR ITSELF AGAIN, TWICE.** It found that page.tsx filtered essential fields on active=true, which would have shipped a one-way door — untick a field, refresh, and it is gone from the UI forever with no way back. It also found that tenantCategories carries id and label but not key, so pill toggling had no key to write.

**THE AUTO-SEND PATH WAS SENDING RAW {{markers}} TO CUSTOMERS AND NOTHING WAS STOPPING IT.** Found by following a loose thread off the S68 investigation. renderTemplateBodyForDraft leaves unresolved markers literal, the Review Queue showed an amber badge for the postcode case, and the unattended cron path had no equivalent check at all. S144 and S145 close it on both the automated and the human send paths. **This was the highest-value thing done today and it was not on the plan when the session started.**

**THE INVESTIGATE-BEFORE-BUILD GATE PAID FOR ITSELF SEVEN TIMES TODAY AND HAS NOW DONE SO ON EVERY ITEM IT HAS BEEN APPLIED TO.** It killed the locked-chips design for S68 before a line was written (Tiptap is not installed; chips need a contenteditable token model), found the four Review-Queue fetch sites that had to stay in sync, established that new-tenant provisioning was clean before any repair migration was designed, and stopped a repair that would have overwritten three healthy tenant rows.

**NUMBERING COLLISION, RECORDED SO IT IS NOT REDISCOVERED.** The quote-route rename shipped under the commit message “S131: rename quote route value email to list”. S131 was already taken by the npm audit item and S132 by the mailbox credential key. The rename is recorded below as **S133** and the commit message is wrong and stays wrong. Next free number is S144. **Check this document before assigning a number; do not infer the next one from the last one seen in a chat.**

**F16 SHIPPED AS FIVE COMMITS ON ONE BRANCH, EACH VERIFIED IN THE RUNNING APP BEFORE THE NEXT WAS BRIEFED.** Shell, then one-template-per-category, then Rules, then Inbox, then a fix commit. That shape was chosen deliberately over one large pass and it earned itself twice: the ordering regression in 3b was caught because 3c existed to catch it, and every commit was eyeballed on a preview rather than inferred from a green build. Full detail in the F16 entry.

**THE CATEGORY/TEMPLATE MERGE WAS REJECTED MID-SESSION, AND THE REASONING IS THE MOST IMPORTANT THING RECORDED TODAY.** DEC17’s UI clause said category and template become one enquiry-type object and the word “category” disappears tenant-side. Building the shell first changed the facts: placing Templates above Categories on the Generate tab delivered most of what the merge was for, because a tenant who should leave categories alone naturally does. What remained was a real asymmetry — a description drives classification and is edited maybe twice ever; wording is edited weekly — and merging would have put a routing-critical field in the drawer a tenant opens most. The specialist category with no template proved the shapes do not match: a merged row cannot represent that state honestly. **What was actually broken was the pairing, not the separation**, and that was fixed instead. See DEC17 as amended.

**F18 IS NEW AND IT IS LAUNCH-BLOCKING.** Review Queue fetches every row for the tenant with no limit, each carrying inbound body, draft body and edited draft, and refetches the lot on every window focus. Escalated is capped at 50 with no “show more” and no indication, so row 51 is silently invisible. At 20 emails a day that is ~7,000 rows a year. Test tenants hide it completely — Joe’s has 61 and 24. **This degrades continuously with success: the busier the mover, the worse their app gets.**

**RETAINED — FOUR THINGS ABOUT THE CLASSIFIER, established 2026-08-07 and still load-bearing.**

- **sort_order does not arbitrate between tenant categories.** The multi-match pre-pass reads every configured condition before any first-match logic runs. Two matches escalate; one match wins by being the only one; zero escalate. There is no path where ordering picks between two matching categories. Ordering still governs STEP 0, STEP 1 and STEP 2, which are genuinely sequential.

- **Exclusion clauses suppress escalation.** A “Not X” clause is what stops the pre-pass counting a second match. Removing one does not tidy the text; it raises the escalation rate.

- **The presence of a category changes the answer to an unrelated enquiry.** The same car-transport enquiry drafted a confident quote reply on a tenant without a specialist category, and escalated on the same tenant once one was added. The outcome tracks which categories exist, not whether the business does the work.

- **no_match may be unreachable in practice.** The SAFETY RULES sit after the multi-match check, so an enquiry that loosely matches two categories escalates as multi_match before no_match is ever evaluated. Logged as S122.

**RETAINED — THE VERIFICATION STANDARD.** The F12(a) test panel cannot verify anything whose output is a stored row or a UI state; it never writes a run. Anything a tenant sees on Review Queue or Escalated must be tested with a real email. See S123.

**RETAINED — SEQUENCING LESSON: do not apply a migration that changes a view’s shape until the code that reads it is on main.** Inbound email only ever hits production, so a preview deployment cannot be tested against the live webhook. When a schema change and a code change must land together and the feature can only be exercised on production, merge first and keep the revert to hand. **Note F16 3b did the opposite deliberately and correctly** — its migration only ADDS nullable columns, which no existing code reads, so applying it before merge was safe and let the preview be tested properly. The rule is about shape changes, not all migrations.

**SEQUENCING SUPERSEDED 2026-08-09 EVENING BY THE LAUNCH SEQUENCING REVIEW.** A theory session that afternoon re-read this document and the Driving Vision against the question of what actually blocks launch, and changed the answer in several places. Full reasoning in movers_app_launch_sequencing_review_2026-08-09.docx; the reprices it produced are absorbed into the entries below and marked REPRICED 2026-08-09 EVENING. **That review’s agreed order was: lock Candidate A, then F15a slice 1. It was executed in reverse, deliberately — see the F15a entry below.** The earlier line here — that F18 is launch-blocking and S82 is not, and that F18 must not drift behind three small items — still holds in direction but rested on a volume figure this document contradicts elsewhere. See the F18 entry.

**S64 PHASE 1 SHIPPED 2026-08-10, SO THE PRIORITY LINE ABOVE IS SPENT.** The additive-ask bug is fixed: routes resolve to one set per enquiry, evidence fields are filtered to that set, and two or three routes render as a single combined ask. Verified on the F12 panel against a three-route tenant (seven bullets down to three) and a survey-only tenant (item list correctly excluded).

**THE HIGHEST-PRIORITY ITEM IS NOW S68, THEN F18.** S68 was parked as “dynamic regions are invisible in the template editor” and that description understated it. The template editor shows four paragraphs; the email that actually goes out carries a bulleted block the tenant never wrote and cannot see. A mover reviewing their template is signing off on copy they have never read. **The operator raised this twice unprompted in one session, which is the strongest signal in this document that it is mispriced.** It is sequenced immediately after S64 phase 1 rather than behind F7 session 2b, because the block’s shape is now settled and building the preview earlier would have meant building it twice.

**THREE THINGS ARE LAUNCH-BLOCKING AND WERE NOT TRACKED AS SUCH BEFORE TODAY.** F19 (per-tenant sending identity — DEC2’s send-as half, never built and until now carrying no item number at all), S43 (which is not cosmetic and now gates the whole OAuth track), and billing, which has no payment path anywhere in this document and no decision recorded either way. See Candidate D.

**F15a SLICE 1 IS SHIPPED AND VERIFIED, AND IT WAS BUILT BEFORE CANDIDATE A WAS LOCKED, ON PURPOSE.** The sequencing review put Candidate A first on the grounds that it defines what launch means. Examined in chat, that turned out not to gate slice 1 at all: Candidate A is an OUTBOUND decision about reply-to, slice 1 is an INBOUND source, and the two do not touch. **The decision that actually gated the migration was Candidate B’s credential half**, because the credential column could not be created without deciding what encrypts it. B was taken instead, locked as DEC24 and DEC25, and slice 1 was built the same session.

**CANDIDATE A IS STILL NOT LOCKED, AND ITS SCOPE HAS SHRUNK.** Three axes were tangled inside it and F15a separates them. The reply-to clause survives untouched and still needs an explicit lock with the revisit trigger named. The contact-form inbound clause does NOT survive for any tenant with a connected mailbox — IMAP replaces it. **And the volume figure does not survive at all, which matters because F18 was repriced against it.** Candidate A’s “dozens a month, not hundreds” held BECAUSE inbound was contact forms only and phone enquiries were out of scope. A connected mailbox sees everything arriving at that address — contact forms, direct email, forwarded enquiries, phone follow-ups sent by email. **So locking Candidate A can no longer settle F18’s sizing, and the instruction in the F18 entry to size it against whatever Candidate A settles is now void.** See F18.

**AUTO-SEND IS NOW A PER-SOURCE SETTING, NOT ONE DEFAULT.** Candidate A says auto-send stays on by default and that still holds for Resend-sourced runs. Mailbox-sourced runs are review-only regardless of confidence until the thread-check guard ships. Verified: a mailbox-sourced run at 0.9 confidence, above the 0.85 threshold, produced a review queue row with send_after NULL and sent nothing.

**Major flags (blockers for future work)**

**F1. AI feedback loop infrastructure**

- Phase 1 SHIPPED 2026-07-21. ai_classifier_feedback table + widget on Review Queue + Escalated. Captures wrong-category labels, suggested-new-category signals, edit-diff via original_draft_body_html, classifier_prompt_version + classification_snapshot per feedback row.

- Phase 2 (later session, blocks broader launch): meta-LLM refiner. Reads ai_classifier_feedback + edit-diffs, proposes structured changes. Owner-visible proposals for structural changes via Manager Agent chat. Bounded invisible tweaks for phrasing only. Never blind full-prompt rewrites (DEC18 ceiling).

- Manager Agent (DEC14, F6) also feeds this loop: user feedback in chat becomes structured labels.

- **NOTE 2026-08-04, IMPORTANT.** A second overlapping table now exists. A2 shipped tenant_agent_classifier_corrections, which stores a wrong-category-key plus a correct-category FK plus a copy of the email, and feeds those back into the assembled prompt as worked examples. ai_classifier_feedback already captured wrong-category labels from the Review Queue widget. The two were designed months apart and the second was designed without reading the first. Neither is wired to a UI for corrections yet, so nothing is broken in production, but the overlap must be resolved before the capture UI lands. See S106.

- **NOTE 2026-08-04.** Phase 2’s target has changed. There is no longer a stored per-tenant prompt to apply a structured diff to. The refiner’s proper output is now a proposed change to a tenant’s enquiry-type DESCRIPTION, which is visible and editable in the UI, rather than to hidden prompt text. See DEC18 and DEC23.

- NOTE 2026-07-29: S56 is no longer a blocker for phase 2. Enable no longer regenerates an existing prompt. The DEC18 requirement — the refiner owns regeneration and applies a structured diff, never a full rewrite — still stands and is now the only path that rewrites a live prompt. See DEC22.

**F2. Backend admin panel**

- Internal panel to view/edit master prompts (global scaffolds + generator meta-prompt + Manager Agent system prompt). Per-tenant classifier prompts NEVER exposed here (DEC12 IP hard rule).

- Prerequisite for iterating prompts in production — currently SQL-editor only.

- Size: 1 session for MVP. Deferred until F1 phase 2 lands.

**F3. LLM provider abstraction layer — deferred as before.**

**F4. Auto Responder Discovery Mode — deferred as before. Sequence after F1 + F6 + F7.**

**F5. WhatsApp escalation — deferred as before (paywalled add-on candidate).**

**F6. Manager Agent build (per DEC14 + Driving Vision)**

- Product pivot: conversational primary interface for /agents surface. Control centre becomes Advanced tab.

- Phase 1 (v1.0): read-only diagnose + explain, no writes. 2-3 sessions.

- Phase 2 (v1.1): write access via same server actions the UI uses. Every change proposed + confirmed in chat before applying. MUST honour DEC22 — any write path that can set enabled must go through the same generate-then-enable ordering.

- Phase 3 (v1.5+): observes manual Advanced-tab changes, adapts.

- Hard rules per DEC1/DEC12/DEC13/DEC14.

- Sequence: after F7.

- **AMENDMENT 2026-09-29.** Entry point decided: a global launcher on every app page (the support-surface slice 3), not only `/agents`. Phase 1 scope for that entry = explain + how-to + read-only diagnose, grounded in the F65 help articles (each article carries `routes` frontmatter so the assistant can pick the article for the current page) plus read-only tenant state; if nothing grounds an answer it says so and offers a ticket prefilled with the transcript. Prerequisite: F65. Reaffirms the 2026-07-22 lock: one user-facing assistant identity, no separate help bot.

**F7. Structured onboarding + essential-info engine**

Website scrape prefill, services (freeform, LLM-normalised), tone, quote-route multi-select, conditional condition question, agent name, contact phone. Transactional Finish RPC provision_default_email_responder. Idempotent, retry-safe, double-submit guarded. Owner-only middleware redirect while agent_onboarding_status IS NULL, never re-triggers once completed or skipped.

Stable tenant-id hash mod 6 variant assignment. Review prompt after Finish. scripts/check_movers_copy.ts wired into lint + test. Quote-route condition no longer rendered. Signoff removed from bodies. Corrected three sections that described behaviour not present in code (tone-gated variants, greeting mapping, blanket ban on literal examples). S51 resolved as rule C. S49 closed. Six tickboxes (storage, packing, specialist, commercial, man_and_van, clearance) plus a freeform “+ add your own” replace the freeform services screen. None ticked by default; zero ticks is valid. House move / quote request is not a tickbox — every tenant gets it unconditionally. Clearance is one item covering house, flat, office and waste. tenant_config.selected_services and additional_services store the selection. Shared pickup_address / dropoff_address / move_date seeded once per agent and referenced by four situations. Provisioning RPC recreated after dropping all overloads. check_movers_copy.ts extended to all seven situations.

- VERIFIED END-TO-END 2026-07-28. Fresh signup, Storage + Specialist + Clearance ticked plus one typed extra, real email into the new tenant’s inbound address. Both the 2-missing and 3+ rendering paths exercised on real email for the first time.

- BUG FOUND AND FIXED IN TESTING (6fce737). .contains(“category_keys”, [key]) — postgrest-js serialises a JS array as a Postgres array literal, which jsonb cannot parse. Generalised as S61.

- SHIPPED 2026-07-30 — RULE C (S51, commit 1c41fff, direct to main). 2-missing now renders two separate p blocks, one per field, each carrying its own hint. Block 1 reuses the 1-missing stem; block 2 uses a new “We’ll also need X” / “We’ll also need to know X” stem. Shape read per field independently. TWO renderers changed, not one — lib/agent-defaults/movers.ts:508 held a second copy of the old join with no test coverage. Verified on real email. The copy path specified in the reference doc is now fully implemented.

The post-Finish review prompt gains a line offering to set up a reply for each, one generation per click, tenant-triggered. Decided against generate-at-Finish: an LLM call inside the transactional RPC breaks atomicity, retry produces duplicate categories, and a typed “piano moving” against an unticked Piano box would generate copy worse than the hand-written seed. Generation writes body copy AND essential-info fields. Constraints: shared keys referenced not re-authored, shape flag mandatory and explicit, generated fields default LOOSE, three to six fields, tenant reviews fields alongside body copy in one approval step. Depends on S55.

Value went UP now that extraction consumes these rows: field list, order and wording all drive the customer-facing block, and are currently SQL-only. Also now needs a shape control, or shape has to be derived on save.

- OPEN QUESTION (S54) — FOLDED INTO S69 on 2026-07-30. Variant pools are a copy decision and belong in the copy review pass, not a standalone item.
