**F8. Mobile UI refinement run — SHIPPED 2026-07-30**

Whole run completed in one session, three sequential briefs on one branch each, three PRs, all merged. Each brief used the step-1 investigate-and-report gate. Every fix verified by the owner on a real iPhone against the branch preview before merge; desktop re-checked on each.

**Brief 1 — Settings information architecture. PR #40, squash e074d24.**

- Nine Settings tabs reduced to seven: Profile, General, Pricing, Fleet, Team, Billing, Jobs.

- Branding moved into Profile as a single card between Profile details and Change password. Vehicles tab collapsed into Fleet, its reminder-offsets and DVSA placeholder cards rendered below the Fleet table. Users relabelled Team, route left as /settings/users deliberately so F9’s links do not need rewiring twice. Default deposit percentage moved to Pricing. New Jobs tab carrying archive retention. Reports promoted out of Settings to top-level nav, above Settings in both sidebar and mobile drawer. Redirects kept on all three removed routes.

- DATA-LOSS BUG CAUGHT BY THE GATE. deposit_percentage and archive_retention_months both live in the tenant_config.jobs_settings jsonb and are written by updateGeneralSettings. The action already merged the jsonb, but unconditionally rewrote both fields from submitted form values — so once the General form stopped submitting them, every subsequent General save would have blanked both. Fixed by updating only keys present in FormData. Audit found no other app write path to jobs_settings. Verified: saving deposit % on Pricing and retention on Jobs leaves General’s fields intact.

- Deposit % and archive retention each render as their own card with their own save button rather than joining an existing form’s save. One button firing two writes into two different config objects reports success on a partial failure.

- GATING NOTE. Branding was gated twice before this: the tab strip’s isVisible condition, and a middleware redirect blocking Crew from every /settings/* route except /settings/profile. Moving Branding onto Profile removes the middleware layer, because Profile is the one Settings route Crew can reach. The in-page conditional render is now load-bearing rather than belt-and-braces. Verified with a real Crew login: Branding does not render.

- Delete-archived-cards was CUT from this brief mid-session. See S76.

**Brief 2 — viewport sizing, mobile navigation, tab strip. PR #41, squash 4141481.**

- The original brief specified a min-h-screen sweep. The investigate gate found no min-h-screen, h-screen, min-h-dvh, 100svh or 100lvh anywhere in the codebase — the diagnosis was wrong and the sweep was cancelled before any code was written.

- ACTUAL CAUSE of dead scroll space app-wide: AppShell’s main carried pb-24 (96px) to clear a bottom bar that is 56px plus safe-area inset. Replaced with calc(56px + env(safe-area-inset-bottom)). This single change fixed every page including /jobs, which had been expected to need its own follow-up. The Jobs board height maths was left untouched and turned out not to be a cause at all; the planned separate Jobs item was dropped as unnecessary.

- Viewport meta needed no change. No viewport export exists, so Next.js applies width=device-width, initial-scale=1, and nothing anywhere set user-scalable or maximumScale. The /quote page “landing zoomed in” was horizontal overflow, not zoom.

- Horizontal overflow on /quote fixed at source: header action group had a non-shrinking inline flex, selected-item rows had fixed controls wider than small card interiors, and review/manual-add rows could force wide selects onto one line. All three now wrap. overflow-x: hidden was explicitly forbidden as a fix.

- All text inputs, textareas and selects forced to a 16px minimum, so iOS Safari stops auto-zooming on focus and never zooms back out.

- Settings tab strip restyled to the existing .quote-tabs pattern: filled pill with white text on the active tab, plain text inactive. Locked to horizontal movement with overflow-y hidden and touch-action pan-x, killing the vertical jerk. Same movement lock applied to BoardTabs and the Quote strip.

- Mobile drawer previously rendered MOBILE_DRAWER_ITEMS, a filtered subset showing only Vehicles, Reports and Settings. Filter removed; the drawer now mirrors the desktop sidebar’s eight items in order, role-visibility filtering untouched.

- Autosave banner z-index lowered from 200 so the burger drawer and its backdrop sit above it. Onboarding step height reduced.

- Fixed overlays were explicitly excluded from all of the above — the mobile drawer’s 100vw width and the calendar and vehicles modals’ 100dvh max-heights are inside position:fixed containers and cannot cause overflow or dead scroll.

**Brief 3 — mobile card layouts and Overview sticky save bar. PR #42.**

- /vehicles renders one card per vehicle below the md breakpoint, table unchanged above it. Cards keep the row’s keyboard affordances: focusable, role=“button”, Enter and Space open the same edit modal.

- Settings > Fleet renders cards below md. Fleet has no modal — Edit swaps a row into an inline DraftRow editor — so the card gains an in-place editing state reusing startEdit / saveEdit / updateVehicle unchanged. Delete keeps its existing confirm modal. Column-header tooltips reattached to field labels within each card via the existing FieldTooltip component.

- Agents Overview gained a mobile-only sticky save bar at z-45, between MobileBottomBar (z-40) and the setup banner (z-50), appearing on dirty and hiding on successful save. Driven by the full overviewDirty computation, NOT the existing pulse condition, which the gate revealed only fires on enabledDirty — so the bar now surfaces changes to fields that previously had no visual cue at all. Inline Save button and its pulse left in place. Setup banner shifts up by one bar height when both are visible.

- FIXUP ON THE SAME PR: a generic form-level onChange handler was recomputing dirty state for every control inside the form, including the Auto-send checkbox, which saves immediately via saveAutoSend() and is not part of the six-field snapshot. Narrowed to the text inputs on the Overview save path.

- ALSO FOLDED IN: the /agents tab strip, missed when Brief 2 restyled the others. Given the same pill treatment and horizontal lock, then a second pass to match SettingsNav’s flat full-width container rather than sitting inside a rounded card.

**Deliberately not done, and why.**

- Pinch-zoom disabling was dropped from scope. iOS Safari has ignored user-scalable=no in-browser since iOS 10 while Android Chrome honours it, so setting it would remove zoom for Android users only. It does work in a standalone home-screen PWA — see F14.

- The sticky save bar sits behind the iOS keyboard when a text field has focus. Accepted: the user closes the keyboard to navigate anywhere regardless.

- Sticky-bar rollout to other forms deliberately NOT done. See S80.

**F9. Dashboard setup checklist widget — PULLED INTO LAUNCH SCOPE 2026-09-15**

- **LAUNCH-SCOPE 2026-09-15, two reasons.** (1) It is **F51's co-requisite**: F51 provisions a partial setup on Skip, and creating an agent row hides `EmptyStateChooser`, which holds the ONLY in-app "Guided setup" link — so without F9 a partially-provisioned tenant has no way back to finish. **Key the checklist and the guided-setup entry on `agent_onboarding_status = 'skipped'` / actual missing state, NOT on "no agent exists".** (2) On its own merits: onboarding **deliberately** omits pricing (too much tweaking to sit in the flow), so without a checklist a paying tenant never learns their quote engine needs pricing set up before it will work. Build F9 first, or ship it together with F51 Slice A.

- **New checklist item from the F51 design:** "Review and enable your agent" — a partial (and a normal Finish) both leave the agent DISABLED by design, so completion must surface it.

- All tenants get it from the start. Disappears or collapses when every item is complete.

- Items: Pricing (check + approve minimum); Vehicles; Agent setup (also the onboarding recovery entry); Add your team; Email sending setup (DKIM / send-as, deliberately kept out of onboarding); Business details (depot address, contact) — **AMENDED 2026-08-13: no longer a hard prerequisite for F10.** computeRouteDistance falls back to the depot address string when depot_lat/depot_lng are null, so only depot_address is required and Settings › General already captures it. A tenant without one gets distance_status no_depot and every route offered, which is the correct fallback. Still worth prompting for, because the feature is invisible without it; Connections (Meta / FB / IG / WhatsApp, future, see F5); Send a test email.

- NOTE 2026-07-29: the “Send a test email” item overlaps F12. Build F12 first and link it here rather than building two things.

- Sequence: AFTER F8, so links do not have to be rewired.

**F10. Quote-route distance routing (survey radius) — SHIPPED AND VERIFIED 2026-08-13**

**PHASES 2a, 2b, 2c AND 2d ARE ALL SHIPPED AND MERGED.** Distance-based quote routing works end to end. A mover sets a survey drive distance in onboarding or in Agents › Rules, chooses whether a nearby customer is offered a survey alone or alongside the other options, and the auto-responder resolves real driving distance from depot to pickup postcode and offers accordingly. Verified with real inbound emails in both directions: Helston pickup at 1.30 miles offered the survey, Truro at 17.59 miles did not.

- **THE MEASURE IS ROAD MILES, DEPOT TO PICKUP. See DEC28.** Not straight-line, not the full round trip. The original entry’s model — geocode both ends, measure straight-line against a radius dragged on a map — was abandoned once the existing /api/routes/distance proxy was found. **The old bullets below are retained only as the record of what was assumed and why it was wrong.**

- **PHASE 2a (PR #55).** Threshold captured as a drive_miles entry in the existing quote_route / policy local rule — not a new tenant_config column, because the rules array already existed and is where the editing UI would look. Onboarding’s free-text “When do you offer each?” step replaced with a structured mileage question, gated on survey being among the selected routes. Empty submits as null, meaning no limit. RPC gained p_survey_drive_miles as argument 17.

- **PHASE 2b (PR #56).** resolveQuoteRoutes rewritten from first-match-wins to subtraction. Route resolution moved below extraction in computeDraftForInput so a pickup postcode exists. computeRouteDistance called with { depot: true } as origin and the extracted postcode as destination. New columns agent_runs.distance_miles and distance_status.

- **PHASE 2c (PR #57).** exclusive capture — “a survey alongside the other options” versus “just a survey” — plus the no-distance warning given visual weight and a self-clearing banner on Dashboard and Agents › Rules. RPC gained p_survey_exclusive as argument 18.

- **PHASE 2d (PR #58).** Survey distance card in Agents › Rules, closing the dead end 2c created — the banner pointed at Rules, where nothing could set a distance. Update only; the rule row always exists when the card is reachable, but the drive_miles entry usually does not, so adding it to the array is the primary case. Clearing the field removes the entry and leaves always intact.

- **STILL OPEN — F10 PHASE 2e, THE QUOTE ROUTES PICKER. NOT STARTED, AND IT HAS TWO UNANSWERED DESIGN QUESTIONS.** Which routes a tenant offers is captured once at onboarding and is unreachable afterwards. A mover who buys a van and starts doing surveys cannot turn the route on. This is the same class of gap 2c had, one level up, and it is the last structural hole in the feature. **(a) Does the picker also write tenant_config.agent_quote_route_policy.routes?** That value is computeDraft’s fallback when no rule matches and six tenants read it; write only the rule and the config goes stale, write both and they stay in sync. **(b) What happens to the drive_miles entry when survey is unticked?** Removing it means an untick-and-re-tick loses the mover’s number; leaving it dormant means a rule gating a route that is not offered. **Also note the card’s render condition is currently computed server-side from localRules, so a client-side picker means the card must appear and disappear against unsaved local state.** Deliberately deferred 2026-08-13 with the decisions unmade rather than taken tired.

- **DEPOT COORDINATES TURNED OUT NOT TO MATTER.** computeRouteDistance falls back to the depot *address string* when depot_lat / depot_lng are null, and the Routes API geocodes it. The old note that only 2 of 12 tenants have coordinates populated is therefore not a blocker — only depot_address is required, and Settings › General already captures it via Places autocomplete. **F9’s depot-capture checklist item drops from prerequisite to nice-to-have.**

- **UNRESOLVABLE DISTANCE PERMITS THE ROUTE, IT DOES NOT DROP IT.** No depot, no postcode, or a failed lookup all leave the route set unchanged. The two failure modes are not symmetric: over-offering a survey is visible to the mover and correctable, under-offering is invisible and they never learn about the jobs they did not win. Same reasoning as blank-means-no-limit.

- **COVERAGE IS NARROWER THAN IT LOOKS AND THAT IS NOT A DEFECT.** Extraction only runs inside if (selectedTemplateRow && templateNeedsMissingInfo), so runs outside that branch never produce a pickup postcode and carry distance_status null. Distance also only resolves from a FULL postcode — a town-only enquiry falls through to permit. See S167 before reading a low hit rate as a bug.

- ORIGINAL ENTRY FOLLOWS, RETAINED AS THE RECORD OF A WRONG ASSUMPTION. UNBLOCKED 2026-07-27. extracted_info.pickup_address and extracted_info.pickup_postcode are now written per inbound run, with pickup_address on a STRICT acceptance threshold specifically so the value is usable for distance maths.

- Problem this solves: the system cannot currently decide per customer whether to offer a survey, photos, or email quoting.

- Model: tenant depot address geocoded once at setup, stored as lat/lng plus a radius. Radius set by dragging on a map. **SUPERSEDED BY DEC28 — no radius UI was ever built, so there was nothing to migrate.**

- Per inbound email: geocode the extracted pickup address, measure straight-line distance against the radius. Not driving distance. **SUPERSEDED BY DEC28 — it is driving distance.**

- Fallback when the address is absent, unparseable, or geocoding fails: render the neutral multi-route sentence. **STILL TRUE.**

- Keep postcode-prefix matching as an Advanced escape hatch — it is what the current Make scenario uses for is_local.

- Surface: Local Rules is where the tenant edits this. **DELIVERED 2026-08-13 by phase 2d.**

- **THE**** ****“****NO GEOCODING ANYWHERE IN THE REPO****”**** ****LINE WAS WRONG AND COST MOST OF A SESSION’S PLANNING.** app/api/_lib/googlePlaces.ts, /api/places/autocomplete, /api/places/details, /api/routes/distance and the route_cache table all shipped 2026-07-09. The dataset decision this entry called “what gates phase 2” was never a real decision. **Record kept as a warning: a backlog line asserting something does not exist is worth one grep before a session is planned around it.**

**F11. Email threading + conversation visibility**

**SHIPPED AND VERIFIED 2026-08-04, in four parts. Parts (a), (c1) and (c2) were built; part (b) was found already built.** Part (d) is outstanding and is the only remaining work under this heading.

**PART (a) — customer’s own subject line. SHIPPED (commit c643bb3, direct to main).** Was a one-line precedence inversion, not a feature. lib/agents/pipeline.ts built draft_subject as selectedTemplate.subject ?? run.subject ?? ““, and the default library seeded”Re: Your moving quote enquiry” into every template’s subject column, so the fixed string always beat the customer’s real subject. New precedence: a deliberately-set template subject wins, then the inbound subject with “Re:” prefixing, then a final constant “Re: Your enquiry” — never the empty string. New helper lib/agents/replySubject.ts handles the prefix, case-insensitively, and does not stack “Re: Re:”. Seeded value removed from lib/agent-defaults/movers.ts so new tenants get a null subject. Migration 20260804000000_f11a_customer_subject_override.sql nulled the seeded string on exact match only, so a tenant who typed their own subject keeps it — 4 rows cleared, applied by hand. Localised prefixes (AW:, SV:) deliberately not handled.

**PART (b) — In-Reply-To and References headers. ALREADY BUILT. Verified 2026-08-04, no code written.** lib/resend/sending.ts line 42 was already setting both headers from agent_runs.message_id. Nobody knew. Confirmed live: the reply nested correctly under the original in Gmail. This is the single clearest argument for the investigate gate in the log — a whole session’s worth of planned work did not exist.

**PART (c1) — strip quoted reply history from inbound before classification. SHIPPED (commits f815b67, 4157421; 7822840 reverted as daa165b).** When a customer replies, the inbound body carries the full quoted history of our own previous email, and nothing stripped it. The classifier read agent_runs.body_text whole, and per-field extraction — standalone per inbound by DEC21 — could therefore extract stale values from a previous hop, or from our own template copy, which literally contains the phrase “The full address you’re moving from”. NON-DESTRUCTIVE BY DESIGN: new column agent_runs.body_text_stripped (nullable text, migration 20260804000001, applied by hand), original body_text never modified. Read paths prefer the stripped value and fall back when null. Codex correctly extended the same preference to the sensitivity-keyword scan, greeting extraction and postcode extraction, which read the same body. Review Queue and Escalated display selects deliberately still read raw body_text — the tenant sees what the customer actually sent. New helper lib/agents/stripQuotedReplyHistory.ts cuts at the first recognised marker: “On … wrote:” across a normalised 1-to-3 line window (mail clients hard-wrap the attribution, so single-line matching fails in production), “—–Original Message—–”, the Outlook underscore rule, the wrapped Outlook From:/Sent:/To:/Subject: block, and runs of “>” lines. No signature stripping. Localised attributions no-op by design.

**THE GUARD BUG, worth reading before writing any similar rule.** The original spec — mine — was “no-op if the marker falls within the first 2 non-empty lines”, intended to catch quote-only emails where stripping leaves nothing. The count included the marker line itself, so one line of real customer content above a marker read as 2 and tripped the guard. That is the commonest shape a short reply takes. It shipped in f815b67, survived the 7822840 “fix”, survived the revert, and was only found by tracing a production row line by line. Correct rule, now in place: count non-empty lines STRICTLY ABOVE the marker and no-op only when that count is zero.

**TESTING NOTE, will bite again.** Part (c1) CANNOT be tested by replying to a live thread. Under Candidate A the reply-to is the tenant’s own mailbox, so customer replies never reach our inbound address. Test by composing a fresh email to the inbound address with the quoted block typed into the body. TYPE IT, do not paste from a chat window or code block — Gmail imports the formatting and re-serialises it on send, which silently ate the test input twice and produced two false diagnoses.

**PART (c2) — original enquiry quoted beneath the reply. SHIPPED (commit da79ddf, direct to main).** Appended at SEND time inside the shared helper sendReplyEmail(), so all three outbound paths (approveQueueItem, editAndSendQueueItem, runAutoSend) are covered by one change. DRAFT-TIME INSERTION WAS INVESTIGATED AND REJECTED: htmlToText strips all tags with /<[^>]+>/g, so a blockquote sitting in draft_body_html is flattened to ordinary paragraphs the moment a tenant edits and saves the draft. Format is an attribution paragraph — “On Tue, 4 Aug 2026 at 13:20, Richard

wrote:”, UK date order, formatted explicitly so server region cannot change it — above a grey left-bordered blockquote. Quotes agent_runs.body_text, NOT body_text_stripped: the tenant’s mailbox should see exactly what the customer sent. “blockquote” added to the sanitizeEmailHtml allowlist; the quoted content is sanitised BEFORE concatenation, because nothing re-sanitises on the approveQueueItem and runAutoSend paths. Null body_text is a no-op, not an error. Two send-path selects widened to carry body_text, classification, from_address, message_id and received_at.

**PART (d) — OUTSTANDING. References chain accumulation. NEW 2026-08-04, NEEDS A MIGRATION.** sending.ts sets both In-Reply-To and References to the same single message_id. A correct References header is the accumulated ancestry of the thread, appended at each hop. Two hops in, the chain is already wrong, and clients that thread strictly on References lose the head of the thread — observed on the Hostinger side during testing, where the original enquiry dropped out of the thread view while Gmail still displayed it correctly. Cannot be fixed from stored data: the inbound References header is not captured. Needs agent_runs.references_header text (or a full headers jsonb), populated from the Received Emails API response, which does expose raw headers. Historical rows are not backfillable. Not urgent while conversations leave for the tenant’s mailbox after turn one, but F15 makes it mandatory — “send reply in thread” is one of that feature’s six adapter operations.

**Still true and still outstanding:** F11 was also the prerequisite for merging previously-extracted values across a thread. Extraction remains standalone per inbound by DEC21, and part (c1) now makes that safer rather than resolving it.

**F12. Tenant test email facility**

**SCOPE (a) SHIPPED, VERIFIED AND MERGED 2026-08-04 (PR #43, branch codex/f12a-test-email-facility, merge commit f501ed4).** A card on the Agents > Templates tab titled “Test A Customer Enquiry”. The tenant pastes an enquiry body, clicks Run test reply, and sees the draft their agent would produce, alongside the classified category, the template selected, the confidence, whether the run would have escalated, and which essential-info fields came back missing. Nothing is sent. Nothing is stored.

- **BODY ONLY as the input, deliberately.** No subject or sender fields. Greeting extraction reads the signoff out of the body itself, so a pasted body ending “Thanks, Joshleigh” still renders “Hi Joshleigh,” correctly. Subject only feeds sensitivity matching, which also scans the body.

- **NO PERSISTENCE AT ALL — this is the hard rule and it is architectural, not a filter.** The alternative (persist behind an is_test flag) was rejected on evidence from the gate: a persisted run inserts an agent_review_queue row carrying send_after, and the auto-send cron worker really does call sendReplyEmail. A stored test run would therefore create a live path from a fake enquiry to a real customer’s inbox. Filtering it out everywhere would have been the only thing standing between a test and a sent email, across four selects that have already drifted once (S99). VERIFIED WITH DATA: agent_runs and agent_review_queue counts unchanged at 71 and 43 across five test runs.

- **ONE IMPLEMENTATION, NOT TWO.** Codex extracted the shared read-and-compute sequence into lib/agents/computeDraft.ts and REFACTORED THE LIVE PIPELINE TO USE IT TOO, with lib/agents/testEmail.ts as the no-write entry wrapper. This was the main risk in the feature — a second orchestrator that drifts from the real one renders a draft the live path would not produce, invisibly, to a tenant who trusts it. Structurally eliminated rather than documented. Because pipeline.ts changed, the real inbound path was re-verified with a live email before merge.

- **RATE LIMIT.** Reuses config_helper_rate_limit_events with a new feature key, test_email, at the same 30/hour per tenant as config_helper. No migration needed — feature_key is free text. Denials are NOT logged to ai_feedback for this feature. Cost is 2-4 LLM calls per run (classification, extraction with a possible retry, greeting), and extraction runs before the escalation exit, so even a run that would escalate pays for it.

- The page-level “Email Response Generator” stub in app/(app)/agents/page.tsx was left untouched. Different feature — see S82.

- **IT PAID FOR ITSELF WITHIN MINUTES.** The first two runs surfaced S64 behaviour (the missing-info block asking for the pickup address when the paste says “moving from Truro TR1 2AB”) and a clear misclassification (see S103 and the DEC17 note). Both would have cost a ten-minute Gmail round trip each to find. Every remaining session pays less verification tax because this exists.

- **SCOPE (b) STILL NOT BUILT** — a real email to the tenant’s own inbound address, which would additionally test address resolution, own-domain skip and dedupe, i.e. the “my agent receives nothing” class of problem. Different build. Unchanged in priority.

- Still overlaps the F9 “Send a test email” checklist item. F9 should now LINK this rather than build anything.

**F16. Agents page restructure — Overview / Generate / Rules / Inbox — SHIPPED AND VERIFIED 2026-08-09**

- **SHIPPED 2026-08-09. PR #49, squash-merged, branch deleted.** Five commits: 895fd66 (shell), 0d2ccc5 (one template per category), 9c9a652 (Rules), 69c8570 (Inbox + mark-as-done), 6c83d32 (sort fix + glow). 16 files, +803 −321. Migration 20260809000000_f16_escalation_resolution.sql, applied by hand.

- **COMMIT 1 — THE SHELL.** Six tabs to four. Inbox holds Review Queue and Escalated as folders with a URL-addressable folder param, and folder switching goes through the same unsaved-changes guard as tab switching, because both surfaces hold editable drafts. Tab content moved unchanged. Old slugs deliberately NOT mapped — nothing outside the app linked to them, so a compatibility shim would have been written only to be deleted. Two callers were rewired instead: the navigate-to-categories window event and the reviewTemplates onboarding link.

- **COMMIT 2 — ONE TEMPLATE PER CATEGORY.** Enforced in createTemplate AND updateTemplate server-side, not only in the dropdown — the UI check was verified to be advisory by forcing a collision with a direct SQL write and confirming the server still refused. “Unassigned” removed (a template with no category can never fire) and system categories removed (escalate means no auto-reply, always). Category cell in the Templates table became a jump link to that category’s row. Add-category moved INTO the template drawer, with the key derived from the label rather than typed, because the one-per-category rule makes the dropdown empty for a settled tenant and the drawer would otherwise be a dead end. **The riskiest part was state preservation**: createCategory revalidates /agents while the drawer holds an unsaved draft, and losing a tenant’s typed email body to a button meant to help them was the worst outcome available. Verified explicitly and it survives.

- **NO DATABASE CONSTRAINT, DELIBERATELY.** A unique constraint on category_id would make the rule structural, but only Joe’s Movers was verified as 1:1 and a constraint fails loudly on migrate for any tenant that is not. Deferred until every tenant’s data is checked.

- **COMMIT 3a — RULES.** Reordered to Sensitivity keywords, Classifier corrections, Postcode map — most-used first, rarely-touched last. Corrections moved off Inbox (where you see what happened) onto Rules (where you set behaviour) and now render ALWAYS with an empty state, rather than appearing only once used; a section that vanishes when empty changes the tab’s shape under the tenant and makes the feature undiscoverable. Correction detail view built, closing S114b. Reply-to pointer card deleted; the working field on Overview untouched.

- **COMMIT 3b — ESCALATED BECOMES A WORKLIST.** Mark-as-done, reversible, done rows hidden behind a toggle. **Implemented as nullable escalation_resolved_at / escalation_resolved_by on agent_runs, NOT as a status change** — status stays ‘escalated’ so escalations remain countable, which D13 and D14 both depend on. Columns are prefixed deliberately: agent_runs.status already describes the run’s lifecycle, so a bare resolved_at would read as “the run finished”, a different fact. Verified in SQL that status survives resolution. The Inbox badge went live by lifting escalated rows into AgentsTabs following S84’s pattern, which also collapsed a duplicate realtime subscription — EscalatedTab and AgentsTabs had both been subscribed to agent_runs for the same tenant.

- **COMMIT 3c — A REGRESSION 3b CAUSED.** 3b was briefed to sort unresolved-first without first checking whether a sort control existed. It did: Review Queue has a persisted Sort dropdown with Date and Needs action, and the hardwired pre-sort was applied underneath both, making them behave identically and rendering Date useless. Fixed by making unresolved-first the DEFAULT choice rather than a rule the tenant cannot switch off. Escalated has no sort control, so its hardwired ordering is correct and stayed. **The lesson is about briefing, not code: an ordering change to a list is a change to a control if a control exists, and that must be checked before it is briefed.**

- **THE ERG WAS BRIEFED FOR DELETION IN ERROR AND RESTORED IN 3b.** The operator said the Email Response Generator belonged on Generate. A grep then showed it lived at page level, outside the tab switch, so it appeared on every tab. That discovery was converted into “remove it” without returning to the instruction. It should have been a question. Restored into Generate in 3b, disabled, Phase 2 pill kept, between Test A Customer Enquiry and Categories. **Working rule added to project instructions as a result: when a discovery contradicts an instruction, return to it rather than reinterpret it.**

- **CLOSES:** S104 (test-card density, absorbed), S114b (correction detail view), most of the unnumbered LOCAL RULES TAB REDESIGN item, and the page-level ERG stub question. **DOES NOT CLOSE S82** — the ERG spec is untouched and the stub is now visible on Generate rather than everywhere.

- **RAISED BY THIS WORK:** F18 (unbounded fetch, launch-blocking), S124, S125.

- Verification standard held throughout: every commit checked in the running app on a Vercel preview, not on a green build. Twelve checks on 3b, ten on 3a, nine each on 1 and 2. The two genuine failures both surfaced that way and neither would have shown in a build.

**F17. Repoint the generator to descriptions, and reseed Testing Mover (A3) — PARTIALLY SHIPPED 2026-08-05**

- NEW 2026-08-04. **F17a and F17b SHIPPED 2026-08-05 evening. The repoint itself and the reseed are both still open, and neither now blocks anything.**

- **F17a SHIPPED (commit e499837).** The enable path in updateAgentOverview called writeClassifierPrompt(), which ran an LLM and wrote a row to tenant_agent_classifier_prompts, and blocked enabling if that call failed. Since PR #44 nothing reads that row at classification time, so a tenant could be blocked from enabling their agent because an LLM failed to produce something no code consumes. Removed. Enabling is now instant with no LLM call and no write to the dead table. Verified on Joe’s Movers: toggle off and on, then a live test email that still classified and drafted normally. DEC22 is now retired in practice as well as on paper.

- **F17b SHIPPED (commit 0a941b5, migrations 20260805000002 and 20260805000003).** S112, the colliding sort_order. See the S112 entry for the failure and the fix.

- **STILL OPEN: THE REPOINT ITSELF, AND ONE QUESTION IN FRONT OF IT.** The investigation on 2026-08-05 found that generateClassifierPrompt.ts is not the only thing that could write descriptions. requestProposedConfig / requestProposedAdditions in generateConfig.ts ALREADY call an LLM that produces a description per proposed category, and approveAgentConfig / approveAgentAdditions already write it. So before repointing the old generator, decide whether F17 repoints it or deletes it and extends the one that already does this job. **This is the same shape of mistake as A2 versus F1 phase 1 — two things authoring the same field, designed without reading each other.** Answer it by reading what generateConfig.ts actually produces, not by assuming.

- Also established: there is no guaranteed description for tenant-added categories. createCategory and updateCategory take the form value and write null if blank, and it stays null indefinitely. The nine seeded categories get curated copy; anything a tenant adds by hand may get nothing. That gap is the real argument for the repoint.

- OVERWRITE RULE, agreed 2026-08-05 and not yet contradicted: the generator never overwrites a non-null description. It fills blanks only. A tenant’s own words are never replaced by a machine’s.

- lib/agents/generateClassifierPrompt.ts currently authors prompt text that nothing reads any more. Repoint it to write tenant_agent_categories.description at onboarding instead. Same model, same tenant context, different target field. Deleting it is NOT the plan — see DEC23.

- Descriptions are now load-bearing: a description IS the classifier’s decision-tree step. A tenant who writes “storage stuff” gets weak classification and no warning. The generator’s job is to turn rough tenant input into a description that classifies well, and the output is visible and editable, which the old prompt text never was.

- **RESEED TESTING MOVER — DEFERRED 2026-08-05, DELIBERATELY, AND IT BLOCKS NOTHING.** It is mummified: two templates with a null category_id that can never fire, a dead quote_request_no_addresses category with no template, and a template whose stored body does not match what the pipeline sends. The reseed was scheduled because Testing Mover was the test bed and its state kept muddying results. That reason has expired — Joe’s Movers (99c73f1b…) is clean, post-#46, and carried every verification on 2026-08-05. **CONDITION OF THE DEFERRAL: do not test on Testing Mover.** Any result taken off it is unreliable, and that is exactly how a wrong conclusion gets drawn. When the reseed does happen, the open question is whether to delete the tenant and sign up fresh or re-provision in place; the deciding factor is whether the Cornwall inbound email address is easily recreated, which was not established. Note the sort_order migration moved Testing Mover’s system categories from the end of its decision tree to the front, so its current shape differs from what it had before 2026-08-05.

- Note when scoping: postcode signals (D, D.5, E in the old skeleton) are absent from the new spine. extractPostcode and lookupTown still do that work in code, but nothing tells the classifier about postcodes any more. Cornwall’s scilly type was classified BY postcode, so if a Scilly-like type is ever wanted again its description has to carry the postcodes explicitly. That is a generator concern and belongs here.

**F18. Review Queue and Escalated fetch unbounded — LAUNCH-BLOCKING**

- **NEW 2026-08-09, raised by the operator while scoping F16 3b.** Review Queue fetches EVERY row for the tenant with no limit. Escalated has a .limit(50) with no “show more” and no indication that anything was cut, so row 51 is silently invisible — arguably the worse of the two, because it fails quietly rather than slowly.

- **Every row carries three bodies:** inbound email body, draft body, and where edited, original_draft_body_html. And refreshRows() in AgentsTabs refetches the whole set on window focus, on visibilitychange, and on realtime resubscribe.

- **The arithmetic is the argument.** A mover receiving 20 enquiries a day is ~600 rows a month, ~7,000 a year, each with three bodies of text, refetched in full every time they switch back to the tab — on a phone, on mobile data. Test tenants hide this completely: Joe’s Movers has 61 review queue rows and 24 escalated.

- **What a fix needs, and why it is not a small item:** a server-side limit with cursor pagination; bodies fetched on expand rather than up front, since that is most of the payload; and a refresh path that does not reset the tenant to page one on every focus event. Naive pagination bolted onto the current refresh does exactly that, and it is worse than no pagination because it interrupts work rather than merely being slow.

- **Probably also wants a retention or auto-archive answer.** An eighteen-month-old resolved row has no reason to be fetched at all. Related to S108 (retention must clear correction email bodies) but not the same item — S108 is about what is stored, this is about what is sent.

- **A display-only cap does NOT fix this** and was considered and rejected: slicing to 15 in the component still fetches every row and every body. The weight is in the payload, not the render.

- **F16 3b did the cheap half already:** both folders now sort unresolved-first, so everything needing action is at the top regardless of how many rows sit below it. That makes a future cap safe rather than dangerous, but it does nothing about payload.

- **Launch-blocking because it degrades continuously with success.** The busiest movers — the ones most worth keeping — get the worst experience.

- **REPRICED 2026-08-09 EVENING. THE VOLUME FIGURE IN THIS ENTRY IS CONTRADICTED BY CANDIDATE A IN THIS SAME DOCUMENT, AND THE CONTRADICTION IS UNRESOLVED.** This entry argues from 20 enquiries a day, roughly 7,000 rows a year. Candidate A states that inbound at launch is website contact forms, that phone enquiries are out of scope entirely, and that per-tenant volume is therefore dozens a month rather than hundreds — concluding explicitly that the in-app inbox does not need to scale before it needs to be good. That is roughly 400 rows a year, not 7,000.

- **WHAT SURVIVES THE CONTRADICTION.** The silent Escalated truncation at row 51 fails quietly at any volume and should be fixed regardless. Three bodies per row refetched on every focus event is still wrong at 400 rows. And nothing anywhere retires an old row, so the set only ever grows. **WHAT DOES NOT SURVIVE:** the claim that this breaks at the exact moment the product starts working. At the Candidate A figure it degrades over years, not weeks. **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-09 LATE. THE INSTRUCTION TO SIZE THIS AGAINST CANDIDATE A’S LOCK IS VOID.** Candidate A cannot settle the volume question any more, because F15a slice 1 changed what inbound is. A connected mailbox delivers everything arriving at the tenant’s real address, not only contact-form submissions, so the low figure’s premise is gone. **The 20-a-day arithmetic is back in play for any tenant on a mailbox connection, and it arrives at the same time the mailbox does.** The silent Escalated truncation still fails at any volume and is still the part worth fixing first. Size the pagination-and-payload half against mailbox-connected volume, not contact-form volume.

**F19. Per-tenant sending identity (DEC2’s send-as half) — LAUNCH-BLOCKING**

- **NEW 2026-08-09 EVENING. This has never had an item number and it should have.** DEC2 locks the transport as “Resend inbound + DKIM-delegated send-as”. The inbound half is built and live. **The send-as half is not built**, and the only trace of it anywhere in this document is an F9 checklist line reading “Email sending setup (DKIM / send-as, deliberately kept out of onboarding)” — a checklist item pointing at a feature that does not exist.

- **Consequence today:** a mover’s replies leave on our infrastructure rather than their own domain. That is a deliverability problem and a trust problem, and it is visible to their customer, which puts it in the unrecoverable class this document reserves for reputation damage.

- **It also blocks the Cornwall cutover.** OPEN AND NOT ESTABLISHED: what the existing Make scenario currently sends as. If it is Cornwall’s own address via Hostinger SMTP, switching to the app changes the visible sender on every reply.

- Scope is a real feature, not a small item: per-tenant sending domain, DKIM record generation and verification UI, a verified/unverified state the pipeline respects, and a fallback for tenants who have not completed it. Sequence relative to S43, which supplies the domain machinery.

**F13. Automated test infrastructure**

NEW 2026-07-29. Roughly half of the 29 July session’s elapsed time was manual setup, teardown and re-verification. Most of it is automatable; the blocker is that there is nowhere safe for an agent to run it.

- BLOCKER: a second Supabase project as a disposable test database. Codex correctly refuses to create and destroy tenants against the real dev project that .env.local points at. Everything else is downstream of this.

- Also needed: a separate preview environment (or per-branch env var overrides) so failure injection — breaking OPENAI_API_KEY, for instance — does not break the environment being used for other work.

- Then: Playwright for signup, onboarding and toggle flows against a branch preview; direct SQL for assertions; a signed POST at /api/agents/inbound instead of sending from Gmail for pipeline coverage.

- Stays manual regardless: does the rendered email look right in a real mail client, and does the UI feel wrong. Judgement, not assertion.

- Also wanted: a tenant-nuke helper. Note that most enable-path resets do NOT need a fresh signup — deleting the agent’s classifier prompt rows and setting enabled = false restores the same state.

- F-sized. Worth its own session before much more manual testing accumulates.

- CONSIDERED AND DEFERRED 2026-07-30. Raised as a candidate to jump the queue and declined: it is a session, not an item, and its value scales with how many build sessions remain. With launch as the near-term goal, the signed-POST harness alone would capture most of the benefit at a fraction of the cost, and F12 scope (a) delivers a similar loop as a shippable tenant feature. Revisit if the session count before launch grows rather than shrinks.

**F15. Unified mailbox connector — SLICE 1 SHIPPED AND VERIFIED 2026-08-09**

NEW 2026-08-01. How a tenant’s existing inbound email reaches the app without changing the address on their promo material. Not launch-blocking given the reply-to decision (see Decision candidates), but looking increasingly inevitable for v1.

- **F15a SLICE 1 SHIPPED AND VERIFIED 2026-08-09 (PR #50, branch codex/f15a-slice1-imap-inbound, squash merge 7eb5005; UI follow-up aec3767 direct to main).** One table, tenant_mailboxes. Connect and verify on password IMAP, fetch new since UID cursor, fetch one full message separately, hand off to the existing pipeline. Manual sync trigger at POST /api/mailbox/sync, Bearer CRON_SECRET. No mailbox_messages table, no OAuth, no writeback, no polling schedule — all as scoped. imapflow is the library. **Verified end to end against a real IONOS mailbox, not a test panel.**

- **THE MIGRATION CARRIED AN UNDECLARED CHANGE THAT WAS CORRECT AND IS WORTH KNOWING.** agent_runs.resend_email_id had NOT NULL dropped. It was not in the brief and was not mentioned in the report-back; it was caught by reading the SQL before running it. It is necessary — a mailbox-sourced run has no Resend email id — but it is a one-way door in practice: restoring NOT NULL later only works while no null rows exist, and there will be null rows from the first mailbox run onward. The same migration added agent_runs.source, default resend_inbound, mailbox rows set to mailbox.

- **TWO DEFECTS FOUND IN VERIFICATION, BOTH FIXED IN THE SAME PR (commit db56c02), AND BOTH WOULD HAVE BEEN INVISIBLE ON A TEST PANEL.** (1) The UID range was inclusive of the stored cursor, and an IMAP UID search of the form n:* ALWAYS returns the highest UID in the mailbox even when nothing matches — so every sync re-processed the newest message forever. Three syncs produced five agent_runs rows from four messages, the same RFC822 Message-ID appearing three times. (2) Dedupe was not running on the mailbox path at all, despite loopPrevention.ts keying on tenant_id + message_id, which would have caught it. **Either alone is bad; together, pointed at a live business mailbox, it re-drafts the newest enquiry on every poll.** Fix: fetch from cursor+1, explicitly discard anything at or below the stored cursor rather than trusting the range, and move mailbox dedupe ahead of the agent_runs insert.

- **THE DEDUPE DEFECT IS THE ONE TO REMEMBER, BECAUSE IT WAS REPORTED AS NOT NEEDING A CHANGE.** The brief asked Codex to check what dedupe keyed on before wiring anything up. It reported correctly that loopPrevention.ts keys on tenant_id + RFC822 Message-ID and therefore needed no change — true of the function, false of whether the mailbox path reached it. **A correct answer to the question asked is not evidence that the thing works.**

- **CURSOR INITIALISATION ON CONNECT — BUILT AND VERIFIED.** Connect sets sync_cursor to the current highest UID in the watch folder, so a first sync ingests only mail arriving after connection. Without it, connecting a real business mailbox would drag the entire existing inbox through the classifier in one run. Verified by deleting the mailbox row, reconnecting against an inbox already holding five messages, and confirming the cursor came back as 5 and the first sync fetched 0. **Note the fallback is verified.lastUid ?? null — unexamined behaviour if lastUid cannot be read, and null is exactly the case where the guard is needed. See S129.**

- **THE REGRESSION CHECK WAS NEARLY MISSED, AND THE REASON GENERALISES.** This slice modified shared code — the migration touched agent_runs, and the fix commit changed pipeline.ts and loopPrevention.ts, all of which sit on the live Resend webhook path. Main deploys automatically, so production was running modified code that had never been exercised. **The reason local testing worked today is the same reason the gap opened:** the mailbox path is pull-based so everything ran locally, and the Resend path only ever hits production, so it was never looked at. Checked afterwards and clean — a real email to Joe’s Resend inbound address produced a row with source defaulted to resend_inbound, resend_email_id populated, and a review queue row behaving as before. **RULE: when a slice adds a second path through shared code, the verification list needs a line for the path that was already working.**

- **VERIFICATION THAT WAS ACTUALLY RUN, recorded because it is the standard this feature should be held to.** Ciphertext in the column not the password; RLS returning nothing to an authenticated tenant; a wrong password rejected without overwriting a working credential; a real external email ingested, queued, and NOT sent; a repeat sync with no new mail returning fetched 0; and a forced re-fetch returning fetched 1, processed 0, skipped 1. The last one is what proves dedupe rather than the cursor fix masking it.

- THE DECISIVE PROBLEM IS NOT TRANSPORT. Forwarding solves delivery in ten minutes at any provider. The problem is that we cannot see what the tenant already did. Forwarding is one-way, so a mover replying from their phone is invisible to us, and with auto-send live the customer gets two answers, possibly contradicting on price. That is the week-one trust failure, and it is worse than any UI bug because the tenant looks incompetent to their own customer.

- IMAP ALONE IS NOT UNIVERSAL. Microsoft killed password auth for Exchange Online outright; Google requires app passwords behind 2FA and keeps narrowing them. Universal coverage means three adapters behind one interface, not one connector.

- ADAPTER 1 — IMAP/SMTP. Covers Hostinger, cPanel hosts, Fastmail, 123-reg, most host-provided mailboxes. Password auth. Buildable and testable today with zero external approval. BUILD THIS FIRST, and not as a warm-up: there is a Hostinger mailbox to test against, and it proves the whole sync engine before any OAuth paperwork.

- ADAPTER 2 — Gmail API. Covers Gmail and Google Workspace. OAuth. Google restricted-scope verification is the long pole and is calendar time, not work time. Start the application early; it runs in parallel with development.

- ADAPTER 3 — Microsoft Graph. Covers M365 and Outlook. OAuth, lighter verification than Google.

- ADAPTER 4, LATER — Meta channels (Facebook, Instagram, WhatsApp). Not mailboxes, but the same shape: connected channel, inbound message, outbound reply, thread. Build the interface right now and they slot in rather than being bolted on. See F5 and D11.

- THE INTERFACE IS SIX OPERATIONS. Connect and verify (credentials in, prove they work, report the address being watched). Fetch new since cursor (cursor = bookmark of how far we have read; IMAP UIDs, Gmail history IDs, Graph delta tokens — the adapter translates). Fetch one full message (separate from the list call so the list stays cheap). Send reply in thread (requires In-Reply-To and References, which is F11 work — F15 makes it mandatory rather than nice-to-have). Move or label (IMAP moves, Gmail labels, Graph moves — same intent, three mechanisms). List folders (so the tenant picks what to watch and where processed mail goes). Everything above the adapter — classifier, templates, queue, auto-send — stays untouched.

- TWO TABLES. tenant_mailboxes: provider type, address, encrypted credentials, watch folder, replied folder, escalated folder, sync cursor, last-successful-sync timestamp, status. mailbox_messages: the mirror — provider message id, thread id, from/to/subject/body, direction (inbound or outbound), received-at, link to agent_runs where one exists.

- THE GUARD THAT JUSTIFIES THE WHOLE FEATURE. Before drafting or auto-sending, check the thread for an existing outbound reply that is not ours. If a human already answered from their phone, skip and mark it. Sits in lib/agents/pipeline.ts after dedupe, before classify. ONLY WORKS IF OUTBOUND IS SYNCED TOO — the tenant’s Sent folder, not just their inbox. Easy to forget when scoping, expensive to retrofit.

- DECISIONS TAKEN 2026-08-01. Watch folder defaults to INBOX and is tenant-changeable. Folder writeback is in v1, not deferred. Gmail has labels rather than folders; the adapter presents both as one flat pickable list and the app does not care.

- WRITEBACK GUARDS, NON-NEGOTIABLE. Send first, then move, never the reverse — a successful send with a failed move leaves the reply gone to the customer and the email sitting in the inbox looking unanswered, so the tenant replies again by hand. Move failures must be retried and surfaced, not swallowed. Moving an already-moved message is a no-op, not an error; the adapter tolerates “already there” and “not found” quietly. Target folders are created on connect, never silently at move time. A message that has left the watch folder is out of scope — do not fight a tenant who filed it themselves.

- HARD RULE, and a decision candidate in its own right: we are moving mail inside a real business’s live mailbox. Only ever move a message we hold an agent_run for. One at a time. Never a batch operation. Never a delete. A bug that bulk-moves the wrong messages is unrecoverable from the tenant’s side and is a trust event they would never come back from.

- CREDENTIAL STORAGE is the biggest new liability in the app. IMAP means holding a working password to the tenant’s mailbox. Encrypted at rest, service-role only, never rendered back to the UI. Deserves its own decision entry rather than being buried in a brief.

- COST SHAPE. IMAP needs polling — a worker on a schedule, per mailbox. At one tenant that is nothing; at two hundred, polling every minute is 288,000 checks a day. Gmail and Graph can push. Poll interval is a real decision, not a default.

- MODEL FOR THE BEHAVIOUR already exists in the operator’s Make scenario: listen for incoming mail, classify, respond or escalate, then move the message to a “Charlie replied” folder, with escalations also moved to a team feed folder. Same shape. NOTE: that scenario sends unattended into a mailbox the operator also works in, which is safe because they are the operator. For tenants the thread-check guard is what makes it safe, and it must be built in rather than assumed.

- **SIZE — REPRICED 2026-08-09 EVENING. THE SPLIT MATTERS AND THE OLD LINE WAS QUOTED BACK AS IF IT WERE A FINDING.** “Multi-session feature, not one brief” was written while scoping this entry, not while building anything, and it describes F15 ENTIRE — two tables, six operations, credential encryption, a polling worker, writeback guards. It does not describe F15a slice 1.

- **F15a SLICE 1 IS ONE SESSION.** One table (tenant_mailboxes); mailbox_messages is not needed yet, because slice 1 feeds straight into the existing inbound handler and agent_runs. Connect and verify against Hostinger on password IMAP. Fetch new since UID cursor, and fetch one full message as a separate call so the list stays cheap. Then hand off to the pipeline that already exists — classification, templates, queue, auto-send and dedupe are all built and working. **Slice 1 adds an inbound SOURCE and nothing downstream.** Explicitly excluded: OAuth, writeback and folder moves, the writeback guards, and any polling schedule. Manual trigger first, exactly as auto-send lived for weeks.

- **TWO THINGS DO NOT COMPRESS, AND BOTH ARE SLICE-1 CONCERNS EVEN THOUGH THEY LOOK LATER.** (1) Credential storage is the biggest new liability in the app and is Candidate B, still unlocked — it is a decision rather than a build, and it wants making BEFORE the column exists, not after. (2) The thread-check guard needs the tenant’s SENT folder, not just the inbox, and that is a later slice. **Until it lands, F15 inbound plus auto-send-on is MORE dangerous than the current forward-free setup**, because the app is now reading the same mailbox the tenant replies from. **Slice 1 must therefore land with auto-send off for mailbox-sourced runs.**

- **SEQUENCING GAIN NOT PREVIOUSLY RECORDED: F15 makes Candidate C answerable rather than competing with it.** Once the app can see a tenant’s full mailbox including Sent, there are real examples of how that specific mover writes — the context a freehand responder would need and currently has none of. It also delivers the thread-check guard, so a freehand mistake can be caught by a human replying rather than racing them.

- D5. NEW 2026-07-30. Two-register seed copy — neutral plus formal, with casual folded into neutral because the current copy already reads casual-friendly. Considered when “Dear” was dropped (S70) and the register mismatch between greeting and body was raised. What it buys: matching register for traditional firms, no functional change. What it costs: roughly 30 bodies at two registers, plus roughly 60 essential-info field rows, because tone lives in the asks and hints more than the bodies — “anything awkward about access at either end, like stairs or tight parking” is the informal part. That breaks the shared pickup_address / dropoff_address / move_date rows locked under DEC20, since a second register means a second set. Permanent tax: every future copy fix doubles, every new situation doubles, the typed-extra generation path must generate both registers, verification doubles at every missing-count, and check_movers_copy.ts needs a register dimension or the two sets silently drift. Three registers was rejected outright as the DEC17 combinatorial trap. REVISIT ONLY on real customer signal that the current register loses business. Gated behind S69.

- D10. Voice onboarding (speech-to-text + text-to-speech). Cost trivial. Deferred for build complexity, not money.

- D13. **NEW 2026-08-07, from S113 item (b). STEP 2’s sensitivity list stays exactly as it is.** Seven triggers in the fixed spine — refunds, complaints, legal disputes, partnerships, explicit callback requests, same-day-with-no-detail, and “anything needing human judgement” — with no tenant control over any of them. The audit found two of them are genuinely the tenant’s policy rather than ours, and decided not to act, because the worst case is an escalation the tenant handles by hand, which is what they would do anyway, and because most of these have no default auto-reply path. **TRIGGER TO REVISIT: escalation volume dominated by escalation_reason**** ****“****sensitivity****”**** ****once there is real traffic.** That is one query, and it exists only because S110 shipped. Watch the human-judgement catch-all in particular — it is currently absorbing every follow-up email through model self-justification, with no rule instructing it to.

- D14. **NEW 2026-08-07, from S113 item (a). The seven PR #46 seed descriptions keep our wording for now, including the exclusion clauses.** The principle is unchanged — we wrote sentences about what a mover’s service covers and seeded them into every signup — but every consequence examined turned out minor. The “Not X” clauses suppress multi-match escalation rather than merely describing, so removing them raises escalations; a wrong category between quote_request and man_and_van produces a near-identical reply; and the reply’s substance follows the tenant’s own quote route policy, not the seed description. **TRIGGER TO REVISIT: a tenant whose enquiries land in a category that does not fit their business, or answers flipping when a mover edits their category list.** Both are now visible because the escalation badge names the colliding pair. See also S121, which is the linkage this depends on.

- **S43 REPRICED 2026-08-09 EVENING — IT IS NOT COSMETIC AND IT NOW GATES THE OAUTH TRACK.** The cheapness above is true of the email half only. Two things sit on top of it. **(1) LAUNCH-BLOCKING ON ITS OWN.** Live inbound is inbound-{tenant_id}@chualexua.resend.app, a Resend sandbox domain. Nothing launches on that. **(2) IT GATES F15 ADAPTER 2.** Google restricted-scope verification needs a Cloud project, an OAuth consent screen, a restricted-scope justification, a demo video, and a CASA security assessment — third-party, paid, weeks of calendar time — and it requires a VERIFIED HOMEPAGE DOMAIN AND A HOSTED PRIVACY POLICY, both on a domain we own. So the Google application cannot be fired early in parallel as was assumed; it is gated behind S43, which is gated behind the brand decision. **The brand decision is a scheduling item now, not a cosmetic one.** Note this blocks nothing for Cornwall — Hostinger is adapter 1, password IMAP, no external approval — and Microsoft Graph is a lighter process again.

- S49. CLOSED 2026-07-28, not a bug.

- S50. **CLOSED 2026-08-04 AS WON’T-FIX. THE PREMISE WAS WRONG.** Was: “template editor is plain-text and escapes HTML on save, so a tenant editing one word destroys the markup of the entire body, invisibly.” Investigated properly on 4 August and disproved. THE EDITOR NEVER EDITS HTML. It edits tenant_agent_templates.body_text in a textarea, and textToHtml regenerates body_html from that plain text on save. Plain text in, HTML out, is sound design, and escaping < and > is CORRECT behaviour for genuine plain text. Verified lossless on a real F7-provisioned tenant: body_html 233 chars before a one-word edit, 236 after (the delta is newlines textToHtml writes between paragraphs), real

- tags throughout, {{missing_info_block}} still in its own paragraph, {{signature}} intact. THE ACTUAL FAULT WAS FOSSIL DATA ON ONE TENANT. Testing Mover’s body_text holds literal

- tags from a pre-F7 or out-of-repo path, and textToHtml escaped them faithfully — garbage in, garbage out, function behaving correctly. Every non-drawer writer derives body_text via htmlToText, and no live writer puts HTML into that column. NOT FIXED, AND DELIBERATELY SO: the affected tenants are disposable and scheduled for deletion. CONSEQUENCES: (1) this is NOT launch-blocking and a new tenant cannot hit it; (2) F7 session 2b loses its urgency — the rich-text-editor decision of 29 July was taken against a misdiagnosis; (3) S68 (invisible dynamic regions) is still real and still wants a legibility fix, but it is a UX improvement, not data destruction. DIAGNOSTIC LESSON, the expensive one: the first reproduction attempt ran against an already-corrupted template and produced a false pass, then a false diagnosis. When testing a data-corruption bug, restore a known-clean row FIRST, or you are testing a corpse.

- S52. WhatsApp number capture. Onboarding field + tenant config column + wa.me link formatting (international format, drop the leading zero) + validation, or the link 404s silently. Unblocks the {{whatsapp_number}} hint on the photos and clearance_photos fields.

- S54. FOLDED INTO S69 on 2026-07-30. Variant pools for the six new situations — all shipped with one body each, so every tenant ticking Storage receives byte-identical copy. Proposed scope was three variants each for storage and clearance. It is a copy decision and belongs in the copy review pass.

- S55. **FIXED 2026-08-04 (PR #44, squash merge, main e60c57e).** The per-tenant prompt snapshot is gone from the classify path. lib/agents/buildClassifierPrompt.ts now assembles the prompt on every classify from a fixed spine (lib/agents/prompts/classifier-spine.ts) plus one decision-tree step per row in tenant_agent_categories, using each row’s description as the step condition. computeDraft.ts calls the assembler and passes the result to classifyEmail as systemPromptOverride. No LLM call, no stored text, no regeneration step. VERIFIED on Joe’s Movers: a piano_move category inserted by hand in the SQL editor classified correctly on the next test run with no toggle and no restart, then was deleted again. Caveat: single run, see S59. Original entry follows. New categories do not reach the classifier. The per-tenant classifier prompt is a stored text snapshot with category keys baked in as literal strings; the pipeline hands it to the LLM verbatim and never queries the category table. Any category created after the agent is first enabled is invisible to the classifier permanently. UPDATED 2026-07-29: this got MORE important, not less. Enable no longer regenerates (see S56), so the accidental regeneration that used to pick up new categories is gone. The regenerate endpoint (S38) is now the only path that refreshes the prompt, and nothing in the app calls it. Bites when the typed-extra path lands. Cross-references DEC22.

- S56. FIXED 2026-07-29 on the S58 PR. Enable toggle regenerated the classifier prompt wholesale on every off->on transition. Confirmed as a REGRESSION of the 17 July 2d-fix, which had already made enable skip when an active row exists — the guard survived in name (skipManualSeed: true) but only protects rows with generated_from IS NULL, so previously generated rows fell through. Proven with data: one test agent accumulated six versions in forty minutes, all different, each a full LLM call. Fix: new skipIfActiveExists option on writeClassifierPrompt, checked before the LLM call and before activateClassifierPrompt so no row is deactivated and no paid generation runs. Passed from the enable path only; the regenerate endpoint keeps force-regenerate as its default. The DEC18 requirement for phase 2 is unchanged.

- S59. **RESOLVED 2026-08-05. DOWNGRADED, NOT CLOSED.** The 2026-08-04 entry below blamed model sampling and is wrong. Investigation gate first, three questions. (1) Capability probe, run directly against the live key: gpt-5-mini rejects temperature outright — 400, unsupported_value, only the default is accepted — and also rejects reasoning_effort “none”, lowest accepted being “minimal”. It DOES accept seed, though acceptance is not proof of effect. gpt-5.1-mini and gpt-5.1-nano do not exist on this account; the key can see gpt-5 through gpt-5.6, and gpt-5.4-mini is present and is the likely candidate if a temperature-capable mini tier is ever wanted. (2) A 30-call harness — 5 enquiries, 6 runs each, prompt SHA-256 logged per run — returned 0/30 category changes, contradicting the six-run finding. (3) The reproduction through F12(a) on the original enquiry returned 6 packing / 2 quote_request. Difference between the two: the harness enquiries were clean, the real one was “can you give me a price for wrapping and boxing up my kitchen before the move?”, which is a price ask AND a service ask AND carries move context. quote_request’s description said “asking for a moving quote”; packing’s said only “Questions about packing.” — so a price request for packing matched quote_request and half-matched packing. Two defensible answers, and the model split. Rewriting packing’s description to own price asks explicitly took it to 8/8. The same treatment took storage to 8/8. **The confidence floor option is dead** — the wrong answer arrived at 90%, the joint-highest confidence in the set, and the right answer at 85%, so confidence does not track correctness. No majority vote, no model swap, no temperature: all three original options were unnecessary. **What remains, and why this is downgraded rather than closed:** the model still samples — confidence drifts 85 to 92 across identical runs — and that sampling will flip the category again wherever a question has more than one defensible answer. Every vague description is a latent S59. The defence is specificity, and nothing checks for it. The original pre-A1 complaint in this entry (generator-authored step ordering varying per tenant and per run) is OBSOLETE — A1 removed the generator from the prompt path and order now comes from sort_order on the category rows. Original entry follows for history.

- S60. REFRAMED 2026-08-01 after a full investigation, parked without a fix. The old wording — “realtime was wired elsewhere but not on this surface” — is wrong. The subscription IS wired and DOES connect: temporary diagnostics confirmed status SUBSCRIBED on the correct channel. What does not happen is that Postgres change events for agent_review_queue never reach the client, so the handlers never fire. Escalated (agent_runs) live-updates correctly on the same connection, so realtime itself works. RULED OUT, all verified by SQL against the live database: publication membership (both tables are in supabase_realtime), RLS policies (identical four-policy shape on both tables), replica identity (both ‘d’), duplicate channel names, and the three fetch paths (all intact after the S84 state lift). CAUSE UNKNOWN. CRITICAL SIDE-FINDING: the effect calls refreshRows() on window focus and visibilitychange, so clicking back into the page fetches rows — fast enough to look like realtime. This has almost certainly been masking S60 the entire time, including during the S23 verification on 1 August where rows appeared after page interaction and were read as live updates. Do not assume any past “realtime working” observation on this surface was real. Not a regression from the S84 lift. Timeboxed and parked by the operator as non-blocking.

- S63. NEW 2026-07-29. ol stripped by the sanitiser. emailPreview.ts:45 allows ul and li but not ol, so an ordered list loses its wrapper and renders as bare li elements. This affects the SENT EMAIL, not just the preview — sanitizeEmailHtml is on the draft render path via renderTemplateBodyForDraft. Latent today: nothing generates ol (the missing-info block emits ul, and tests assert that markup). Will bite the moment a tenant edits a template or a generated body uses one. Small fix, but confirm nothing depends on ol being stripped before adding it.

- **WHAT THE ROOT CAUSE ACTUALLY WAS, AND IT WAS NOT COPY.** Route-conditional essential-info fields were seeded ONCE at onboarding, per tenant. Which route applies is a PER-ENQUIRY decision. A static seed cannot express that, so the seeder included every ticked route’s field and the block asked for all of them. **A timing mismatch, not a wording problem.** Compounding it, getEssentialFieldPayload at actions.ts:496 mapped field rows and silently DROPPED route_key, which is defined in movers.ts and never reached the database — so even at render time nothing could tell which field belonged to which route.

- **THE BUG WAS LATENT, NOT LIVE.** Every tenant at the time was single-route or null. The 2026-08-09 framing of “a wrong email reaching a real customer today” was wrong on the facts; it would have bitten the first new signup ticking two boxes. Recorded because the priority call was right for the wrong reason.

- **SHIPPED IN PHASE 1.** route_key persisted on tenant_agent_essential_info_fields and backfilled (item_list → list, photos → photos, survey_availability → survey). New lib/agents/resolveQuoteRoutes.ts, deterministic, no LLM, no external call: reads an ordered rules array, first match wins, falls back to tenant_config.agent_quote_route_policy.routes then to [‘list’]. Rules stored as ONE row on the existing tenant_agent_local_rules table, kind quote_route, key policy, value {rules:[…]} — an ordered array rather than a sort_order column, so the write is atomic and there is no reorder bookkeeping. Route sentence moved from seed-time baking to a runtime {{route_sentence}} substitution. Fields filtered AFTER extraction. Combined evidence asks added for the four multi-route keys.

- **FILTERING HAPPENS AFTER EXTRACTION, ON PURPOSE, AND PHASE 1 DID NOT NEED IT TO.** Phase 2 resolves routes from the extracted pickup postcode; filtering before extraction would make that circular. Doing it in the final order now means phase 2 changes only the resolver and not the plumbing. Extraction over-asks internally by a field or two, which is harmless.

- **THE COMBINE STEP HAPPENS BEFORE THE MISSING-FIELD COUNTING LOGIC**, via synthetic combined field rows. Codex flagged this in the investigation and it was the right catch — combining afterwards would have broken S51’s 0/1/2/3+ rules, since a combined ask must count as one item.

- **BLOCKER FOUND IN INVESTIGATION.** The DB check constraint on tenant_agent_local_rules.kind excluded quote_route, so the insert would have failed. Constraint and the LocalRuleKind union both extended. **The UI was deliberately NOT touched — see S134.**

- **EXISTING TENANTS MIGRATED BY EXACT MATCH ONLY.** Stored body_html carried a literal route sentence. The migration replaces it with the placeholder only where the paragraph matches a known QUOTE_ROUTE_SENTENCES string verbatim, so a tenant who edited their own sentence keeps what they wrote. 4 of 4 live rows converted, confirmed by query.

- **VERIFIED ON THE F12 PANEL, BOTH DIRECTIONS.** Three-route tenant: seven bullets down to three, with one combined evidence line. Survey-only tenant: item_list is seeded on that tenant and correctly did NOT appear. **Not verified by real email** — worth one before the next inbound-touching change, per S123.

- ORIGINAL ENTRY, RETAINED. NEW 2026-07-29, STRUCTURAL. Quote-route asks are additive when they should be exclusive. A tenant ticking all three quote routes gets a route sentence offering email, photos or a survey — a choice — and then a missing-info block that asks for the item list AND photos AND survey availability, plus the four core fields. The customer is told to pick one and then asked for everything. Same class as the route-sentence / missing-info collision caught on 28 July. Conditional fields (photos, survey_availability) are seeded per route with no notion that the routes are alternatives. Needs a design decision on what a multi-route tenant should ask for: the cheapest route’s fields only, the core fields plus a single “or” line, or route selection driven by F10 before the block is assembled. Own session. Consider taking with S59.


- S67. NEW 2026-07-29. Overview save affordance on mobile. The Save button sits below the fold when the Enabled toggle is in view, on any phone size. Mitigated on the S58 PR — Overview now warns on nav-away with unsaved changes, and the Save button pulses when dirty — but the button is still invisible at the moment the user acts. Two shapes proposed: a sticky action bar that docks Save to the bottom of the viewport when dirty (fixes the whole form), or a confirm sheet on the Enabled toggle itself that explains the one-time setup and runs it on confirm (better for enabling specifically, and puts the expectation before the wait rather than after). Compatible; not exclusive. SHIPPED 2026-07-30 as part of F8 Brief 3 (PR #42). Sticky action bar chosen, mobile only, Overview only. The confirm sheet was declined: it would have meant two different save mechanisms on one screen, on the first surface a new tenant configures. Desktop deliberately unchanged — the Save button is already visible there, and changing a working screen for symmetry is not a reason. Rollout to other forms is S80.

- S69. **ADDITION 2026-08-10, FROM LIVE READING.** The access ask (“anything awkward about access at either end, like stairs or tight parking”) uses stairs as its example, and **almost every house has stairs**, so the example does not discriminate. If the field survives S137 at all, the example wants to be something that actually signals cost — more than one flight, no lift above the ground floor, no parking within carrying distance. **Also absorb the four combined evidence asks shipped in S64 phase 1** into the copy reference; they were written in chat and the reference doc does not yet contain them. Original entry follows. NEW 2026-07-30. Full copy review pass. Chat-only, no code. Read every situation’s rendered output at 0, 1, 2 and 3+ missing, across both noun and clause shapes, and correct docs/reference/auto-responder-template-copy-reference.md. Prompted by the observation that the customer-facing block now assembles from more joints than existed when the rules were written — stem, shape, ask, hint, route sentence, missing-info block — and each joint is a place the copy can read slightly wrong without any rule being violated. Known items for the pass: (a) access is shape=noun, so rule C renders “We’ll also need anything awkward about access at either end”, where the clause stem would read better — shape flags are per-field data, so this is a copy call not a code fix; (b) S54’s variant pools, folded in here; (c) whether any situation reads badly at a missing-count that has never been seen on a real email. ABSORBS S54. GATES D5.

- S71. NEW 2026-07-30. Direct greeting picker — Hi / Hello / Dear chosen explicitly by the tenant, not derived from tone. Inferred Dear is a product error; chosen Dear is the tenant’s call, and they can match their signature signoff to it. NOT a tone control: a tone dropdown was designed and cancelled this session, because with Dear gone tone decides one word between two near-identical options, and the signoff it also used to imply is baked into signature_html at onboarding and edited directly by the tenant. SEQUENCE AFTER F7 SESSION 2b. A tenant choosing Dear today would get a formal opening on body copy they cannot safely edit (S50) and cannot see the structure of (S68). The choice only becomes real once the editor works.

- S72. NOT A BUG. Logged and withdrawn 2026-07-30. “Hello Johnson” on a live drafted reply was read as the greeting LLM picking a surname. Johnson was the first name in the test email. Extraction was correct. Number retained as withdrawn rather than reused, so the session summary’s reference to it resolves.

- S74. NEW 2026-07-30. Reg column on /vehicles is empty on every row while the registration sits inside the Name field — “Iveco GN16 WMX”, “Renault WX15 EXW”. Whoever seeded the data typed the reg into name because that is the natural thing to do, and the dedicated column stayed unused. Decide whether Reg deserves its own column at all, or should be derived. Cosmetic today, but it is the column a mover would sort by.

- S75. NEW 2026-07-30. Branding edit permission is gated on role === “owner” || (role === “admin” && permissions?.quote_params). Branding edits behind a quote-params permission reads like a copy-paste from the pricing gate. May be deliberate as a general “can edit tenant config” flag. Confirm intent, then either rename the permission or split it. Untouched during F8 by design.

- S76. NEW 2026-07-30. Manual delete of archived job cards. Cut from F8 Brief 1 mid-session because the semantics were undecided and the control is destructive and irreversible. Automatic cleanup already exists: supabase/functions/jobs-archive-cleanup/index.ts deletes cards past each tenant’s retention window and removes attachment storage BEFORE the row. Any manual path must reuse that ordering or it orphans files in Storage — see S35. “Delete all archived, any age” was the instinct but wipes completed job history including customer details, totals, deposits and attachments, and retention already handles the expired case, so a manual “delete expired now” is just running the cron early. The likely real want is narrower: clear out one old period, not everything. Design first, then build.

- S78. NEW 2026-07-30. Field tooltips misbehave on both platforms. Desktop: an opened tooltip renders underneath adjacent content and cannot be read. Mobile: tooltips open and stay open until explicitly closed, so several stack at once and interacting elsewhere does not dismiss them. Same shared FieldTooltip component, so one fix. Pre-existing, surfaced during F8 Fleet testing.

- S79. NEW 2026-07-30. Numeric inputs do not clear their zero placeholder on input. Typing 750 into a field showing 0 displays 0750 until save; the saved value is correct. Looks like a bug to the user, who would reasonably not save. Found on the Fleet add-vehicle path; check every numeric field.

- S80. NEW 2026-07-30. Unsaved-changes guard missing on Settings > Fleet inline editing, and the sticky-save-bar rollout. Editing a vehicle card and navigating away loses the edit with no warning. Not a regression — the shared guard was wired for the Agents tabs and for Templates, Categories and Local Rules, and Fleet was never in that set. Related question raised the same session: whether the F8 sticky save bar should replace individual save buttons app-wide. Probably yes eventually, but it is a sweep across every form, each with its own dirty-state logic, and it collides with S66 (save-then-revert) and S32 (guard audit), which live in exactly the same forms. Doing them separately means touching every form three times. TAKE S32, S66 AND S80 AS ONE SESSION. SCOPE NOTE 2026-08-01: the Review Queue draft editor was fixed separately as S97 and is no longer a hole, but it is NOT a settings form and would have been missed by a settings-form sweep. When the audit runs, enumerate every surface holding unsaved user input, not every form — S97 found three distinct loss paths on one surface, only one of which was nav-away. **UPDATE 2026-10-01: Fleet guard half DONE (S32 2a). Sticky bar SLICE 1 of 4 DONE (PR #160 `790c2e9`):** `SaveBarProvider` + `useSaveBar` + `StickySaveBar` in AppShell, Overview migrated (three keys), desktop row under main, mobile fixed above the pill, "Saved." flash, guard-only forms linked with a per-form hint. Inline Save buttons STAY (bar mirrors). Drawers/modals keep own Save. **REMAINING:** slice 2 Settings forms with a ref (Business info, Deposit, Retention, Branding, Pricing, General), slice 3 lifted handlers (Profile name, Fleet, Distance bands as two keys each), slice 4 Agents (Voice, Templates, Categories, Rules, Mailbox with Verify primary). Resume block: 2026-10-01 summary section 5; spec: `docs/investigations/s80-sticky-save-bar-investigation-2026-10-01.md`.

- S81. NEW 2026-08-01, STRUCTURAL. The crew nav filter is an allowlist (CREW_PATHS in navItems.ts) and the middleware block is a separate hardcoded denylist of explicit pathname checks.
