There is no shared source, and a single audit during S73 found three separate drifts between them — /agents in neither, /quote in nav but blocked in middleware, /reports blocked at page level only. Needs one source of truth both layers read. DELIBERATELY NOT DONE INSIDE S73: that was direct-to-main and this is a refactor of a permissions layer, which is the wrong thing to do in the same commit as a security fix. Note that page-level gating (canEdit and the /reports 403) should stay as a third layer regardless — it is what limited the S73 exposure to configuration rather than customer data.

- S82. NEW 2026-08-01. Email Response Generator — the unfinished stub on the Local Rules tab. Spec now known and confirmed by the operator: the tenant pastes or pulls in a customer enquiry, types a rough shorthand reply, and the LLM polishes it into sendable copy. Worked example — in: “Yes can do 12th. Loading same day, packing day before. want to book?” out: a properly formed reply opening “Hi Michelle,” confirming availability on the 12th, explaining that loading is same-day and packing the day before, and asking whether they would like to book. A time saver for the human-handled cases. DIFFERENT SHAPE FROM THE AUTO RESPONDER: no classifier, no template selection, no review queue row — a straight LLM call reusing the tenant’s tone preset and signature. Needs a rate limit like every other tenant-triggered LLM call; reuse the config_helper_rate_limit_events pattern. **UPDATED 2026-08-09 BY F16. HOME DECIDED: the Generate tab, not Local Rules.** It is a generation action and sits with the other generation surfaces, between Test A Customer Enquiry and Categories. The stub now renders there, disabled, with the Phase 2 pill — it was briefly deleted in F16 3a in error and restored in 3b. **Unblocked and pulled forward in discussion as a likely next session**, on the grounds that it is small and its stub is now visible to a tenant. Note it was deliberately NOT built inside F16: it needs a rate limit, a server action and an LLM call, and folding a new feature into a restructure branch would have held five verified commits behind one unverified one. **F18 is more important than this item** — see the sequencing note in the header.

- S86a. SHIPPED 2026-08-01, same commit. matched_sensitivity_keyword. Removing the freeform reasoning text exposed a gap: there was no stored field recording WHY a row escalated, and the freeform LLM text was checked first in the derivation chain so it almost always won. Killing it would have made every keyword match read as a generic “Flagged for your attention” — the tenant’s own rule firing invisibly, on exactly the enquiries that matter most. Investigation confirmed there was NO code-side keyword matching at all: S47 works by instructing the LLM, and classifyEmail only parses the JSON response. Fix: the pipeline now performs its own case-insensitive substring check against the tenant’s keyword list, across BOTH subject and body_text, first match wins, and writes it to agent_runs.classification.matched_sensitivity_keyword. RECORD ONLY — it does not trigger, suppress or alter any escalation, the LLM remains the only thing deciding, and the S47 prompt injection is untouched. Escalated now reads “Matched your sensitivity keyword ‘porkchops’”. Existing rows have no key and fall back to the generic lines; no backfill, all tenants disposable. Plain-language set: matched keyword; “Flagged for your attention” (requires_escalation); “The agent wasn’t confident enough to reply” (low_confidence); “The agent couldn’t categorise this enquiry” (invalid_category); “No template set up for this type of enquiry” (no_template).

- S91. LOGGED AND SUPERSEDED 2026-08-01, NOT REUSED. Was “remove the category pill from Review Queue rows”. Absorbed into S95 as a toggle, then the pill was removed outright by S96. Number retained as superseded so this session’s summary resolves.

- S98. NEW 2026-08-01. Mobile overflow when three or more pills are visible on a Review Queue row. With Confidence toggled on, the pill cluster pushes the sender and subject text out of view. Small and contained to ReviewQueueTab. Surfaced immediately after S95 shipped, by the operator on a real phone.

- S99. NEW 2026-08-01. The Review Queue server select and the three realtime selects are not identical: the realtime ones join agent_runs.error and the server select does not. Pre-existing, not introduced by this session’s work. Means a row loaded on page load will not display an error, but the same row after a realtime or focus refresh will. Cosmetic today; the class of bug matters more than this instance — there are now four selects that must be kept in step by hand, and both S23 and S96 had to touch all four. Consider extracting one shared select definition.

- S100. NEW 2026-08-04. The company switcher dropdown bypasses the unsaved-changes guard. Found during S97 verification: every other navigation path warns on an unsaved draft edit, but switching company does not. The guard intercepts anchor clicks and in-app link navigation; the switcher almost certainly performs a router.push or a server action plus a hard reload, which sidesteps that interception. Same fix shape as the tab-switch case already handled in S97. Small. Belongs with the S32 / S66 / S80 forms session, which should now be scoped as “every surface holding unsaved user input”, not “every form”. **DONE 2026-10-01 (PR #157 `d3dd619`).** Switcher wraps its POST + reload in `requestLeave`, so the confirm comes BEFORE the cookie moves (was: browser dialog after the switch, Stay left a split-brain page). Verified with a real two-company login (richjstowell@gmail.com owner of Testing Mover + S199 Movers). Same wrap on company add/delete/restore, sign out, Stripe redirects.

- S101. NEW 2026-08-04, CAUSE UNKNOWN, WATCH THIS. Interacting with the company switcher on Testing Mover produced a redirect to onboarding, which then STUCK — the operator could not escape it until switching to another company and back. Testing Mover already has templates, categories and a completed agent, so there was no legitimate reason to onboard. Two candidate causes and they are very different in severity: (a) tenant context is cleared or confused on switcher interaction, the onboarding gate reads no tenant and bounces — a live bug any real tenant can hit, and the bad one; (b) Testing Mover genuinely lacks an onboarding-complete flag because it predates F7 onboarding — benign, affects only fossil tenants, but would mean the gate has no “has templates and categories therefore done” fallback. The stickiness points at (a): a missing flag would bounce on every visit including after switching back, and it did not. NEEDS AN INVESTIGATE GATE BEFORE ANY FIX — what column the gate reads, what the layout and middleware do on switcher interaction, and whether Testing Mover’s flag is actually set. Related to S100 only in that both were surfaced by the same control.

- S102. NEW 2026-08-04. Template save takes several seconds. No LLM call is on that path. The likely cause, from reading rather than measurement, is revalidatePath(“/agents”) in createTemplate and updateTemplate forcing the Agents page to refetch categories, templates, local rules, review queue, escalated and sometimes feedback — six datasets for a one-row write. Cosmetic, but it reads as the app struggling on the surface a tenant edits most.

- S103. **CLOSED 2026-08-07 AS A WRONG-KEY-NAME DIAGNOSIS. NO WORK NEEDED.** Filed 2026-08-04 claiming escalated runs record no category, on the basis that classification->>‘category’ was NULL on three escalated rows. The stored key is category_key, not category. No code anywhere writes classification.category, escalated rows do get category_key written before status flips to escalated (computeDraft.ts ~line 245, pipeline.ts ~line 72), and the UI itself reads category_key via escalationReason.ts. The original query was reading a key that has never existed. Confirmed by Codex investigation during the S110 session and by a live row. **Lesson worth more than the item: a NULL from a jsonb key lookup is indistinguishable from a missing write, and Postgres will not warn you. Check the key name before diagnosing behaviour.**

- S104. **CLOSED 2026-08-09, ABSORBED BY F16.** NEW 2026-08-04. Test-card density on mobile. The F12(a) results panel carries too much small text for a phone. The Missing Essential Info block is the worst offender on space and is arguably the least useful of the five fields, given the same information is visible in the rendered draft immediately below it. Do NOT fix in isolation — DEC17 decides what a tenant should see about categories and templates at all, and this panel displays both. Sequence after DEC17.

- S105. NEW 2026-08-04. Test-run latency. One F12(a) run was timed at 37 seconds. The three LLM calls run in sequence — classify, then extract, then greeting — and greeting extraction does not depend on the classification result, so it can fire concurrently for roughly a third off. Worth reading the ordering properly before assuming that is the only win. Latency does not matter on the inbound path where nobody is watching; it matters a great deal on a surface where a tenant is sitting waiting for it. NOTE 2026-08-04: S59’s likely mitigations include running the classify call more than once and taking a majority, which multiplies this. Read the two together. **UPDATE 2026-08-05: that multiplier is gone.** S59 resolved with no majority vote, so nothing now multiplies the classify call. Measured classify-only latency on 2026-08-05 was 7.8s median, 18.1s worst, which sizes the classify share of the 37s.

- WHY THAT WAY ROUND. ai_classifier_feedback already had a shipped capture UI — the “Wrong response?” widget on Review Queue and Escalated — and it carries two signals A2’s table never had: suggested-new-category, and the edit diff via original_draft_body_html, both of which F1 phase 2 needs. The only thing A2’s table did better was store a copy of the customer email rather than a pointer to it, which matters because a retention purge of agent_runs would otherwise silently destroy a worked example. That was fixed with two nullable columns, email_subject and email_body, written at capture time.

- THE SPLIT WRITE-SIDE / READ-SIDE OPTION WAS REJECTED, and the reason generalises: it needs a copy step from one table to the other, and that step can fail on its own. The tenant marks a reply wrong, nothing lands, they mark it again, still nothing. Silent partial failure on the one feature whose entire value proposition is that it visibly learns.

- **THE VIEW IS THE INTERESTING PART.** ai_classifier_feedback accepts partial rows — a tenant can say “you need a new category for this” without naming a correct one. Useful to F1 phase 2, useless as a worked example, and actively harmful if injected as one. Rather than a filter in the read path that a future reader has to remember, the qualifying rows are exposed as a view, classifier_worked_examples, created WITH (security_invoker = on). It requires predicted_category_key, correct_category_id and a non-empty email_body, and joins tenant_agent_categories to return correct_category_key flat. loadClassifierExamples.ts reads the view and structurally cannot see anything else. This was the operator’s instinct — that mixing two purposes in one table would get messy — taken seriously rather than argued down, and the view is the answer that keeps the separation without a copy step.

- loadClassifierExamples.ts kept every constant unchanged on purpose: EXAMPLES_PER_PAIR 2, EXAMPLES_GLOBAL_CAP 30, EXAMPLE_BODY_MAX_LENGTH 1200, truncateAtWordBoundary, the cap warning, the final slice, and the exported ClassifierWorkedExample interface. buildClassifierPrompt.ts needed no change at all. resolveCategoryKey and its union type are gone — the view returns the key flat, so the Supabase join-typing workaround died with it.

- The snapshot uses preferredInboundBodyText() — body_text_stripped if non-empty, else body_text — exported from computeDraft.ts rather than duplicated, so the stored example is byte-for-byte what the classifier was actually shown. Codex proposed this against the brief and was right: a duplicated copy would drift the day someone changed the preference in one place.

- A missing agent_runs row or empty body never fails the tenant’s click. The feedback row is written with nulls and a warning is logged.

- ai_classifier_feedback had ZERO ROWS before this session. The widget shipped 2026-07-21 and had never been used in production. Its first real exercise was the 2026-08-05 verification, and it worked.

- Two feature_key values now exist in that table: auto_responder_classifier, written by the real widget, and classifier, written by a hand-inserted test row. Harmless, but do not assume one value when querying.

- S107’s note about classifier_prompt_version recording CLASSIFIER_SPINE_VERSION rather than a per-tenant row version is unaffected and still outstanding. The column is dead weight now and was deliberately left alone.

- S107. **DONE 2026-09-18 (F31 PR 1b, PR #127 `707bfd2`).** The operator console drill-in at `/admin/tenants/[tenantId]` shows the exact assembled classifier prompt per agent, built by the same shared loader + assemble function the live pipeline uses, so it cannot drift. Operator-only (DEC1 / DEC12 hold). Original entry archived in movers_app_backlog_archive_APPEND_2026-09-18.md. Still outstanding from the original note: `classifier_prompt_version` records the spine version, and since F31 PR 2a the payload also records `classifier_spine_override_version_id`.

- S108. **NEW 2026-08-04. UPDATED 2026-08-05 — THE COLUMNS MOVED, THE POINT STANDS.** A future retention policy must clear correction text, not just agent_runs. Since S106 the columns are ai_classifier_feedback.email_subject and ai_classifier_feedback.email_body, not the dropped corrections table. They copy the customer email at capture time deliberately, so that purging agent_runs does not silently destroy a tenant’s accumulated training. The table comment records why the copy exists but not what a purge must do. Whoever writes the retention policy must clear correction bodies too, or the copy defeats the purpose it was created for. No policy exists today; this is a note for when one does.

- S109. **NEW 2026-08-04. Line endings are noise in every diff.** Codex writes LF, the repo is CRLF, so every touched file reports far more changed lines than actually changed — A2’s computeDraft.ts showed -21 for what was a four-line edit. Costs a few minutes of doubt on every review. A .gitattributes with a text=auto rule would end it.

- **WHAT IT WAS FILED AS, AND WHY THAT WAS WRONG.** Filed 2026-08-05: multi-match escalation is a hardcoded constant in the shared spine, and the tenant should decide whether a two-service enquiry escalates or gets auto-quoted. Three options were sketched — escalate on any multi-match, primary-ask wins, fixed precedence. **None were built, and the reason is that the tenant already has the control.** STEP 0 outranks the multi-match pre-pass (verified as check 7 on 2026-08-05), so a single correction on a move-plus-piano enquiry permanently overrides multi-match for that kind of enquiry. That control is concrete — a real email, a highlighted phrase, a chosen category — which is the form a non-technical mover can actually reason about. A toggle would have been a second, weaker control over the same behaviour.

- **THE REAL DEFECT WAS THAT THE TENANT COULD NOT SEE WHICH CASE THEY WERE IN.** At least five distinct causes escalate an email — sensitivity keyword, sensitivity signal from STEP 2, multi-match, no-match, and the STEP 2 human-judgement catch-all — and only the first was badged. Everything else read “Flagged for your attention”. A tenant cannot correct what they cannot diagnose, so the case where a correction helps most was the one that looked identical to a complaint.

- **WHAT SHIPPED.** classifier-spine.ts gained two keys to the returned JSON: escalation_reason (null | “sensitivity” | “multi_match” | “no_match”) and matched_category_keys (null, or the colliding keys). CLASSIFIER_SPINE_VERSION bumped 2 -> 3. classify.ts parses them, computeDraft.ts persists them into classificationPayload as returned with no derivation or defaulting. escalationReason.ts became the single source of badge text for both call sites. No migration — classification is jsonb.

- **PRECEDENCE, AND IT WAS THE REGRESSION RISK.** matched_sensitivity_keyword is a structured field written by code and is more reliable than a model-reported enum. If it is non-null the existing keyword badge renders and escalation_reason is ignored entirely. Verified: a bereavement email still rendered “Matched your sensitivity keyword ‘bereavement’” after the merge.

- **THE LABEL LOOKUP WAS THE S114 TRAP AND IT WAS PREEMPTED.** The badge must name categories by tenant-facing label, not key, and labels are not in the classification payload. getEscalationDisplayReason gained a key->label parameter, page.tsx builds it from tenant_agent_categories, and AgentsTabs.tsx carries it through to the client-side realtime path — otherwise the badge would have worked on first page load and degraded on refresh. Three files beyond the four briefed, all justified. **The brief named this risk explicitly because three consecutive S114 briefs built controls over data the component could not see. Naming it in the brief is what stopped it recurring.**

- **VERIFIED WITH REAL EMAILS ON JOE’S MOVERS, 2026-08-07.** (1) House move plus upright piano -> escalated, escalation_reason multi_match, matched_category_keys [“quote_request”,“specialist”], badge read “Matched both Quote request and Piano / specialist items”. (2) Bereavement email -> existing keyword badge unchanged, precedence held. (3) Car transport to Alicante -> escalated, but as multi_match rather than no_match; see S122. (4) Normal quote request -> Review Queue, quote_request, 0.9, escalation_reason null, drafted as before. Test 5 (pre-S110 rows still render the generic badge) was **skipped by decision** — Joe’s had no pre-merge rows and there are no live tenants, so the fallback path is unexercised. Testing Mover still holds rows that would exercise it.

- **classifier_prompt_version now reads 3 and is correct.** An earlier suspicion of an off-by-one in the version label was withdrawn — the 1-vs-2 values seen on older rows are genuine version drift, not a labelling bug.

- **A TEST FIXTURE WAS CREATED AND LEFT IN PLACE.** Joe’s Movers seeded only quote_request, storage and packing, so it had no category pair capable of colliding. A specialist category (“Piano / specialist items”) was added by hand with the post-#46 seed wording to create the overlap. **It has no template behind it.** Delete it or leave it, but know it is there and that it is not how Joe’s was seeded.

- S111. **RE-CONFIRMED WITH CLEANER EVIDENCE 2026-08-10, AND THE CONFOUND IS NOW RULED OUT.** Identical enquiry text sent to two tenants on the same build. Joe’s Movers REJECTED both “Causewayhead in Penzance” as dropoff_address and “roughly around 3rd September” as move_date; s131 Movers ACCEPTED both. Two of two loose fields flipped, which makes chance unlikely. **The obvious confound was checked and eliminated by SQL: label and prompt_hint on both fields are byte-identical across the two tenants.** So this is not copy drift. An investigation is briefed to establish whether the extraction prompt carries anything tenant-specific (business description, tone, category set) — if it does the variance has a cause in the input and is fixable; if the prompt text is identical it is model non-determinism and wants a different answer. **Do not fold this into a UI session.** Original entry follows. NEW 2026-08-05. Missing Essential Info varies run to run. Observed during the storage 8-run on Joe’s Movers: the classification held at storage 8/8 while the Missing Essential Info output changed roughly every other run. That is the EXTRACTION call, not the classifier — a different LLM call with a different prompt, untouched by anything shipped on 2026-08-04 or 2026-08-05. Same class of problem one layer down, and worse in consequence: a wobbly category picks a different template, but wobbly extraction changes the questions asked of the customer in the reply that actually goes out. Two identical enquiries, two different sets of follow-up questions. Likely the same root cause as S59 — essential-info fields defined loosely enough that “is this missing?” has more than one defensible answer. Related to S64 and S104 but is neither. Wants its own investigate gate; do not fold it into a UI session.

- ROOT CAUSE, and it took an investigate gate to find: TWO INDEPENDENT WRITERS. S34’s trigger tenant_agents_create_system_categories fires AFTER INSERT ON tenant_agents and writes ignore=0, escalate=1. provision_default_email_responder then inserts the seed payload with sort_order = array index starting at 0, and its on conflict (agent_id, key) do nothing skips the two rows that already exist. So the trigger’s values stick and two pairs collide.

- DECISION TAKEN: system categories stay FIRST in the decision tree. ignore=0, escalate=1, content categories from 2 upward. The tree stops at first match, so the cheap do-not-reply checks should get first refusal on every email. It is also the pre-existing behaviour on most tenants, so the choice changes nothing about how anything classifies — it only makes the values distinct.

- FIX PART 1: getDefaultLibraryPayload assigns index + 2, reserving 0 and 1 for the trigger. The trigger was NOT changed; its values were already correct and it runs first.

- **FIX PART 2 FAILED ON THE FIRST ATTEMPT, AND THE PATTERN IS WORTH KEEPING.** Migration 20260805000002 ran row_number() over ALL rows including system categories, then used a CASE to avoid overwriting the system ones. So the system rows consumed sequence positions AND kept their old values, and those positions were handed out a second time to content categories. Result: duplicates remained on two agents, and ignore/escalate were not forced to 0/1 at all. **Excluding rows from an UPDATE is not the same as excluding them from the window that generates the numbers.** 20260805000003 filters to is_system = false inside the window CTE and assigns 0 and 1 explicitly by key. Verified against the database, run twice, second run changed nothing.

- 20260805000002 stays in history. It ran against the database and must not be edited.

- SIDE EFFECT, agreed before running: on two older agents (12899d1f, 14206318) the system categories were sitting at the END of the tree, not colliding. The corrective migration moved them to the front. That is a real change in decision-tree order on those two tenants, not a cosmetic renumber. One of them is Testing Mover, which is deferred for reseed anyway.

- Checked before running: zero rows where is_system = true and key not in (‘ignore’,‘escalate’). If a third kind of system category is ever added, this migration’s logic does not cover it.

- moveCategory swaps sort_order by list position and was previously operating on undefined ordering. Re-tested by the operator after the fix and it behaves.

- S113. **CLOSED 2026-08-07. ONE ITEM SHIPPED, TWO DEFERRED AS D-ITEMS WITH TRIGGERS. NOTHING NOW SITS IN FRONT OF F16.** Raised 2026-08-05: PR #46 seeded seven category descriptions written by Claude, including exclusion clauses like “Not full household moves” on man_and_van, which is a policy about what a tenant’s man-and-van service covers, written by us, into every future signup. Scope was three items — (a) the #46 descriptions, (b) STEP 2’s sensitivity list, (c) the multi-match outcome. Outcome below, per item.

- **(c) SHIPPED as S110.** See that entry.

- **(b) DEFERRED as D13. No change to the sensitivity list.** Reasoning, and it is the operator’s: escalating costs the tenant a message they would answer themselves anyway, and for most of these triggers there is no auto-reply path by default. The failure mode is doing nothing where something was possible, not doing something wrong, which is the cheap direction to be wrong in and is the entire design of the safety net. The audit still separated the list: refunds, complaints and legal disputes are clear mechanism; explicit callback requests and same-day-with-no-detail are policy in the sense that a one-van operator might want an auto-reply and a larger firm might not; partnerships sits oddly between ignore and escalate. **The catch-all**** ****“****anything needing human judgement****”**** ****is the item worth watching, because it is doing more than the named triggers.** Every follow-up email escalates through it — the model self-justifies with is_initial_enquiry=false even though no rule instructs that — and it is likely the largest single source of escalations.

- **(a) DEFERRED as D14. The seed descriptions keep our wording for now.** The argument shrank three times during the session and each shrink was the operator’s. First: the “Not X” clauses are not tidy-up text, they are what suppresses multi-match escalation, so removing them raises the escalation rate rather than cleaning anything. Second: a man-and-van operator receiving the quote_request reply instead of the man_and_van one is barely inconvenienced — both templates ask the same core questions and differ mainly in crew wording, so the cost of a wrong category is much lower than assumed. Third: **the reply’s substance follows the tenant’s own quote route policy, chosen at onboarding, not the seed description** — so a slightly wrong category still produces a reply the mover would recognise as theirs. What survives is the original principle, that we are writing sentences about what a mover’s service covers, with the consequences now known to be minor in every case examined. Revisit on the trigger below, not on a date.

- **THE TRIGGERS, so that**** ****“****monitor it****”**** ****is a thing rather than an intention.** For (a): a tenant whose enquiries land in a category that does not fit their business — visible now, because the badge names the pair that collided — or a mover changing their categories and the answers flipping. For (b): escalation volume dominated by escalation_reason “sensitivity”, which is one query once there is real traffic. **Both became measurable on 2026-08-07 because of S110, and neither was measurable before it.**

- **WHAT DID NOT SURVIVE THE AUDIT, recorded so it is not re-raised.** list_completeness was challenged on 2026-08-05 as the strongest example of policy and was defended successfully by the operator — “3-bed house, standard contents, nothing unusual” is subjective and carries no information, three sofas and five wardrobes being one person’s normal, and treating it as none is correct. The rule stays as written. Category description visibility was found already shipped: CategoriesTab has had an inline editable description field per row since before S113 was raised.

- **THE DESIGN, and the reasoning matters more than the shape.** A correction is {evidence text, correct category}. The tenant either highlights a phrase in the customer’s email or types a sentence. Both land in the same column and are treated identically — the distinction between “highlight” and “note” was explored at length and abandoned, because from the system’s side both are a short piece of text plus a category and the split bought nothing. **The key insight, which decided everything else: evidence only helps if it NARROWS WHAT THE MODEL SEES.** Adding a reason alongside the full email body changes nothing — the body is still there and the false indicators are still in it. So the evidence REPLACES the body in the prompt. email_body remains on the table as the record of where the correction came from, and is surfaced through a View message modal, but it never enters the prompt.

- **EVIDENCE IS MANDATORY.** Saving with neither a highlight nor typed text is refused with a message explaining what is needed. Deliberately not a disabled button — a disabled button teaches the tenant nothing about why.

- **PENDING VERSUS ACTIVE, and this is the safety property.** A correction saves instantly with status ‘pending’ and does not enter the prompt. A contradiction check runs, and only a clean result flips it to ‘active’. The view classifier_worked_examples requires status = ‘active’, so **a contradictory or unchecked correction cannot reach the prompt, enforced in SQL rather than in application code.** LLM failure, invalid model output, or an unresolved conflict all leave the row pending, which is safe.

- **THE CHECK NEVER BLOCKS A SAVE.** Save writes pending and returns; the client then calls a separate check action. Not fire-and-forget from inside the save — deploy target is still undecided and background work after a response can be killed on serverless. Auto-retry re-checks pending rows on page load. No cron, no worker, no retry button.

- **WHAT THE CHECK COMPARES.** The new correction against all active corrections for the agent AND all category descriptions. gpt-4o-mini at temperature 0, which required adding an optional temperature parameter to callLLM. Deliberately not gpt-5-mini: it is a reasoning model that may ignore or reject temperature, and a check that answers differently on the same pair is worse than no check. temperature 0 is near-deterministic, not deterministic, and nothing should claim otherwise.

- **THE MODEL IS NEVER TRUSTED WITH AN IDENTIFIER.** Active corrections and categories are presented to the model as NUMBERED LISTS and it returns an index, which code maps back to a real row. This replaced an earlier design that asked for a UUID — the model echoed the prompt’s own placeholder, “UUID-1”, straight back, which produced a Postgres 22P02 on write. Indices removed the failure class rather than patching the instance. Indices are still validated against the database before use; an out-of-range or non-integer index leaves the row pending.

- **CONFLICT RESOLUTION IS ASYMMETRIC AND THE UI REFLECTS THAT.** Correction-vs-correction is symmetric — both sides shown with evidence, date, category label and a View message link, two buttons, Use the new one / Keep the existing one. Correction-vs-category-description is NOT symmetric: there is no competing row, so it gets ONE button (Discard this correction), the description text shown in full, and a link to Categories. Codex twice rendered this as a two- or three-button choice in which every button did the same thing; that was rejected each time. **A tenant who thinks their description is wrong must go to Categories to change it. That friction is correct — a description governs every future enquiry and should not be edited from a screen about one email.**

- **CONFLICT VISIBILITY.** A conflicted correction shows an orange pill on the queue row that opens the full conflict panel. An always-on inline panel was built, found to be a second incomplete implementation of a panel that already existed, and deleted. One panel, one implementation.

- **NO CATEGORY KEYS TENANT-FACING.** Labels only, everywhere.

- **VERIFIED END TO END ON CORNWALL, PRODUCTION, WITH REAL EMAILS.** All nine steps. The decisive one: email 1 corrected with the highlighted phrase “only the contents of my garage, nothing from the house itself”; email 2 sent with different sender, subject, dates, towns and postcodes, sharing only that phrase; it followed the correction. Also verified: the view returns evidence_text and no email body; conflict detected and recorded with the right target row; resolution deletes the loser and activates the winner; category-description conflict caught and typed correctly; one correction row per run; Needs-new-category mode unchanged and still saves without evidence.

- **THE FIVE BUGS, KEPT BECAUSE FOUR OF THEM ARE ONE PATTERN.** (1) A Supabase !inner join returned an object where a UUID was expected, so the check silently failed and returned 200 having written nothing. (2) The SAME join, in a different query, made every existing correction render as → unknown in the check’s prompt — so the model was asked whether “elephant toast → packing” contradicts “Elephant toast → unknown” and correctly could not say. It was fixed in one query and left broken in the other, and the second one cost a full extra diagnostic round. The fix was to drop the join entirely and build a categoryMap in code. (3) The page-level select omitted conflict_type and conflicts_with_id, so **three consecutive UI briefs added badges and panels to a component that structurally could not see whether a conflict existed.** (4) The placeholder UUID echo, above. (5) The check swallowed its own errors and returned success, which is what hid (1) for two rounds.

- **THE PROCESS LESSON, and it is the expensive one.** When a UI change does not take, check that the DATA reaches the component before briefing the UI again. Three briefs were spent on presentation before anyone checked the query.

- **CLOSED. Does not block F16.**

- S114a. **NEW 2026-08-07. RACE: two corrections saved seconds apart can both go active without seeing each other.** Each check reads the active list, and if the first has not yet flipped to active when the second reads, neither sees the other and both activate. Auto-retry only re-checks rows that are status = ‘pending’ and conflicts_with_id is null, so already-active rows are never re-checked and the pair stays live forever. Observed in real use: two corrections fourteen seconds apart both went active with identical evidence and different categories. Window is small and a tenant correcting two things within seconds is unusual, so this is NOT urgent — but the contradiction check’s guarantee is “a contradiction never survives in the prompt”, and this is the one hole in it. Options not yet weighed: re-check active rows periodically, check at prompt-assembly time instead, or serialise checks per agent.

- S114b. **SHIPPED 2026-08-09 IN F16 COMMIT 3a. CLOSED.** Each correction row is now expandable and shows the original enquiry (subject and body, body collapsed with show-more), the wrong-to-right pair by tenant-facing label, the note, status and conflict, and the date. The enquiry body needed a join to agent_runs that the corrections query did not previously make; it follows the S23 precedent rather than a new fetch shape. Corrections are deliberately NOT editable — a correction is a record of something that happened, not a setting. Original entry follows. NEW 2026-08-07. The Classifier Corrections list is read-only apart from delete. Each row shows category label, evidence, date and state, and the only action is Delete. A tenant cannot open a correction to see the email it came from, see which enquiry it has since affected, or edit it. Raised twice during the S114 session and deliberately deferred both times — F16 restructures this page and should absorb it rather than have it built twice.

- S114c. **NEW 2026-08-07. Diagnostic logging for the contradiction check is still in place and was left in deliberately.** feedback-actions.ts logs the active-corrections list, the assembled prompt, the raw model response and each validation step. It earned its keep — the → unknown bug was invisible without it. Remove or reduce it once the feature has run for a while without incident, not before.

- S115. **NEW 2026-08-05. The agent Save button still shows a**** ****“****Setting up****”**** ****message for a step that no longer runs.** Surfaced by the operator immediately after F17a removed the generator call from the enable path. Cosmetic, small, and it now describes something that does not happen — the app telling a tenant it is doing work it is not doing. There is still a save round-trip delay, which is probably just the round-trip and not the removed generator; do not assume the message and the delay are the same problem.

- S116. **NEW 2026-08-05, STANDING CONDITION RATHER THAN A BUG.** No Supabase client anywhere in the repo is typed against the generated Database type. SupabaseClient and createClient appear nowhere; database.types.ts is imported in six files and only ever for the standalone Json type. So .from(), .select(), .insert() and .upsert() all resolve to loosely-typed shapes, and a misspelled column or table name typechecks cleanly and fails at runtime. This is why S106 needed no type regeneration and why database.types.ts has been stale since at least 2026-08-04 with no visible consequence. Regenerating is an ad hoc npx supabase gen types run — there is no CLI in devDependencies and no package.json script — and it would pull in schema-wide drift from every migration since it was last generated, so it does not belong inside a small commit. Worth doing deliberately, on its own, with the drift reviewed.

- S117. **NEW 2026-08-07. React hydration error #418 on the agents page. Pre-existing, not S114.** ReviewQueueTab.tsx line 402 uses Date.now() in a useState initializer, so the server renders one value and the client another. Introduced by the scheduled-send work, surfaced while debugging S114 because it appears in the console on every load of that page. No visible symptom beyond the console error. Small, isolated, fix when convenient.

- S118. **NEW 2026-08-07, PROCESS NOTE RATHER THAN A BACKLOG ITEM, but it cost real time today.** Codex/Claude Code committed without pushing three times in one session, each time reporting the work as done. It also reported items complete that were not built at all — the View message modal was claimed twice before it existed — and reported “verification passed” on the strength of a successful tsc run and a page that loaded. **Rules that came out of it, worth applying to every brief from now on: (1) briefs must say**** ****“****commit AND push, confirm the hash on origin/main****”****; (2) npm run build is the check, not tsc — Vercel fails on eslint rules that tsc never runs; (3) treat every**** ****“****complete****”**** ****claim as unverified until seen in the running app.**

- S119. **PARTIALLY CLOSED 2026-08-11, AND THE REMAINDER IS SHARPER THAN THE ORIGINAL ENTRY.** .mcp.json — local MCP server config, created this session when Supabase was connected as a connector, and it may carry connection details — is now in .gitignore (f016995, direct to main). **scripts/tmp/ turned out to be TRACKED, not merely unignored**, which the original entry got wrong. Ignoring it therefore needs git rm –cached first, which means someone has to look at what is in there before removing it from the index. Small, own job, do not fold it into a merge. Original entry follows. NEW 2026-08-07. scripts/tmp/ is still untracked and still not gitignored, despite a note to the contrary on 2026-08-05. Claude Code created and deleted another temporary script there today (check_feedback.ts). Harmless so far, one git add . away from not being.

- S120. **NEW 2026-08-07. The F12(a) test panel prints a raw enum at the tenant.** On an escalating enquiry the panel reads “No draft reply would be produced for this enquiry. Escalation reason: classifier_requested.” That string is the panel’s own pre-existing wording and predates S110 — it is not the new escalation_reason field — but it is machine vocabulary shown to a non-technical mover, and it says nothing useful. escalationReason.ts is now the single source of tenant-facing escalation text and the panel should call it. **Deliberately NOT folded into F16:** S104 went into F16 because it is layout and F16 rebuilds the tabs, but this is a string in one file with no tab dependency, valid before or after F16. Small.

- S121. **NEW 2026-08-07. Does the quote route policy reach the reply on every category, or only quote_request?** Raised by the operator while closing S113 (a), and it is the load-bearing question underneath that whole item. A mover chooses at onboarding whether they survey, ask for a list, or ask for photos, and when. Per the copy rules only quote_request takes a route sentence, so a clearance, man_and_van or specialist enquiry gets none — meaning a mover who said “I always survey first” may not see that reflected on six of their seven enquiry types. **If the route policy links through properly, a slightly wrong category still produces a reply the mover recognises as theirs, and D14 stays deferred comfortably. If it does not, the wrong category matters more than the audit concluded.** Read-only investigation first; do not brief a fix.

- S122. **RESOLVED 2026-08-12 — no_match IS reachable, and the earlier escalation was the category-naming bug, not a structural hole.** Tested directly during S151 verification: “Do you clean carpets?” sent to Joe’s Movers returned escalate with escalation_reason **no_match** at 0.8, reasoning naming all four configured categories and stating none fit. So the SAFETY RULES do fire. **The original observation was real but misdiagnosed** — the Spain/car-transport enquiry escalated as multi_match because quote_request matched everything shaped like a quote request, which is exactly what S151 fixed. **The one caveat that survives:** no_match is only reachable when fewer than two conditions match, so an enquiry that genuinely straddles two configured services still escalates as multi_match first. That is now correct behaviour rather than a masking bug. Original entry follows. NEW 2026-08-07. no_match may be unreachable, and the badge is what exposed it.** The SAFETY RULES that escalate an unfamiliar request sit AFTER the multi-match pre-pass in the spine, so an enquiry that loosely matches two categories escalates as multi_match before no_match is ever evaluated. Observed on the day S110 shipped: “do you do man and van jobs to Spain? Need a car transported to Alicante” escalated on Joe’s Movers with the badge “Matched both Quote request and Piano / specialist items”. Right outcome, wrong reason, and specialist is a strange match for transporting a car. **Two readings and they point the same way: either the model reaches for the nearest categories rather than declaring no match, or no_match is structurally unreachable.** Worth one check with a genuinely unmatchable enquiry (“do you sell insurance?”) before assuming either. Not urgent — the safety net still caught it — but it means an escalation badge can name a plausible-looking pair when the real answer is “we do not do this at all”, which is worse than useless to a tenant deciding whether to correct.**

- S123. **NEW 2026-08-07, PROCESS NOTE. The F12(a) panel cannot verify anything whose output is a stored row or a UI state.** It says so on its own card — “never writes a run or queues an email” — and two rounds of testing were spent reading its output as evidence for a badge that only exists on the Escalated tab. It exercises the live classifier, extraction and draft rendering and stops there. **Rule: if the thing being verified is a row, a badge, a queue position or anything a tenant sees on Review Queue or Escalated, the test is a real email. The panel is for classifier and copy behaviour only.** Compounding this, inbound email only ever hits production, so a preview deployment cannot be exercised at all — see the SEQUENCING LESSON at the top of this document.

- S124. **NEW 2026-08-09, AND PROBABLY ALREADY ANSWERED — VERIFY BEFORE BUILDING.** Raised by the operator as “a tenant should be able to add a category while adding a template”. F16 commit 2 built exactly that: a + New category control inside the template drawer, key derived from the label, new category selected on creation, draft preserved. **Check the shipped drawer against what was wanted and close as delivered if it matches.** The only plausible gap is that the drawer creates a category with a label and description but nothing else — no priority, no active toggle — which is deliberate, since those are the advanced fields the Categories card exists for.

- S125. **NEW 2026-08-09. Email Response Generator as a dashboard widget.** Small standalone tool, good fit for the busiest screen, and the operator’s instinct is right that it is the kind of thing a dashboard should carry. **BLOCKED ON S82** — the stub has no behaviour, so shipping the card now puts a dead control on the page a tenant looks at most. Sequence strictly after S82 builds the real thing. Note the ERG’s home on the Agents page is settled (Generate tab), so this is an addition, not a move.

- S127. **DONE 2026-09-22 via S339 + S340.** App reproduces the v5 workbook 52/52 scenarios (tolerance 0.005) through `buildEngineConfig` from DB-shaped rows; 32 settings all drive outputs; catalogue 165/165 vs the Cubic tab; wiring trace clean. The pre-v5 "Ultimate" Google Sheet differs from v5 only in figures that are tenant settings (fuel 0.55 vs 0.60, efficiency ladder) and in the hand-typed vans figure, which became F56. **Cornwall non-email cutover is no longer gated by this.** Original entry follows. **NEW 2026-08-09 EVENING. Does the app’s quote engine reproduce cornwall-quote-engine.php and the v5 workbook? NOT VERIFIED, AND IT GATES THE NON-EMAIL HALF OF THE CORNWALL CUTOVER.** The reference material is all in the project — cornwall-quote-engine.php, quote-engine.js, cubic_catalogue_v5.json, cubic_aliases_v1.json, engine_config_v5.json, Cornwall_Movers_Quote_Engine_v5.xlsx — and nobody has checked the app’s /quote output against it. **If it does not reproduce live pricing, the cutover is a port job, not a switch**, and that is a materially different size of work. Check before moving anything over. Cheap to answer: one known job priced both ways.

- S130 AMENDMENT, 2026-08-13. **THE PREMISE OF THIS ITEM IS WRONG AND IT IS BIGGER THAN IT READS.** There is no Supabase CLI on the operator’s machine — not on PATH (where.exe supabase finds nothing) and not in package.json, which carries only @supabase/ssr and @supabase/supabase-js. So supabase db push is not a capability lost to orphaned migrations; it is one that has never existed locally, and the SQL editor was never a workaround but the only route there has ever been. **Three more orphans were added 2026-08-13** (20260812161117_survey_drive_miles, 20260813130000_agent_runs_distance, 20260813170000_survey_exclusive), taking the pile to fifteen. supabase migration repair cannot be run without a CLI installed and a project linked. **Re-scope before starting: this is install-and-link-a-CLI first, then repair fifteen rows, not repair twelve and resume.**

- S130. **NEW 2026-08-09 LATE. Twelve migrations are applied in the database but absent from schema_migrations, and this now blocks supabase db push outright.** Surfaced when pushing the F15a migration: db push refused and listed twelve local files dated 20 to 29 July as “to be inserted before the last migration on remote”. They are all shipped features — S34 system categories, F1 feedback capture, the F7 schema foundation and quote route policy, atomic provisioning, onboarding status, the tone RPC, extracted info, services checklist, essential field shape and category scope, S65 signature fallback. **They are applied; only the bookkeeping is missing**, which is the SQL-editor desync already in the quirks log, accumulated. Running –include-all would re-run them and is not safe. **The fix is to verify each against the live schema and repair as applied, one at a time.** Until it is done every migration has to go through the SQL editor by hand, which is exactly the path that created the problem. Own session. **AMENDED 2026-08-17. The pile is now SIXTEEN.** 20260815000000_agent_runs_quote_routes.sql added under S196 part 2, applied by hand. **Its filename is dated the 15th for work done on the 17th and was deliberately left alone** — it still sorts after 20260814193000, and renaming it would break the match between this list and what was actually applied. **Also confirmed 2026-08-17: there is no S130 tracking file anywhere in the repo.** The orphan list exists only in this document. If that is intended, fine; if anyone has assumed a file exists, it does not.

- S131. **NEW 2026-08-09 LATE. npm audit reports 13 vulnerabilities, 1 critical.** Noted, not acted on. **Do NOT run npm audit fix –force** — it upgrades across major versions. Most audit noise sits in dev dependencies that never reach production and the count means little until reachability is known. Worth one deliberate pass: identify which are reachable at runtime, fix those, ignore the rest.

- S132. **NEW 2026-08-09 LATE. MAILBOX_CREDENTIAL_KEY has no rotation or recovery story, and losing it is unrecoverable.** DEC24 puts the key in an env var. It now exists in .env.local and in Vercel and must be byte-identical in both, or anything encrypted in one environment is unreadable in the other. **If it is lost, every connected mailbox is dead and the only remedy is every tenant retyping their mailbox password** — the reversal cost that was named when the decision was taken and is now live. Wants: the key recorded wherever the service role key is kept, and eventually a rotation path that re-encrypts existing rows rather than orphaning them. Not urgent at one connected mailbox. Increasingly urgent per tenant added.

- S134. **NEW 2026-08-10. The quote_route rules have storage and no editing surface.** S64 phase 1 ships an ordered rules array on tenant_agent_local_rules and writes one always rule at onboarding, but LocalRulesTab was deliberately left untouched, so a tenant cannot see or change it. **Today the only way to change a route policy is to redo onboarding.** Wants: rule rows in Agents › Rules with condition and route pickers, add / reorder / delete. **Sequence with S64 phase 2** — building the UI before distance conditions exist means shipping a picker with one option in it. Note the tab already renders postcode_map and sensitivity_keyword the same way, so the pattern exists.

- S135. **NEW 2026-08-10. Combine the two address asks into one bullet when both are missing.** Same mechanism as the combined evidence ask that shipped in S64 phase 1 — two related fields, one line, built as a synthetic field row before the counting logic. “The full addresses you’re moving from and to.” One less bullet, and a shorter list is materially less overwhelming to a customer. Small once phase 1 is merged, because the combine machinery now exists.

- S136. **NEW 2026-08-10. Pickup and dropoff acceptance thresholds are asymmetric, and it now shows.** pickup_address is STRICT (needs a house number) and dropoff_address is LOOSE (an area counts). That was locked deliberately on 27 July so the pickup value would be usable for distance maths. Observed live: “Fore St, St Ives, TR26” failed pickup while “Causewayhead in Penzance” passed dropoff, so a customer who gave comparable detail for both was asked for one and not the other. **Reads as arbitrary from the customer’s side.** But loosening pickup removes F10’s input, so this is a DEC21 threshold decision and interacts directly with S64 phase 2. Do not change one without the other. **SEE ALSO S152**, which asks the same question from the tenant’s side rather than the customer’s.

- S138. **NEW 2026-08-10. survey_availability should be optional, not mandatory to the survey route.** The field assumes the mover wants the CUSTOMER to propose dates. The operator’s own firm does the opposite — the team manager fits surveys around jobs, so asking the customer for two dates starts a round of cat-and-mouse the mover then has to unwind. **For that shape of business the survey route has no essential field at all**; the evidence is the visit itself. This breaks an assumption made while designing phase 1 (that every route carries evidence) and it is worth stating plainly: **scheduling is not evidence.** Fix falls out of S137 — the tenant toggles the field off and the survey contributes nothing to the ask line while the route sentence still offers the survey. **UNBLOCKED 2026-08-11 BY S137.** No code is now required: the tenant greys the survey_availability pill on the Rules tab and the field stops contributing. Left open only to confirm on a real survey-route enquiry that the route sentence still offers the visit with no date ask attached.

- S139. **NEW 2026-08-10. The F12 test enquiry textarea is too short and cannot be resized.** A realistic enquiry does not fit, so it has to be read through a small scroll window. Desktop at minimum. Trivial, and it is on the surface used to verify almost everything else.

- S140. **NEW 2026-08-10. The onboarding website scrape failed on a JavaScript-rendered site.** Observed on a real signup. **Not established whether this is a regression or whether it never worked on client-rendered sites** — a scrape reading raw HTML would never have had content to find. Read-only investigation first: establish what the scraper fetches and whether any JS-rendered site has ever succeeded. Matters more than it looks, because the scrape prefills the services checklist and the depot address, and a silent failure there sends the mover into onboarding with everything blank.

- S141. **NEW 2026-08-10. Services checklist screen wording.** “Which services do you offer?” with “House moves and quote requests are included automatically” underneath does not make it clear that the mover’s core business is already covered. Change to “Which OTHER services do you offer?” and “Removals / Moving services is included automatically”. Copy only.

- S142. **NEW 2026-08-10. The onboarding quote-route screen asks a per-tenant question that is really per-enquiry, and the copy gives it away.** “Tick all that apply” sits above two options that both end in the word “first”, and a tenant cannot coherently do two things first. **The incoherence is not a wording bug — it is S64 surfacing at the point of capture.** The screen is asking for a single fixed answer to a question whose real answer is conditional: the operator’s own is “photos or a list normally, free survey within 15 miles”, which no combination of three tickboxes can express. **Do not patch the copy.** This screen becomes the rules capture surface, and it should also replace the freeform quoteRouteCondition question, which is collected today only when 2+ routes are ticked and then never read by anything. Sequence with S134 and S64 phase 2.

- S144. **NEW AND SHIPPED 2026-08-11. Auto-send was sending drafts containing unresolved {{markers}} to customers, unattended.** Commit 6fd91e5, direct to main. renderTemplateBodyForDraft leaves any marker it cannot resolve as a literal string (lib/agents/renderReply.ts:40-43). The Review Queue surfaced the postcode case as an amber badge, so a human reviewing a draft would see the mess and fix it — but the cron path selected on auth, resolution, send_after, sender and recipient, and then sent draft_body_html as-is with no inspection of its contents at all. A quote-route template plus an enquiry with no recoverable postcode plus auto-send eligibility equals a customer receiving “we cover the {{town}} area”. **The fix scans for unresolved markers before send and diverts rather than skipping.** Skipping would have left the row matching the selection query forever, re-picked every run, eating the 25-row budget permanently — it had to reach a terminal state for the auto-send path. Content scan rather than reading the existing postcode_missing column, because that column covers only postcode and town while the render path leaves ANY marker literal. Verified with a real inbound email: guard held, nothing sent, clean drafts still auto-send.

- S145. **NEW AND SHIPPED 2026-08-11. S144’s diversion was invisible to the tenant, and the human send path had no guard at all.** Commits 02d1029 and f16a7c6, direct to main. Two gaps found in testing S144. First, the diversion reason was written to agent_review_queue.classifier_reasoning, which **S86 removed from the Review Queue display on 1 August** — written, never shown, so the row looked like any other pending row. Second and worse: **Approve + Send on a diverted row sent the email with the literal marker in it, to a real inbox, with nothing warning the operator.** Fixed with an amber badge reusing the postcode-badge pattern, plus a confirm modal on both Approve + Send and Edit + Send naming the offending marker. **The confirm warns rather than blocks** — the operator may have a reason. OUTSTANDING, TRIVIAL: the badge reads “Not sent — unfilled placeholder”; the em dash makes it longer than it needs to be and it should read “Not sent. Unfilled placeholder”. Fold into the next commit that touches ReviewQueueTab. **A type error shipped to main in the first commit** and broke the Vercel build — see the quirks note on optional-argument handlers bound directly to onClick.

- S146. **NEW AND CLOSED 2026-08-11 WITHOUT A CODE FIX. Investigation only. The conclusion is the valuable part.** The S64 phase 1 migration wrote {{route_sentence}} into tenant_agent_templates.body_html and never into body_text, while the template editor saves body_text and regenerates body_html from it. **So the first edit of any affected template destroys the marker silently and permanently**, and since the send path reads body_html only, the customer then never learns how the mover wants to quote the job. Confirmed live on Joe’s Movers: marker present at the migration timestamp, gone after an edit made during this session’s own testing. **Audited all 20 writers of those two columns. 19 write both. The one that does not is the S64 backfill itself — a one-off, already run.** New-tenant provisioning was traced and is clean: getDefaultLibraryPayload derives body_text from body_html with htmlToText before the RPC, so all three markers land in both columns. **Verified end to end on a fresh signup, including the save-round-trip that destroyed Joe’s.** Existing test tenants are disposable and are being re-provisioned rather than repaired. **A repair SQL was drafted and abandoned** — it reimplemented textToHtml in SQL, and its preview showed it would have rewritten three healthy rows whose HTML came from textToBodyHtml. Re-provisioning is both cheaper and produces rows identical to what a real tenant gets. **The general lesson is in the quirks log: any migration touching body_html or body_text must write both, or the editor will silently undo it.**

- S147. **NEW AND SHIPPED 2026-08-11. Merged as fa4fd0c (PR #52). This is where S68 was actually solved.** Started as a cleanup — the editor had ended up with three views, and one of them was lying. The plain preview substituted some markers, stripped others, and left anything it did not recognise as raw highlighted {{syntax}}, so on a freshly provisioned tenant the route sentence displayed as raw markup while the missing-info block and signature were invisible entirely. **That breaks the one constraint S68 exists to enforce.** Collapsed to two views: preview and raw, corresponding one-to-one. Then the content pass, prompted by the operator’s own observation that the labelled view still did not let a tenant read their email. **The rule shipped is computed, not hardcoded: does this region resolve to exactly one value for this tenant right now?** If yes, render the real resolved text inline, tinted, with provenance behind an (i). If no, render a pill with a worked example. **This is why S64 phase 2 will not reopen this component** — when distance rules give the route sentence more than one possible value, it becomes a pill on its own. Also shipped: green rather than amber tinting (amber reads as “needs attention” and these regions are correct), an explicit “See an example” link because pill styling elsewhere in the app is non-interactive, unrecognised markers now named rather than anonymous, deep links from the signature region to the Agents › Overview signature card and from that card onward to Business Info in Settings › General, both using the existing scrollIntoView + animate-attention-pulse pattern extracted into a shared useHashHighlight hook. **A real first-click bug was found and fixed**: onClickCapture/onFocusCapture on the preview wrapper swallowed the first click on any inner control, so a tenant clicking the example link would have concluded it was broken.

- S148. **NEW 2026-08-11. The greeting renders as a region in the template preview but has no corresponding marker in raw, because it is prepended at render time.** Breaks the one-to-one correspondence S147 otherwise established between what a tenant sees in preview and what they can edit in raw. **Design decision, not a bug — deliberately not fixed.** Three options, reported by Codex and not chosen: keep it and accept one region with no raw marker; remove the greeting from preview, which restores the mapping but makes the preview less like the real email; or materialise the greeting as a real marker in the template body, which restores the mapping but changes the authored template shape and needs a broader decision. **Interacts with S71** (greeting picker), which is gated behind this surface anyway.

- S149. **NEW 2026-08-11. Contact email is not asked at onboarding, so a new tenant’s signature ships with an unresolved value on their first customer email.** Everything else in the seeded signature — company name, phone, website, owner name — arrives from onboarding or the tenant record. Contact email is set only in Settings › General › Business Info, which a new tenant has no reason to visit. **Spotted while wiring the S147 deep link from the signature card to Business Info.** Either ask for it at onboarding or fall back to something sensible rather than rendering an empty or wrong address. Small, and it lands on the very first email a customer receives.

- S150. **NEW 2026-08-11. The S145 badge is driven by the queue reason being manual_hold, which is a state and not a cause.** If anything else in the app ever sets manual_hold for a different reason, the row will display “unfilled placeholder” when there is no placeholder. One grep to establish whether manual_hold is set anywhere other than the S144 diversion path. **Not urgent, but the badge is a trust surface** — it exists to tell a tenant why something did not send, and a badge that lies is worse than no badge.

- **PRODUCT DECISION TAKEN, NOW DEC27.** The move is the primary service; packing, storage and specialist items are secondary. A full property move that also involves any of them is a removals enquiry. Any of them alone is its own category.

- **THE FIX WAS NOT WHERE THE ITEM SAID IT WAS.** Two rounds of category-description rewriting both failed identically on move+storage. The blocker was the spine’s MULTI-MATCH CHECK — it instructs the model to count how many conditions the enquiry satisfies, and never says a stated exclusion cancels a match, so the model counted service mentions and skipped the disqualifier sentence inside the condition it was counting. Live reasoning field, verbatim: “‘Moving out of Truro’ (removals signal) and also ‘need it all in storage’ (storage signal), so it matches both”. **One generic bullet in the spine fixed it — a condition counts only if satisfied INCLUDING any exclusions stated within it.** Deliberately carries no category keys and no removals precedence, so it stays vertical-agnostic and works for any tenant’s exclusion text.

- **SCOPE WAS 184 ROWS, NOT 7.** tenant_agent_categories (7), tenant_agent_essential_info_fields.category_keys (35 jsonb arrays), agent_review_queue.category_key (68), agent_runs.classification (73 on category_key, 3 in matched_category_keys), tenant_agent_classifier_corrections.wrong_category_key (1). **tenant_agent_templates was in the brief and should not have been — it stores category_id, not a key.** Historic agent_runs rows were rewritten by decision, because escalationReason.ts and AgentsTabs.tsx both map stored historic keys through the CURRENT category list, so leaving them would have degraded past badges to raw key text. **Accepted cost, now permanent in the data:** pre-migration runs have migrated keys but un-migrated reasoning prose, so a row can read “Matches both specialist and quote_request” while its matched_category_keys say [“removals”,“specialist”]. Explained in the migration comment, nowhere in the UI.

- **VERIFIED WITH EIGHT REAL INBOUND EMAILS, ALL AT PROMPT VERSION 4.** Joe’s Movers: move+storage -> removals 0.9 drafted (the target case, failed twice before); storage-only -> storage 0.9 drafted (the over-fire risk, clean); move+packing -> removals 0.9; piano alone -> specialist 0.9, matched_category_keys null; piano inside a move -> removals 0.9; “do you clean carpets?” -> escalate with escalation_reason no_match. A1A2 Verify Movers: man and van -> man_and_van 0.9; office move -> commercial 0.9. **The last two mattered most** — commercial and man_and_van descriptions were never rewritten and still carry the older phrasing, so they were the surface most likely to break under a new counting rule. They did not.

- **S122 IS RESOLVED BY THE CARPET TEST**, see that entry. Original entry follows. **NEW 2026-08-11. The quote_request category is named after the shape of the message, every other category is named after a service, and the classifier is paying for it.** Categories are packing, storage, clearance, commercial, specialist, man_and_van — and quote_request. A piano move genuinely IS both a quote request and a specialist job, so the model matches two categories and the multi-match pre-pass escalates. **Observed live twice on Joe’s Movers**: “do you do man and van jobs to Spain” on 7 August (recorded as S122) and “I need to get a Piano moved from St Ives” on 11 August, both with the badge “Matched both Quote request and Piano / specialist items”. It will keep firing on specialist, commercial and man_and_van enquiries, which is every service category a mover adds. **This is NOT a copy change.** Codex confirmed category.label reaches the model in both prompt paths — buildClassifierPrompt falls back to label as the matching condition text when description is absent, and the fallback builder renders “{key}” ({label}): {description} explicitly. So renaming the label alters classification behaviour and needs the same care as a prompt change. **Two separable questions, and they should be answered in that order**: (a) does the KEY move, which is a data migration across tenant_agent_categories, tenant_agent_templates, the category_keys arrays on 95 essential-field rows and any stored classifier prompt, or (b) only the LABEL moves, which is cheaper but still changes model behaviour. **S133 is the precedent and the warning** — that rename needed a data migration nobody expected, and normaliseQuoteRoutes would have silently dropped the old value to an empty array. **Sequence: design conversation first, no code.** Interacts with S122 (which may partly dissolve into this), S141 and S142.

- S152. **NEW 2026-08-11. Address strictness is a hardcoded product assumption and should be a tenant setting.** pickup_address is STRICT and needs a house number; dropoff_address is LOOSE and accepts a town. A mover who wants full delivery addresses has no way to say so. **Surfaced during S137 verification**: an enquiry reading “Camborne to Truro” had its delivery address accepted and its collection address rejected, so the reply asked for one and not the other. The pill under test worked correctly and had nothing to do, because the field was never missing. **The two ends are not equally free.** Loosening or tightening dropoff is a pure product guess with nothing downstream. Tightening or loosening pickup is load-bearing — F10 / S64 phase 2 distance maths needs a geocodable point, so a tenant loosening pickup silently breaks distance-conditional routing. **Likely shape is per field per enquiry type**, the same axis as the S137 pills, since a mover may want full delivery addresses on house moves and not on man-and-van jobs — which argues for building it on the S137 surface rather than a new one. **This is the tenant-facing half of S136 and the same DEC21 threshold decision. Do not design either alone.** Design conversation, and it is naturally forced by S64 phase 2.

- S153. **NEW 2026-08-12. A NULL category description silently downgrades classification to label-matching, and provisioning can create categories that way.** buildClassifierPrompt falls back to the category LABEL as the matching condition when description is absent. So a category with no description is matched on one or two words with no conditions and no exclusions attached. **Four live rows are in this state**: Testing Mover storage and scilly, Newco Removals feedback and scheduling_change. **This cost real debugging time during S151.** The move+storage verification email was sent to Testing Mover by mistake and escalated as multi_match, which read as the fix having failed — it had not, the fix has nothing to attach to on a tenant whose storage category has no description. scilly is the worst of the four: a custom Isles of Scilly category matching on the bare word “Scilly”. **The backfill is trivial; the question behind it is not.** All four sit on tenants created by Codex or Claude Code rather than through real onboarding, which suggests the provisioning path can produce a description-less category. If it can, this will keep happening and every new tenant is a candidate. **Investigate the provisioning path first, backfill second.** Interacts with F17 (repoint the generator to descriptions) and F7.

- S154. **NEW 2026-08-12. Joe’s Movers has a specialist category and no specialist template, so a correctly-classified piano enquiry still escalates.** Observed twice during S151 verification: “I need a piano moved from St Ives to Penzance” classified as specialist at 0.9 with matched_category_keys null — a clean single match — then escalated anyway with a null escalation_reason because there was no template to render. **Right classification, no draft.** The classification half of S151 is only visible in the database; from the tenant’s side the enquiry still lands in Escalated. Joe’s has 6 categories and 3 templates. **The general question is whether a category without a template should be creatable at all**, or whether the gap should surface in the UI rather than at classification time — a tenant who adds a service and no template gets silent escalations with no explanation. Seed the specialist template for Joe’s as the immediate fix; the general case is a design question and probably belongs with F7.

- S155. **NEW 2026-08-12. man_and_van should only match on explicit phrasing, and today it matches on inference.** Operator decision taken in the S151 design conversation: a customer with a small load has NOT asked for a man and van, and the product is aimed at movers rather than man-and-van operators, so the category should fire only when the enquiry says “man and van” or close to it. **The current description resolves the pair from its own side** (“Not full household moves”), which stops the double-match but still leaves the model free to infer the category from load size. Verified working on explicit phrasing during S151 (man_and_van 0.9 on “how much is a man and van from Truro to Falmouth”); the inference case is untested. Description-only change on the same axis as the S151b disqualifiers. Cheap. Sequence after S153, since the wording will want to match whatever convention the description backfill settles on.

- S156. **NEW 2026-08-12. Removals is force-included at onboarding, so a man-and-van-only operator cannot describe their business.** The services checklist adds the core moving category automatically and offers the rest as extras. That is right for the target customer and wrong for anyone whose whole business is one of the “extras” — they end up with a category they never wanted, which under the S151 boundary clauses now actively claims their enquiries. **This is more than the copy fix in S141**, which changes the wording to say the inclusion is happening; this makes it selectable. **Watch the interaction with DEC27**: the primary/secondary rule assumes removals exists as the thing everything else defers to. A tenant with no removals category needs those disqualifier clauses to not fire, or a man-and-van operator’s enquiries fall through to no_match. **Do not build S156 without re-reading DEC27.** Sequence with S141 and S142 on the same screen.

- S159. **NEW 2026-08-13. Number inputs on the add-vehicle card keep their leading zero.** Initialised to 0, and typing appends rather than replacing, so entering 35 produces 035 and 694 produces 0694. Cosmetic on screen and 035 parses to 35, so the stored value is probably right — **but that was assumed, not checked.** Confirm the saved row before deciding this is display-only. Same pattern likely applies to any other controlled numeric input initialised to zero.

- S160. **NEW 2026-08-13. An expired session inside a client component renders a raw Next.js runtime error instead of redirecting to login.** Observed on /vehicles: the page rendered, the client component then requested its data, the middleware returned a login redirect instead of a component payload, and Next surfaced “An unexpected response was received from the server” with a stack trace. **The middleware redirect works; it is the RSC fetch path that does not handle it.** Real users will hit this on any idle tab. Low priority, but it is the ugliest possible failure for something that happens routinely.

- S161. **WITHDRAWN 2026-08-13 before being worked.** Was raised as “refresh token rotation failing under concurrent requests” after AuthApiError: Invalid Refresh Token appeared in the dev log. On inspection this was a stale token after a long gap — correct behaviour, not a bug. **Recorded only so the log line is not re-raised as a defect.** A Supabase session lives in browser cookies and does not die with a dev server restart, so a restart is never the explanation for one of these.

- S162. **NEW 2026-08-13. Job-size condition for photos versus list.** Raised while establishing that distance decides survey-or-not but nothing decides photos-or-list. A mover might reasonably want a list for a 1-bed and photos for a 5-bed. That is a bedrooms threshold, not a distance one, and it fits the same rules[] shape as drive_miles — a new condition_type with a numeric condition_value. **No demand for it yet.** Recorded so the shape is not reinvented. Note the combination sentences already support offering photos and list together, so this is about narrowing, not enabling.

- S163. **NEW 2026-08-13. No post-onboarding surface for editing which quote routes a tenant offers.** quoteRoutes is captured once during onboarding and is unreachable afterwards. A mover who buys a van and starts doing surveys cannot turn the route on; a mover who stops doing them cannot turn it off. **This is now F10 phase 2e** — see that entry for the two open design questions. Kept as a separate S number because the same gap is named from the rule-editing side by S134, and the two are probably one item. **Check S134 before scoping either.**

- S164. **NEW 2026-08-13. Visual aid for choosing the survey distance threshold.** A mover typing a number into an empty box has nothing to calibrate against. **The obvious answer is an isochrone polygon and it is the wrong one:** Google has no distance-contour API, so drawing one means either ~180 Routes calls per render via ray sampling and binary search (about $0.90 each time the number changes, with an interpolated boundary that can disagree with the routing decision), or a second mapping vendor whose road network differs from the one the decision uses. **Cheaper and better: a named-town distance list** — “at 20 miles: Truro 12, Falmouth 18, Newquay 19, Bodmin 24 (outside)”. A handful of depot-to-town legs, reusing route_cache, same data source as the decision so the picture and the answer cannot diverge. Movers read place names, not blobs. **Not until someone struggles to pick a number.**

- S165. **NEW 2026-08-13. Fourteen test tenants have accumulated and there is no cleanup path.** Several are abandoned mid-onboarding with null agent_onboarding_status and no routes — Test co 2, Test co 3, Hello Movers, Hello No Movers, NewCo Removals, S57 Missing Domain Verification. Any query counting “tenants” includes them. **Deleting one has an unmapped blast radius** across tenant_agents, categories, templates, essential fields, agent_runs, agent_review_queue, mailbox credentials and route_cache. Some are also documentation of a sort — several are named after the item they verified. **Decide what a disposable tenant is and how it is removed before the count doubles again.** Not urgent, and not a mid-session job.

- S167. **NEW 2026-08-13. Distance only resolves from a full pickup postcode, so town-only enquiries never route.** computeRouteDistance is called with extracted_info.pickup_postcode, deliberately, because the field mask requests distance and duration only and carries no match confidence — Google resolves a vague string to something plausible and returns a confident number that nobody on the inbound path sees. **But the operator reports that customers frequently omit postcodes**, and a bare town name is not vague: Camborne to Helston is unambiguous, and a centroid is entirely adequate for “is this 15 miles or 40”. Those enquiries currently fall to permit and offer every route. **Option: extract a town alongside the postcode and use it only when no postcode exists, accepting centroid accuracy on that path.** Blocked on knowing how often it actually happens — the real sample is the Cornwall website enquiries in the Hostinger mailbox, and it is a manual count of thirty or so, not a query. Test-tenant data proves nothing.

- S168. **NEW 2026-08-13. There is no shared warning or danger token, so every cautionary surface in the app invents its own colour.** –warn / –warn-bg / –warn-border exist but only in app/(app)/quote/quote.css. vehicle-expiry-bar-expired and vehicle-expiry-bar-due hardcode hex in dashboard.css. The phase 2a onboarding warning shipped with text-[var(–warning, #b45309)] — an inline fallback for a variable that does not exist. Phase 2c added a fourth hardcoded copy deliberately rather than refactor mid-slice. **Promote the –warn trio to globals.css and consolidate.** Cheap, and it gets more expensive with every warning surface added.

- S169. **CLOSED 2026-08-13 — built in F10 phase 2c rather than deferred.** Was raised as “Dashboard survey-distance banner, deferred, needs two sequential queries on the busiest page”. The coupling objection was real but small — roughly 150ms, and it makes the Dashboard’s render depend on agent configuration — and it lost to the point that the mover who most needs the warning is exactly the one who skipped onboarding and never opens Agents. Built with the agent lookup before the existing Promise.all and the rules query inside it. **Dashboard banner is dismissable for the session only; the Rules-tab banner is not.** Dismissal is deliberately not persisted: hiding a live problem indefinitely is worse than a banner someone has to look at.

- S170. **NEW 2026-08-13. Four tenants have an email_responder agent, no quote_route local rule, and null agent_quote_route_policy.** Testing Mover, Newco Removals, S57 Verification Tenant and Test co 2. Three of them show agent_onboarding_status = completed. **Rule presence and config routes are perfectly correlated across all fourteen tenants** — every tenant without a rule also has a null config value — so there is no fallback to recover from and nothing records what those movers offer. They resolve to [“list”] by default, which may not be true of any of them. **Probably an artefact of tenants created before the quote-route onboarding step existed**, but that was inferred, not established.
