No surface exists to inspect or fix it and nothing flags it. Note this does not affect the phase 2d card, which is unreachable for these tenants because needsSurveyDistance returns false without a rule.

- S172. **WITHDRAWN 2026-08-13 before being worked — already implemented.** Was raised as “the missing-info block keeps its heading and bullets for a single item”. renderMissingInfoBlockHtml already renders one field as a sentence, two as two sentences, and three or more as a heading plus bullets. The survey-only draft that prompted this had three missing fields, so bullets were correct; once S171 removed two of them it rendered as a sentence with no change. **Recorded so it is not re-raised.**

- S173. **REPRICED 2026-08-17 FROM LAUNCH-BLOCKING TO A QUALITY ITEM. ITS CORRECTNESS CASE IS SPENT.** The claim below — that one flat list cannot express a choice and that no amount of patching will make it — was tested by read-only investigation (docs/s173-per-route-blocks-investigation-2026-08-16.md) and the structure turned out to be already sound.

- **THE LIVE ROUTE DISTRIBUTION IS WHY.** Six of nine agents are single-route “list” — no choice offered, so no fault possible. One is survey-only, covered by S171. Two are three-route, and S64 phase 1 already collapses multi-route asks into one combined “or” sentence. **Only one of those two was broken, and only because item_list was seeded without a route_key (S195).** Same code, opposite outcomes on two tenants, decided by one column value.

- **WHAT SURVIVES, AND IT IS REAL.** The combinatorics: QUOTE_ROUTE_SENTENCES and COMBINED_EVIDENCE_ASKS still need a hand-written entry per route combination — seven for three routes, fifteen for four — **which is what keeps S174 unaffordable**. And copy quality: one canned combined sentence reads worse than three labelled blocks carrying their own asks. **Neither is a wrong email to a customer, which is why this is no longer launch-blocking.**

- **THE DESIGN WORK WAS COMPLETED BEFORE THE REPRICE AND SHOULD NOT BE RE-DERIVED.** Agreed shape: per-route blocks; **copy stored in rows rather than code**; seeded defaults; **no editor in the first slice**; **three blocks shared across enquiry types, not nine per-category**; and a code-level fallback string per route so a missing row degrades to current behaviour rather than producing an offer with no ask attached.

- **REASONING BEHIND THE TWO NON-OBVIOUS CHOICES, BECAUSE BOTH WILL LOOK ARBITRARY LATER.** Shared-not-per-category: S137’s per-category pills already vary **what** is asked per enquiry type, so per-category blocks would duplicate a dimension that is already solved and ask a mover to write the photos block five times. Rows-not-code: the Driving Vision has the Manager Agent editing this copy conversationally, and a chatbot cannot edit a constant — so the copy has to live in data eventually, and building it in constants means writing it twice. **A per-category override is additive later, not a restructure.**

- **EXPLICITLY REJECTED: a nullable category_key column as a pre-built override.** Null-means-all has already bitten this codebase once — that is exactly the shape that caused S171, where route_key: null quietly doubled as an evidence marker and took three patches to untangle. If per-category overrides are wanted, add them as their own explicit thing.

- **ONE ARGUMENT IN THE ORIGINAL ENTRY IS WEAKER THAN IT READS.** The “our voice, every tenant identical” objection to code-generated copy was written before S68 shipped. S68’s template preview shows system-filled regions with worked examples, so code-generated blocks would be **visible** to the tenant even if not editable. Invisible copy was the real sin; identical copy is a lesser one.

- ORIGINAL ENTRY FOLLOWS, RETAINED AS THE RECORD OF WHAT WAS ASSUMED AND WHY IT WAS OVERSTATED.

- S173 (ORIGINAL). **NEW 2026-08-13. RESTRUCTURE THE QUOTE-REQUEST DRAFT INTO PER-ROUTE BLOCKS. This is the real fix for a fault that three separate patches have now exposed, and it is larger than a typical S item — consider promoting it to an F number when it is scoped.** The draft currently renders one sentence listing the offered routes, then a single merged missing-info list underneath, unattached to any of them. **Routes are an**** ****“****or****”****; the ask list is an**** ****“****and****”****.** A customer told “we can quote by email, or from photos if that’s easier” is then asked for the inventory AND the photos. A customer offered only a survey was asked for the inventory too, until S171 hardcoded that case away. **One flat list cannot express a choice, and every patch so far has fixed one combination and left the others wrong.**

- **THE MODEL: each offered route becomes its own labelled block carrying its own ask.** The operator’s existing Make scenario already does this and the difference in feel is the point — “By email: send through a rough room-by-room list.” / “Or WhatsApp: send a video walk-through.” The customer reads one block and does one thing, instead of reading three options and a pile of homework that belongs to all of them.

- **IT ALSO KILLS A COMBINATORIAL PROBLEM.** QUOTE_ROUTE_SENTENCES and COMBINED_EVIDENCE_ASKS need a hand-written entry per route combination: three routes is seven, four is fifteen, five is thirty-one. Per-route blocks compose whatever is selected, so a fourth route costs nothing. **This is what makes S174 affordable at all.**

- **IT ABSORBS THREE OTHER ITEMS.** S171’s hardcoded suppression becomes dead code and must be deleted with this. S176’s pricing-shaped lead-in disappears, because each block frames its own ask. The excluded_route_keys column scoped and rejected in S171 is never needed, because item_list cannot appear in a survey-only draft when there is no email block to carry it.

- **OPEN QUESTION, AND IT DECIDES THE SIZE: code-generated blocks or template-editable ones?** Code-generated ships fastest and is consistent, but “By email:” becomes our voice and every tenant’s email reads identically — which is the opposite of what makes the operator’s own samples good. Template-editable needs a per-route block concept in the S68 template editor plus defaults for tenants who never touch it, and is much bigger. **Code now with template control later is viable but means rewriting the copy once.** Do not start this without settling it.

- S174. **NEW 2026-08-13. WhatsApp as a delivery channel on the photos route. OPTIONAL, NEVER DEFAULT.** Establishing this is a channel and not a fourth route was the useful part: it is not a different way of assessing a job, it is a different pipe for the same photo evidence, and it is offered because getting a customer to send a video by email or a file-sharing service is friction. So the route set stays at three. **Needs a per-tenant WhatsApp number and an explicit opt-in** — some movers do not use WhatsApp at all, and some would not want customer photos arriving there. **Depends on S173**; bolting a second channel onto the current merged ask block would make it worse.

- S175. **NEW 2026-08-13. route_key is not editable anywhere.** EssentialFieldsSection renders it as a read-only badge (“Only when quoting from a survey”) and the only save action it calls is setEssentialFieldCategories, which writes category_keys and active. A mover who adds their own field cannot scope it to a route. **Revisit after S173**, which may change what route scoping means or remove the need for it — per-route blocks own their asks, so a field’s route membership may become a property of which block it sits in. **NOTE 2026-08-17: S195 changed the ground here.** route_key is now a real per-field value on every tenant rather than a mostly-NULL column, and it now materially controls both filtering and combining. **So making it editable is a bigger decision than when this was raised** — a mover clearing a route_key would silently reintroduce the S195 fault. If this is ever built, it needs a guard, not just a control.

- S176. **NEW 2026-08-13. The missing-info lead-in assumes every ask is a pricing input.** MISSING_INFO_LEAD_IN is hardcoded to “To price it accurately we’ll need” in extractEssentialInfo.ts. A survey-only draft therefore reads “To price it accurately we’ll need a couple of dates that suit you for a free home survey” — dates are scheduling, not pricing. **Resolved by S173**, which gives each route block its own framing. Do not patch the lead-in separately; that would be a second stopgap on top of S171’s. **NOTE 2026-08-17: S173 is repriced, so**** ****“****resolved by S173****”**** ****now means**** ****“****waiting on a quality item****”**** ****rather than**** ****“****waiting on a launch blocker****”****.** The lead-in is still wrong for scheduling asks and still should not be patched alone. If S173 slips indefinitely, re-raise this on its own merits rather than leaving it parked behind something that is no longer urgent.

- S177. **NEW 2026-08-13. Survey scheduling asks the customer to propose dates, and movers would rather offer slots.** survey_availability asks for “a couple of dates that suit you”, which puts the burden on the customer and then obliges the mover to reject dates that do not fit a working week. **Raised in a previous session and re-confirmed here.** Offering slots means knowing availability, which means the calendar, so this is materially bigger than a copy change. Related to S173’s survey block — note the operator’s own Make scenario describes the survey (“one of us pops round, around 30 minutes, fixed quote the same day”) and never asks for dates in the body at all, which may be the cheaper answer.

- S178. **RETIRED 2026-08-14 — DO NOT REUSE THIS NUMBER.** Allocated for “UIDVALIDITY is captured on connect but nothing checks it on sync”. **The check already exists and is correct**, in lib/mailbox/ingest.ts on the unmodified side of the S129 diff: a changed UIDVALIDITY sets sync_cursor to null, status to error, and a plain-language last_error. Raised as a finding without reading the surrounding code. Recorded so it is not raised a third time — this is the same failure mode as S172 and S161. **Note the accidental interaction, which is favourable:** that branch nulls the cursor, and post-S129 a null cursor refuses to sync rather than reading the whole mailbox. Recovery is a reconnect, which repopulates from verified.lastUid.

- S179. **NEW 2026-08-14. At least one sync failure path writes last_error without flipping status.** Observed on the IONOS row: last_error held the raw string “Command failed” for five days while status stayed ok and the card showed green. updated_at was eleven minutes after last_success_at, so a sync failed and left the status alone. **Not all failure paths do this** — the S129 cursor refusal correctly sets status to error. Confirmed separately that last_error DOES clear on a successful sync, so this is a write-side gap, not a stale-value problem. Partially mitigated by S186, which keys the tenant-facing banner off last_error rather than status for exactly this reason, but the status field itself is still wrong and anything else reading it is misled.

- S180. **RESOLVED-AS-MISDESCRIBED 2026-08-16. Investigated under S61; this entry was wrong about the mechanism.** A poison message throws at fetch or insert, so **no agent_runs row is ever created** — but the outer catch does write tenant_mailboxes.status and last_error, so the failure is already visible via the S186 banner and now the System Health mailbox line. It was never fully invisible. **What is actually missing is narrower: nothing distinguishes attempt 1 from attempt 50.** State is byte-identical on every sync except updated_at; there is no attempt counter on agent_runs, mailbox_messages or tenant_mailboxes, and nothing records how many messages sit blocked behind the failing one. So a transient blip and a permanently stuck queue look the same to anyone reading the card. **A nullable failing_since timestamp on tenant_mailboxes is cheaper than a counter and more useful for display** (“failing for 3 days”). Not built. Re-raise with the polling slice — it only really bites on a schedule.

- S181. **RESOLVED 2026-08-16 under S61, and it turned out to be free.** The received status guess in this entry was correct and better than expected: received is the insert-time status on **both** inbound paths — lib/mailbox/ingest.ts and the Resend webhook route — and the pipeline only moves the row off it inside the drafted branch. So a run that died mid-pipeline is still sitting at received and **is detectable by database query alone, on either path**. Surfaced as “N stuck” on the Processing enquiries line using a **15-minute threshold held as a code constant, not a column**. No migration, no attempt counter, no retry mechanism — the item that looked most likely to need a build needed nothing. **The threshold is deliberately generous:** the longest observed real run is under 12 seconds and extraction times out at 12, so nothing legitimate survives 15 minutes, while a run still in flight showing as a failure on a trust surface is the worst available outcome. Confirmed live that a stuck row has no agent_review_queue row at all, so the widget links to Review Queue generally rather than to a row that does not exist.

- S184. **NEW 2026-08-14. Keep the IMAP diagnostic script as a supported tool rather than a scratch file.** scratch-imap-check.mjs authenticates against a mailbox using the installed imapflow, prints the full folder list with paths, names and delimiter, and on failure dumps the raw error name, code and server response text. Credentials come from environment variables supplied at the shell for one run. **It settled the Hostinger question in a single execution after the UI had failed opaquely three times**, and it will be wanted at Cornwall onboarding and every tenant after. Also makes S182’s implementation trivial since client.list() is exactly what the picker needs.

- S185. **RETIRED 2026-08-14 — DO NOT REUSE THIS NUMBER.** Allocated for “signature name should beat the From display name”, then found to be a defect against already-locked behaviour rather than a new design question. Fixed under S87/S90; see those entries.

- S188. **RESOLVED 2026-08-16 under S61 — it was not the wall this entry predicted.** The constraint is real but it is specific to **realtime**: tenant_mailboxes is not in the supabase_realtime publication and carries zero RLS policies under DEC24/DEC25, so a client subscription cannot work and cannot be made to. **But the Dashboard is a server component, and the S186 banner had already established the pattern** — createAdminClient() server-side, pass the row down as a prop. No restricted view was needed and none was built. **The general rule still stands** and is worth keeping: anything wanting live updates from a service-role-only table needs a server path. The answer for server-rendered surfaces is simply to read it on the server and refetch on focus and visibilitychange, which is the pattern that has been quietly masking S60 all along and is sufficient here.

- S189. **NEW 2026-08-14. A manually forwarded enquiry is greeted correctly and would be addressed to the wrong person.** With From set to the forwarder, the greeting resolves to the original customer inside the forwarded content while the reply goes back to whoever forwarded it. **Almost certainly not worth fixing:** server-side auto-forwarding preserves the original From, IMAP ingestion does not forward at all and is the path Cornwall will use, and a tenant forwarding from their own business address is likely caught by skipped_own_domain. Recorded so it is not rediscovered as a bug. Manual person-clicks-Forward is a path the app does not really serve, and this is a symptom of that rather than a defect in either the greeting or the addressing.

- S190. **NEW 2026-08-16. BCC-to-inbox has no toggle.** Every outbound reply BCCs tenant_config.contact_email, locked 9 July as the MVP substitute for a Sent folder — without it a tenant has no record of what went out, because replies leave as their own address. Noticed when test replies kept landing in the tenant’s real mailbox alongside the customer’s. There is no off switch. Changing contact_email would stop it, but that field also feeds business info and template variables, so it is not a workaround. **F15 writeback makes this obsolete** — once the app writes to the real Sent folder, the BCC copy becomes noise in the inbox. **A toggle now is therefore a stopgap for something already scheduled for removal**; weigh it that way rather than as a permanent setting.


- S192. **NEW 2026-08-16. The mobile bottom-bar count badge renders slightly oval on a physical iPhone.** Cosmetic, and **it does not reproduce in desktop browser mobile emulation**, so it cannot be chased in devtools. The widget badges had the same fault and were fixed with a fixed square width/height plus flex-shrink: 0; the bottom bar uses the same shared CountCircle base, so the cause is elsewhere in that bar’s own layout. Explicitly parked by the operator as not worth the time.

- S193. **NEW 2026-08-16. The reveal-password eye icon on the inbound mailbox card does nothing once credentials are saved.** A stored password cannot be shown back, which is correct — but the icon still renders and clicking it produces no response and no explanation. Either say why it is unavailable, or do not render the icon when there is nothing to reveal. Small, and it sits on a card a tenant only opens when something is already going wrong, which is the worst moment for a control that silently does nothing.


- **PART 1 — DISTANCE NOW FALLS BACK TO THE FULL STREET ADDRESS WHEN NO POSTCODE IS PRESENT.** The postcode gate was a choice in computeDraft.ts, not a constraint of the proxy: computeRouteDistance accepts { placeId?, address?, depot? } and passes the string straight to Google, and **the depot end of the same call has always relied on exactly this fallback**. Scale: 46 runs had a full street address with no postcode inside it against 9 with a postcode — **84% of extracted addresses skipped distance entirely and were offered every route**. Safe because pickup_address is captured under STRICT (“a full street address; a town, area or bare postcode alone is NOT present”), so the vague input DEC28’s gate guarded against is already excluded upstream. Shipped: prefer postcode, fall back to pickup_address, no_postcode only when both are absent; new distance_status value **resolved_approx** for address-derived distances (no migration — plain text column, no constraint); **regionCode:**** ****“****GB****”**** ****added to the Routes request** against St Ives Cornwall / Cambridgeshire ambiguity, which held in testing.

- **PART 1 VERIFICATION, INCLUDING THE CHECK THAT ALMOST GOT SKIPPED.** Depot is Helston TR13, threshold 15 miles — confirmed from tenant_config rather than assumed, after an earlier expectation was written against Truro and was wrong. Truro with postcode ~17mi → no survey. St Ives no postcode ~26mi → no survey. Manchester no postcode → no survey. Town only → no_postcode, all routes. **Then Helston no postcode, under 1 mile → survey offered** — the check that mattered, because **a fallback that always returned**** ****“****far****”**** ****would have passed every other test identically**. Finally real inbound email: 14 Meneage Street Helston TR13 8AA → resolved, 1.637 miles, correct for that street to Pen An Drea.

- **PART 2 — AUTO-SEND NOW HOLDS A SURVEY OFFER AT UNKNOWN DISTANCE (PR #62, squash 820a0b4).** The investigation found **two gates, not one**. Gate 1 (computeDraft.ts:476–479, draft time) checks agent flag, template flag and confidence — and has distanceStatus and the resolved route set in scope, unconsulted. Gate 2 (the auto-send cron, send time) checks only for unresolved template markers. **Gate 2 was chosen because its divert mechanism already existed and works:** send_after null, reason manual_hold, human-readable reason appended to classifier_reasoning, run back to drafted, badge already rendering in the Review Queue. **Gate 1 would have produced a silent hold** — its only not-eligible reason is auto_send_off, which the queue does not render at all.

- **PART 2 NEEDED A NEW COLUMN, AND THAT IS THE INTERESTING PART.** Gate 2 could not see which routes had been offered — the route set was computed at draft time, used to render, and **thrown away**. Holding on distance_status alone would have caught 16 scheduled drafts that never offered a survey. So quote_routes text[] on agent_runs persists what was offered (migration 20260815000000, applied by hand, **orphan sixteen**). Hold condition: **quote_routes contains**** ****“****survey****”**** ****AND distance_status is no_postcode or lookup_failed**. Badge text derives from distance_status, so a distance hold reads “Distance unknown” rather than the placeholder wording. **Side benefit worth keeping: a run can now answer**** ****“****what did we offer this customer****”**** ****from a row.** Before this the app decided, rendered and forgot.

- **THREE CONSTRAINTS INSIDE PART 2 THAT A LATER CHANGE MUST NOT QUIETLY BREAK.** (1) **NULL quote_routes NEVER holds.** It means “not recorded”, which is neither “no survey offered” nor “hold it”; 91 pre-existing runs carry NULL and getting this wrong would pull back every already-scheduled draft. (2) **no_depot is deliberately unhandled** — only one tenant has a drive_miles rule and it has a depot, so no live tenant can reach that state, and DEC29’s banners already prompt for it. Building a message for an unoccupied state is speculation. (3) **No new reason value was added** — that would mean dropping and recreating a check constraint; manual_hold already means “held, needs a human” and the badge disambiguates.

- **PART 2 VERIFICATION, ON REAL INBOUND EMAIL, PRODUCTION.** Email 1 (address with postcode) → resolved 1.637mi, quote_routes [“list”,“survey”,“photos”], scheduled_auto_send, **not held** — the negative case, and it matters as much as the positive. Email 2 (town only) → no_postcode, same route set, and after the cron reached it: send_after null, reason manual_hold, **“****Distance unknown****”**** ****badge rendered, nothing sent**. **The planned before/after count of pre-existing scheduled rows turned out moot** — baseline was 0, because the 16 seen earlier the same day had all since been resolved. Nothing was at risk and nothing was proven by it.

- S197. **NEW 2026-08-17. The F12 test panel throws a server error when the page is suspended mid-request.** Reproduced three times. **The trigger is locking a phone screen between runs, not navigating away** — the operator changed neither tab nor app. Mobile Safari suspends the page, the in-flight fetch is killed, and the abort surfaces to the user as a server error. Likely an aborted request being reported rather than caught, **which is a smaller fix than the serverless timeout first suspected**. **The F12 panel is the primary verification surface for every draft-path item in this document, so it must not report failures that did not happen.** Small, isolated.

- S198. **NEW 2026-08-17. LAUNCH-BLOCKING. A BODY-FETCH FAILURE IS SWALLOWED AND THE PIPELINE DRAFTS A CUSTOMER REPLY FROM AN EMPTY EMAIL.** Resend’s email.received webhook carries an **envelope, not a payload** — 531 characters of addresses, subject, message_id, email_id and attachments: [], with no text key and no html key at all. The body must be fetched separately via lib/resend/receiving.ts, which throws on a non-OK response. **Observed with RESEND_API_KEY invalid:** the fetch returned 400 at 36ms, the error was logged — and **the pipeline carried straight on**, making OpenAI calls at 6.30s and 3.44s to classify an empty email, rendering a draft, and creating a review-queue row with **Approve + Send live beside**** ****“****No message body****”**. The run reached drafted. error was NULL. **The app produced a confident customer-facing reply from a subject line, and a mover could have sent it.** A missing body must abort the run and mark it errored so the S61 health surface can report it — **that widget exists for exactly this class of failure and had nothing to show.** **Contributing cause worth deciding on:** commit cbaf3a1 returns 200 from the inbound webhook before running the pipeline, which is correct for webhook timing but means any post-response failure has no path back to Resend — no retry, nothing surfaced. Consider whether a body-fetch failure should be the deliberate exception that returns non-200 so Resend retries.

- S199. **DONE 2026-09-15. Onboarding now captures a depot address.** Shipped across four commits direct to main (`52118cf` capture + server check + save-order fix, `f5131b4` typed-address resolve/confirm/postcode fallback, `dc9f43c` Settings parity [RETIRES the parked stale-coordinates task], `cffe546` depot-removal warn+confirm). **NO migration; the provisioning RPC signature was never touched**, so the create-or-replace overload trap flagged in the original entry never applied — depot is written alongside the RPC call, not through it. Shipped as **conditionally required** exactly as the original entry recommended (survey among routes AND mileage entered), and confirmed that only `depot_address` is needed, not coordinates. Live damage at fix time was ZERO (all 5 survey-rule tenants already had a depot). 10/10 prod checks by the operator. **Full detail archived in movers_app_backlog_archive_APPEND_2026-09-15.md.**

- S200. **NEW 2026-08-17. TEMPLATE ROT ON FOUR LIVE TENANTS, TRACKED NOWHERE UNTIL NOW.** Found incidentally by the S173 investigation. **Tenant dde2e18b (Newco Removals) has TWO removals templates**, both carrying only {{signature}} — no missing-info block, no route sentence, so neither asks the customer for anything. **DEC17 is one template per category and is supposed to be enforced server-side; two rows says otherwise, and that is the part suggesting a code fault rather than test residue.** Tenant 99c73f1b has a missing-info block and **no route sentence** — the customer is asked for details but never told how the mover quotes; same tenant as the two pipeline-error runs in the 2026-08-16 header. **Two tenants have no removals template at all**, so a house-move enquiry escalates. And **{{route_sentence}} sits in body_html only on 5 of 6 tenants — which is S146 still live**: the editor saves body_text and regenerates body_html, so the first template edit silently deletes the route sentence forever. **S146 was closed on the basis that test tenants would be re-provisioned; five rows say that did not happen.** Audit rather than a build, so it sizes itself.

- S126. **NEW 2026-08-09. The Review Queue sort preference is browser-side, not stored per tenant.** Confirmed by search: no table and no column holds it. Consequence is small but real — a mover who sets a sort on their laptop gets the default on their phone. Not a bug, and arguably correct for a view preference. Recorded so it is not rediscovered as one. Would become worth fixing only if other view preferences accumulate.

- DESIGN ITEM (unnumbered) — LOCAL RULES TAB REDESIGN. **MOSTLY DELIVERED 2026-08-09 BY F16.** The tab is now Rules, ordered Sensitivity keywords, Classifier corrections, Postcode map; sensitivity keywords are folded in properly, the reply-to pointer card is gone, and the ERG stub has moved to Generate. **F10’s SURVEY DISTANCE IS NOW ON THE TAB, delivered 2026-08-13 by F10 phase 2d** — a collapsible card at the top of Rules editing the drive_miles threshold and the exclusivity mode. **STILL OPEN FROM THIS ITEM: the sensitivity keyword active/inactive toggle has NOT been removed** — that decision stands and is unbuilt. Original entry follows. NEW 2026-08-01. The tab is structurally wrong, not merely cluttered. Sensitivity keywords do not belong there; the Email Response Generator sits there unfinished (S82); and the reply-to override caption points at the Overview tab. Proposal from the operator: rename the tab to something covering the whole set, fold sensitivity keywords in properly, add F10’s survey radius (initially seeded from onboarding, tenant-updateable thereafter), and keep postcode matching as the advanced escape hatch. Absorbs S21. This is a design conversation, not a brief. ALSO DECIDED IN THE SAME DISCUSSION, not yet built: THE SENSITIVITY KEYWORD ACTIVE/INACTIVE TOGGLE IS TO BE REMOVED. Present means active; delete to remove. The failure modes are asymmetric — a keyword left toggled off silently allows an automated quote reply to a bereavement or dispute, while an accidentally deleted keyword costs retyping one word. There is also no real use case for temporarily disabling “funeral”. Proven in-session: a keyword left inactive produced a false negative that read as a broken feature to the person who built it.

- DESIGN NOTE (unnumbered) — REASONING PILLS ARE INCONSISTENT BY ACCIDENT. NEW 2026-08-01. Only postcode_missing has a pill, because it is a dedicated boolean column on agent_review_queue added for the quote-routing path. Every other missing field lives inside the missing-info block and was never given a flag. Nothing decided that a missing address deserves a pill and a missing move date does not. Not a bug to fix in isolation — the question is whether the row should surface what was asked for at all, and that belongs with S64 or the F11/inbox work.

- DESIGN NOTE from the same session, recorded because it will come up again. Agents Overview mixes instant-save controls (Enabled toggle, Auto-send toggle, Auto-send delay slider) with explicit-save fields, which reads inconsistent. It is correct: a toggle or slider has no invalid intermediate state, so every state it passes through is safe to persist. A text field does — “0.” mid-typing on a threshold, “Jo” on the way to “Joe’s Movers”, and a half-typed reply-to regex would be live against real inbound mail. Instant-save for controls with no invalid in-between state, explicit save for anything being composed. Possible refinement later: save the two numeric fields on blur, leaving only the two text fields needing the bar.

- WHY F12(a) JUMPED AHEAD OF REPLY-FROM-APP 2026-08-04. Reply-from-app is half-crippled until F15: under Candidate A the customer’s response goes to the tenant’s own mailbox, so the tenant would answer in the app and read in Hostinger. It closes half a loop. F12(a) has no such dependency, is useful to a new tenant the day it ships, and collapses the operator’s own ten-minute-per-check verification loop, which every remaining session was paying. That reasoning was borne out inside the same session — see the F12 entry.

- **AMENDED 2026-08-04 by DEC23. The per-tenant stored prompt is RETIRED from the classify path.** There is no prompt_text to expose because there is no stored prompt. The IP rule survives and now attaches to the spine (lib/agents/prompts/classifier-spine.ts), which is shared by every tenant and must never be shown to one. What a tenant sees and edits is their own enquiry-type descriptions, which are their words about their own business, not our prompt architecture.
