**DEC17.** Category-template model = 1:1 conceptually. AMENDED by DEC20.

- Each non-system category has exactly one template. Pipeline already behaves this way.

- Multiple-templates-per-category dropped as premature.

- AMENDMENT (DEC20): there is NO separate incomplete-quote-request category. One quote-request template carries a dynamic missing-info block instead.

- System categories (ignore + escalate) remain templateless.

- UI implication: category/template split hidden from the user. PARTIALLY DELIVERED by S96 (template pill replaced the category pill on Review Queue rows).

- **CORRECTNESS CLAUSE DELIVERED 2026-08-04 (PR #44).** The fix was NOT more categories. It was three fixed safety rules written once into the shared spine, above and below the tenant steps: sensitivity escalates before any tenant step is evaluated; matching more than one type escalates; matching NONE of the types escalates, explicitly including any request for work the business does not appear to do. The camel enquiry now escalates. Nobody had to anticipate camels. **Single run when written; re-verified 2026-08-05 at 8 runs on a fresh tenant using an out-of-scope boiler-repair enquiry — 8/8 escalate. The camel enquiry itself has not been re-run and should be, to close DEC17’s own proof properly.**

- **THE UI CLAUSE IS DELIVERED, WITH ITS CENTRAL DECISION REVERSED. AMENDED 2026-08-09 BY F16.** Tabs became Overview / Generate / Rules / Inbox as agreed. **THE CATEGORY/TEMPLATE MERGE IS REJECTED.** They remain two objects on one tab, Templates above Categories. Reasoning, recorded so it is not re-litigated: (1) placement delivered most of what the merge was for — a tenant who should leave categories alone naturally does when they are at the bottom of the page; (2) the two are edited on completely different cadences, a description drives classification and changes maybe twice ever while wording changes weekly, and merging puts a routing-critical field in the drawer a tenant opens most; (3) a category with no template is a legitimate state — Joe’s “Piano / specialist items” is one — and a merged row cannot represent it honestly, which is the schema saying these are two things; (4) DEC18’s refiner proposes edits to DESCRIPTIONS, and if descriptions live inside the template drawer that feature has nowhere clean to land. **“****The word category disappears tenant-side****”**** ****NO LONGER HOLDS.** It is demoted, not hidden. What was actually broken was the pairing, and that was fixed instead: one template per category enforced server-side, a jump link from template to category, and add-category from inside the template drawer. **The rest of the clause below was delivered as written.** Original text retained for the record: tabs become Overview / Generate / Rules / Inbox. GENERATE is one row per enquiry type — name, plain-English description of when it applies, and the wording — with category and template merged into a single object. No category dropdown, no template count column, and orphan templates become structurally impossible. RULES is the no-reply cases in order: escalate rules, sensitivity keywords, local rules; the reply-to override card and the Email Response Generator stub are removed. INBOX merges Review Queue and Escalated as folders, both badged, with mark-as-done on escalated rows so the badge can clear. The word “category” disappears tenant-side entirely; the table stays in the database because ignore and escalate are outcomes with no template and the classifier still needs to return something for them. DECIDED IN THE SAME DISCUSSION: an enquiry type that both replies AND escalates is rejected as needless complexity — escalate means no auto-reply, always. Note this changes Cornwall’s scilly behaviour, which currently replies and escalates; reseeding Testing Mover makes that moot.

- **ESCALATED IN PRIORITY 2026-08-04, WITH EVIDENCE.** F12(a) made the classifier’s blunt edges visible in seconds. A test enquiry asking to move camels from one field to another classified as Quote request at 85% confidence and drafted a house-move reply, when it should have escalated. That is not a prompt-wording bug to patch in isolation: with a small set of coarse categories, anything unusual is forced into the nearest one at high confidence instead of falling through to escalation. DEC17’s “enquiry types made more nuanced” is the structural fix for exactly this, which makes it a correctness item and not merely a UI cleanup. Note that Testing Mover is still on the v1 manual-seed classifier prompt, so re-test on a generated-prompt tenant before concluding how much is prompt and how much is model. Cross-reference S59 (classifier step ordering varies per run) and S64 (additive route asks) — all three are the same underlying problem: classification logic never designed against a multi-situation tenant.

**DEC18.** F1 phase 2 refiner ceiling — legible structured diffs only, never blind full-prompt rewrites.

- **TARGET CHANGED 2026-08-04.** There is no stored live prompt to diff. The ceiling survives; what it applies to moves. The refiner’s output should now be a proposed edit to a tenant’s enquiry-type DESCRIPTION — visible in the Generate tab, reviewable before acceptance, and owned by the tenant. That is strictly more legible than a diff against hidden prompt text, so the spirit of DEC18 is better served than before.

- Summarising correction history into proposals is the RIGHT home for summarisation, and the only one. It must not happen in the prompt path — see DEC23.

- NOTE 2026-07-29: now the only path that rewrites a live prompt, since enable no longer regenerates. SUPERSEDED by the above.

**DEC19.** Codex stale-main hygiene rule. Unchanged. EXTENDED 2026-07-29: parallel Codex sessions share one working tree on disk. Branch switching swaps committed files; uncommitted work carries across and can be committed to the wrong branch. Either run one session at a time, or give the second session its own clone. Never run git add ., git commit -a or git reset –hard while a second session is active. EXTENDED 2026-07-30: a commit is not a deploy. Every brief that ships direct to main ends with an explicit git push origin main plus a git status -sb report, because Vercel builds from GitHub and an unpushed commit produces no error — only a stale build that looks like a failed fix.

**RESOLVED 2026-08-04 — THE MANUAL-GIT WORKAROUND IS RETIRED. Codex runs git normally again.** Fix was a fresh clone into a directory Codex had never touched: C:-app-2, then icacls grant of (OI)(CI)(M) to Stowell-XPS15on the new tree, plus (OI)(CI)(RX) on C:.config so the global gitignore stops erroring. The old tree’s two explicit DENY ACEs against the orphan SID did not carry into the fresh clone. Note that the first fetch still hit a permissions wall and Codex requested elevated access, which was granted and then persisted for the session — so the sandbox gates .git writes by prompt rather than blocking them outright, and the fresh clone may not even have been strictly necessary. Codex has since run fetch, add, commit, push, gh pr create and gh pr merge unaided. HOUSEKEEPING OUTSTANDING: the old movers-app directory is still on disk and should be renamed to movers-app-OLD, then deleted once movers-app-2 has a day or two of use; rename movers-app-2 back afterwards if the path matters. Superseded text follows. EXTENDED 2026-08-01 — CODEX CANNOT RUN GIT. WORKAROUND WAS IN FORCE. Codex Desktop on Windows adds two explicit Deny ACEs to the .git directory against an orphan SID, plus a CodexSandboxUsers group entry. From the host’s point of view the SID resolves to nothing, so the operator’s own PowerShell git works normally; inside the Codex sandbox it resolves to Codex itself, so Codex is denied. One of the two entries is (OI)(CI)(IO) — inherited by every NEW file created inside .git — which is exactly why FETCH_HEAD and index.lock fail while some other git operations sometimes succeed. Confirmed by icacls and matching openai/codex issue #21304. Running version was codex-command-runner-0.146.0-alpha.3.1; an auto-update to the alpha channel is the likely reason this started on 1 August after roughly three weeks of working normally. icacls /remove:d did not take. UNTIL FIXED: the operator runs the stale-main hygiene and all git add / commit / push in PowerShell, and Codex writes code only. Every brief must state that Step 0 has been done manually, must instruct Codex not to run git fetch, checkout or reset, and should warn that git add may fail with a permissions error on .git/index.lock so Codex reports and stops rather than investigating.

EXTENDED 2026-08-01 — CLEAN TREE DEFINITION. Before .gitignore was fixed, the hygiene gate false-positived every session on .claude/settings.local.json and body.json, which are permanent local-only files. Both are now gitignored, so a clean tree means genuinely no modified, staged or untracked files. If either reappears in git status, something has changed in the ignore config.

EXTENDED 2026-08-01 — THE STOP RULE IS NARROWER THAN IT WAS. “Stop on any command failure” caused Codex to halt on a quoting error in its own search command. Correct wording: stop and report only for ENVIRONMENT failures — PATH, permissions, network, missing tooling — and do not investigate those. Do NOT stop for a failure in a command Codex wrote itself, such as a quoting or syntax error; fix it and continue. A search returning no matches is a result, not a failure.

EXTENDED 2026-08-01 — CODEX HAS NO NETWORK ACCESS TO SUPABASE from its sandbox. Any brief needing live data must ask the operator to run the query and paste the result. The signature is TypeError: fetch failed. Briefs should say so up front rather than letting a session burn a turn discovering it.

**DEC20.** Auto Responder template seeding, variants and rendering — locked 2026-07-27, EXTENDED 2026-07-28 by the copy session, EXTENDED 2026-07-30.

- Seed, do not generate. Flat pool of 6 body variants by stable tenant-id hash. Tone gating cancelled. Route sentence stored separately and substituted at seed time; route sentences never ask for anything. Quote-route condition is routing config, never rendered. Signature is sole source of the signoff. Essential fields store ask + hint. Full addresses, not postcodes. Item list ask is specific, not rough. Photos and survey availability are conditional fields, not baked into route sentences. LLM work at onboarding time only; inbound rendering deterministic. No regeneration when services change. Copy lives in docs/reference/auto-responder-template-copy-reference.md and Codex never authors it.

- EXTENSION 2026-07-28: missing-info block is 0 renders nothing, 1 inline, 2 as rule C (two separate blocks, one per field, each with its own hint), 3+ bullets, fixed field order. IMPLEMENTED 2026-07-30 (S51).

- EXTENSION 2026-07-28: every field carries a shape of exactly noun or clause, selecting the stem (“we’ll need X” vs “we’ll need to know X”). Codex sets it from the doc, never derives it, never rewords an ask to change its shape.

- EXTENSION 2026-07-28: seed copy may only contain claims true for every mover. No timings, turnaround promises or service guarantees.

- EXTENSION 2026-07-28: only quote request takes a route sentence. The other six situations substitute the missing-info block alone.

- EXTENSION 2026-07-28: pickup_address, dropoff_address and move_date are shared rows across quote request, piano/specialist, commercial/office and man-and-van. All other keys are per-situation. NOTE 2026-07-30: this is the extension a second copy register would break. See D5.

- EXTENSION 2026-07-28: long-distance is not a seeded situation. Distance does not change what is asked.

- EXTENSION 2026-07-28 (checklist session): seeding is gated by the tenant’s checklist selection. Every tenant gets quote_request unconditionally; the other six seed only when ticked. Typed extras are stored as strings and never seed anything. Generation, when it lands, is post-Finish and tenant-triggered only — never inside the provisioning transaction.

- EXTENSION 2026-07-28 (checklist session): essential-info fields are scoped to categories via a category_keys jsonb array on the field row, not a join table. Category keys are text with no FK, deliberately. The pipeline filters fields by the classified category and zero matches is valid, rendering an empty block. There is no fallback to “all fields”.

- EXTENSION 2026-07-30 (greeting): the greeting word is resolved at render time from tenant_config.agent_tone, and is the ONLY tone-driven element resolved at render. The signoff is not — buildOnboardingSignature() bakes the toneSignoff() output into signature_html as literal text at onboarding, and the tenant edits it directly thereafter. Changing agent_tone after onboarding therefore changes the greeting and nothing else. This asymmetry is deliberate: the tenant owns the signature, and rewriting a field they can edit is worse than leaving it. Greeting set is Hi and Hello only (S70). Any future tenant-chosen greeting is an explicit picker, not a tone inference (S71).

- EXTENSION 2026-07-30 (register): seed body copy is tone-neutral, which in practice means lightly informal. Any greeting added to the mapping must sit correctly on that register, or the body must move first. This is what ruled out Dear. See S69 and D5.

- **AMENDED 2026-08-10 BY S64 PHASE 1. THE ROUTE SENTENCE IS NO LONGER BAKED IN AT SEED TIME.** Seeded quote-request bodies keep {{route_sentence}} as a live placeholder and it is substituted at render from the route set resolved for that enquiry. Everything else in DEC20 stands: seed do not generate, LLM at onboarding only, inbound rendering deterministic. **The dynamic layer grew by one substitution and gained no non-determinism.**

- **CONSEQUENCE THAT NEEDS WATCHING.** {{route_sentence}} is now a second invisible machinery marker in a body a tenant can edit, alongside {{missing_info_block}}. A tenant who deletes that paragraph silently loses their route sentence. This is more weight on S68, which is why S68 moved up.

- RESOLVED 2026-08-10, WAS OPEN AGAINST THIS DECISION 2026-07-29: S64. The conditional-field model assumed routes are additive. For a multi-route tenant they are alternatives, and the rendered block asked for all of them. Fixed by resolving routes at render time.

**DEC21.** Essential-info extraction architecture — locked 2026-07-27.

- SEPARATE LLM CALL AFTER CLASSIFICATION, never merged into the classifier call. Failure isolation is the reason.

- RESULT STORED AS jsonb ON agent_runs (extracted_info), not a separate table.

- BINARY present/missing. status IS A STRING, NOT A BOOLEAN, so a third state can be added later as copy work rather than a migration.

- ACCEPTANCE THRESHOLDS ARE PER FIELD, defined in the extraction prompt. pickup_address STRICT because F10 depends on it. dropoff_address, move_date, access_notes, photos, survey_availability LOOSE. item_list strict-ish. Tenant-added fields default to LOOSE. Unknown always resolves to missing.

- POSTCODE DERIVED from pickup_address into its own jsonb key.

- EXTRACTION NEVER FAILS THE RUN. Explicit 12s timeout on this call only.

- NO CROSS-EMAIL MERGE. Every inbound is treated as standalone until F11 threading exists.

- INBOUND RENDERING STAYS DETERMINISTIC.

**DEC22. OBSOLETE 2026-08-04 — the thing it protected no longer exists. THE CODE ENFORCING IT WAS REMOVED 2026-08-05 (F17a, commit e499837).** Until that commit the enable path still called the generator, still wrote a row to tenant_agent_classifier_prompts, and still refused to enable the agent if the LLM call failed — a gate on a write with no runtime consumer, which could only create a failure mode, never prevent one. Removed, with a comment at the removal point recording that DEC23 retired it. Verified on Joe’s Movers by toggling off and on and then sending a live test email. There is no active classifier prompt row to be missing, because there is no stored prompt. An agent enabled with no categories now produces a spine with an empty tenant-steps block, and the spine’s own safety rules escalate everything, which is loud and safe rather than silently degraded — exactly the outcome DEC22 wanted, achieved structurally instead of by convention. The enforcement-by-convention note and the updateAgentOverview single-path rule can be dropped. Do NOT delete this entry: S55, S56 and F6 phase 2 still cross-reference it and will read as broken without the explanation. Original text follows. An agent is never left enabled without an active classifier prompt — locked 2026-07-29.

- The guarantee is about end state, not preconditions. First enable generates then switches on. Later enables just switch on, because a prompt already exists. If generation fails, the agent does not switch on. There is no state where enabled is true and no active prompt row exists.

- Why it matters: an agent enabled without a prompt does not stop. The pipeline falls back to the global scaffold, so mail keeps flowing and the tenant sees drafts — just consistently worse ones, or endless escalations, with nothing on screen explaining why. Silent degradation, at the worst possible moment: a new tenant’s first minute after onboarding.

- ACCEPTED COST: if the generator is unavailable, a tenant cannot enable their agent at all. Previously they could, and it silently underperformed. Visible failure is preferred to invisible degradation.

- ENFORCEMENT IS BY CONVENTION, not a database trigger. A trigger would also reject direct SQL, which is used constantly against disposable test tenants. There is one permitted path today: updateAgentOverview in app/(app)/agents/actions.ts. If a second path ever appears, revisit enforcement.

- CROSS-REFERENCED FROM: S55, S56, F6 phase 2. Any work touching the enable path or writing enabled must answer: does this leave an agent enabled without a prompt?

- Regeneration of an existing prompt is NOT part of enabling. It happens only via the regenerate endpoint (S38) and, later, the F1 phase 2 refiner under DEC18. SUPERSEDED — see DEC23.

**DEC23. Runtime classifier prompt assembly plus the correction loop — locked 2026-08-04, shipped the same day (PR #44, PR #45). AMENDED 2026-08-05 by S106.**

**DEC23 AMENDMENT 2026-09-18 (F31 PR 2a, operator decision).** The spine text in source control stays the DEFAULT, versioned and tested as before. On top of it sits an operator override layer that covers every global prompt INCLUDING the spine: `prompt_versions` (immutable rows) + `prompt_publications` (append-only log; the newest global row per key decides what is live; `version_id` null means "use the code default"). Locked rules: (a) if the DB cannot be read, or an override is missing a required placeholder, the code text is used, so a draft never fails because of this system; (b) nothing is ever overwritten: every save is a new version, every publish and every revert is a new publication row, full history is kept forever; (c) each version stores a fingerprint of the code default it was based on, so the console can flag "code default changed since your edit"; (d) workflow is save as draft, test in the console against a chosen tenant, publish, revert to any version or to the code default; a spine version must be tested before it can be published; (e) each run records `classifier_spine_override_version_id` next to `classifier_prompt_version`, so a change in results can be traced to a version. The determinism guarantee is unchanged for a given live spine: same rows in, byte-identical prompt out. Spine edits are expected to be rare and to follow a diagnosis, not to be routine. Per-tenant customisation is covered by DEC56.

- AMENDMENT, STORAGE ONLY: worked examples are no longer read from tenant_agent_classifier_corrections, which is dropped. They are read from the classifier_worked_examples view over ai_classifier_feedback. The substance of DEC23 is unchanged — corrections are still concrete examples, still never summarised, still evicted by (wrong -> right) pair rather than by recency, and worked examples still sit ABOVE the decision tree with an explicit STEP 0. Only the location of the rows changed.

- CAVEAT ADDED 2026-08-05: “corrections improve accuracy through use rather than configuration” is now known to be true and insufficient. See S114. A correction carries no statement of what made the classification wrong, so the model generalises from the whole email unobserved. DEC23’s third layer is real but is not yet safe to expose to a tenant. **RESOLVED 2026-08-07.** S114 shipped: a correction now carries tenant-supplied evidence, that evidence replaces the email body in the prompt, and nothing enters the prompt until a contradiction check passes. Layer 3 is safe to expose and is exposed. The claim stands as originally written, with one addition — accuracy improves through use, and **the tenant can now see and delete every lesson the agent has learned.**

- THE PROMPT IS NEVER STORED. It is assembled on every classify by string concatenation: a fixed spine, plus one decision-tree step per active category row using that row’s description as the condition, plus worked examples from the correction table, plus the tenant’s sensitivity keywords. Same rows in, byte-identical prompt out. No LLM writes prompt text at any point.

- WHY THIS OVER A STORED SNAPSHOT. A stored snapshot cannot see a tenant’s edits (that was S55, and it failed silently for weeks). It also required an LLM to author it per tenant, which was itself a source of drift — S56 caught one agent accumulating six materially different versions in forty minutes. Removing the generator from the prompt path removed real variance. It did NOT remove the variance in S59, which lives inside the model and would have been identical under a frozen snapshot.

- ACCEPTED COST, AND IT IS REAL: there is no longer an artefact to read. Under the old design you could open a row and see exactly what a tenant was running. Now the prompt has to be reconstructed to be examined. Mitigation is cheap and should be taken — surface the assembled prompt on the F12(a) test panel, service-role only, never tenant-facing. See S107.

- THE THREE LAYERS, which is the whole design and should be restated rather than re-derived. LAYER 1, the tenant writes their business in their own words: one list of enquiry types, each with a description and the wording to send. Nothing else — no keywords, no thresholds, no step order, no decision trees. LAYER 2, a fixed safety net we own: unsure escalates, matches nothing escalates, matches two escalates, sensitive escalates. The system’s default answer to anything unfamiliar is give it to the human, so a tenant never has to anticipate edge cases. LAYER 3, correction: the tenant sees a wrong call in the Inbox, says what it should have been, and that correction joins the prompt as a worked example on the next run. Accuracy becomes a function of use, not of setup.

- CORRECTIONS ARE STORED AS CONCRETE EXAMPLES AND ARE NEVER SUMMARISED. An example works because it is specific; summarising ten into a generalisation converts the strongest available signal back into the weakest, which is a description — the exact thing examples exist to compensate for. Summarisation also means an LLM writing prompt text again, compounding across generations, which is the S56 failure with extra steps. Summarisation belongs in F1 phase 2 as a PROPOSAL to change a description, where it is visible and reversible. See DEC18.

- EVICTION IS BY (wrong -> correct) PAIR, NOT BY RECENCY. Keep the two most recent per pair, global cap 30, warn on the cap. A flat recency window is wrong because a working example generates no further corrections, so it looks idle and gets evicted by unrelated newer ones, the fixed mistake returns, and the tenant re-fixes something they already fixed. Grouping by pair means an example can only be displaced by a newer correction of the same mistake, which is the one time you want it replaced. Hitting the cap of 30 is diagnostic, not routine — it suggests the tenant’s descriptions are wrong.

- WORKED EXAMPLES MUST OUTRANK THE DECISION TREE, and this was learned the hard way in the same session. A2 first emitted them below the tree; a matching tenant step fired and STOPped before the examples were ever considered, and the correction had no effect at all. A2b moved them above the tree and added an explicit STEP 0 that consults them first. Any future edit to the spine must preserve that ordering.

- THE GENERATOR SURVIVES, REPOINTED. It no longer authors prompt text. It writes the tenant’s enquiry-type DESCRIPTIONS at onboarding — same model, same tenant context, different target field. Strictly better placed: the output is visible and editable in the Generate tab, it runs once on a field rather than rewriting itself, and a bad result is something the tenant can see and fix rather than a silently degraded prompt. See A3.

- DEC1 and DEC12’s IP rule are unchanged in force and now attach to the spine.

**DEC24. Mailbox credential encryption is APP-SIDE, not database-side — locked 2026-08-09, shipped the same session.** AES-256-GCM in Node, key in the env var MAILBOX_CREDENTIAL_KEY, iv:authTag:ciphertext stored in one text column. The database only ever holds ciphertext. Supabase Vault and any Postgres-side encryption are explicitly rejected.

- **What decided it:** with app-side encryption a database compromise alone yields nothing usable, because the key is not in the database. Vault puts key and data with the same vendor. Against that, app-side puts the key wherever the deploy target ends up — and DEC16 settles that as Vercel Pro, so it does not float.

- **HONEST RECORD OF HOW THIS WAS DECIDED: Vault’s current supported status was never actually checked.** It was flagged at the time as needing verification and the session had minutes left. The pick is defensible on failure shape alone, but it was made without full information and that is why it is written down rather than assumed.

- **REVERSAL COST IS THE REASON THIS IS A DEC.** Switching encryption later means re-collecting the mailbox password from every connected tenant by hand. Losing the key means the same thing. See S132.

**DEC25. Mailbox rows are service-role only, and a stored credential is never rendered back — locked 2026-08-09, shipped the same session.**

- **RLS is enabled on tenant_mailboxes with NO policies at all.** Authenticated and anon get nothing, including the non-credential columns. Every read goes through the service-role client. The tenant sees address, watch folder, status and last-success through a server-rendered page, never by querying the row. Verified: pg_policies returns zero rows for the table.

- **No decrypt path returns a credential to any client component**, and the password field is empty on every load. A show/hide toggle on the field is acceptable and was added — it reveals only what the user is currently typing, never anything stored. That distinction is the whole rule: your own input is yours, a stored credential is not retrievable by anyone.

- **VERIFY BEFORE PERSIST is part of this decision, not an implementation detail.** Connect authenticates against the mailbox FIRST and only writes the row on success, so a failed attempt can never overwrite a working credential. Verified deliberately: resubmitting with a wrong password left the ciphertext byte-identical and the status unchanged at ok. **A mover cannot break their own live mailbox connection with a typo, and that property is worth more than the error message it produces.**

- **Cost accepted:** every mailbox read is server-side forever. Mild constraint, cheapest possible policy surface, and it matches the never-rendered-back rule structurally rather than by convention.

**DEC26. Gates are deterministic. LLM judgement is advisory and asynchronous, never a send-time gate — locked 2026-08-11.**

- Raised by the operator off the back of S144/S145: if we now check emails for unresolved markers before sending, why not have an LLM check every outbound reply makes sense?

- **Cost is not the objection.** A reply body plus a short instruction is roughly 600-900 tokens in and tens out. At 200 tenants and ~20 enquiries a day each that is around 4,000 calls daily — low single-digit dollars on a cheap model. Estimated, not quoted.

- **Latency is.** Classify already measures 7.8s median and 18.1s worst. The cron path can absorb that; a tenant who has just clicked Approve + Send cannot. S105 exists because latency on a watched surface matters and latency on an unwatched one does not.

- **Non-determinism on a gate is a different animal to non-determinism on a draft.** S111 is open right now because identical input produced two different extraction results on two tenants. A wrong extraction produces a slightly-off email; a wrong GATE blocks a correct email or passes a broken one, inconsistently, so the tenant cannot learn the rule and the operator cannot reproduce the complaint. Worse, a wrong block puts the row in Review Queue and makes the tenant overrule the machine — twice and they stop trusting the badge, which is the thing that makes S145 worth having.

- **And it is the model marking its own work.** The system that produced the draft would be judging whether the draft is sane, with no independent source of truth about what the email should say.

- **What we do instead: keep growing the deterministic checks.** S144/S145 caught a real class of failure precisely, at zero latency and zero cost, with no false positives. Others in the same family are available — empty missing-info block when fields are missing, signature absent, body below a plausible minimum length, a marker that resolved to an empty string where it should not have. Each one is cheap and each one is certain.

- **The LLM check is revisitable as SAMPLING, not gating.** Check a small percentage of already-sent replies asynchronously and surface patterns to the operator. Gets the signal without the latency, the cost, or the false-block problem. Not scheduled.

**DEC27.** **LOCKED 2026-08-12 (S151). The move is the primary service. Packing, storage and specialist items are secondary.** A full property move that also involves packing, storage or a specialist item is a REMOVALS enquiry and gets the removals template. Any of those three on its own is its own category. **Rationale:** every category description already ends with “including requests for a price or quote”, so every category is a request — naming one of them after the shape of the message rather than the service was the original defect. Packing-only is rare in practice (the operator’s own business has never done one); storage-only and specialist-only are common and stay routed to themselves. **Mechanically this lives in two places and both are needed:** the secondary categories carry a binary disqualifier (“does NOT match if the enquiry also involves moving belongings to a new address”), and the spine’s multi-match check honours it (“a condition counts as satisfied ONLY if the enquiry meets it INCLUDING any exclusions stated within that condition”). **Commercial and man_and_van are deliberately NOT part of this** — they resolve their own pair from their own side and adding a second exclusion risks a plain house move matching nothing. **Re-read this before S156**: it assumes a removals category exists for the others to defer to.

**DEC28. Distance for quote-route selection is ROAD MILES, DEPOT TO PICKUP — locked 2026-08-13, shipped the same session.** Quote-route conditions resolve against driving distance from the tenant’s depot to the pickup address, measured by the existing Google Routes proxy via computeRouteDistance (lib/routes/distance.ts). **Not straight-line. Not the full round trip.**

- **Why road miles.** The endpoint already existed and returns miles with travelMode: “DRIVE”. Straight-line would require geocoding both ends from raw address strings, rejected on two grounds: an offline postcode dataset is roughly 150 MB against a 500 MB free-plan database ceiling that puts the project read-only when breached, and Google’s terms cap coordinate caching at 30 days so a permanent lookup table cannot be built from API results. Road miles is also what the Cornwall quote engine prices on, so routing and pricing share one measure and cannot contradict each other. Movers reason in road miles — fuel and hours, not crow-flies.

- **Why depot to pickup.** The condition governs one thing: whether the auto-responder offers a survey. Survey feasibility is a function of getting a surveyor to the property and back. Delivery distance is a pricing input with no bearing on it. **A 5-mile pickup with a 200-mile delivery is surveyable and scores as local — correct, not a compromise.**

- **Condition type is drive_miles.** Not within_miles / beyond_miles, and not distance_radius — the latter was the name in a pre-existing test asserting the type was ignored, and it is a straight-line word for a road-miles measure. Renamed while the only reference was that test. Direction comes from condition_value, not from two type names.

- **Threshold lives in the local rule, not tenant_config.** kind: “quote_route”, key: “policy”, an entry in value.rules[] carrying condition_type / condition_value / routes / exclusive. One value per tenant. A tenant_config column would have been a second home for one concept.

- **RULE ORDER IS SIGNIFICANT. always is the terminal entry and conditional entries go before it.** resolveQuoteRoutes iterates in array order; always is a catch-all, so anything appended after it can never fire. This is the constraint most likely to be violated by the next person to touch the array.

- **SEMANTICS ARE SUBTRACTIVE, NOT FIRST-MATCH-WINS.** The base set comes from the terminal always entry. A conditional entry whose condition is NOT met subtracts its routes from that set. Met and non-exclusive leaves the set unchanged. Met and exclusive: true replaces the set with that entry’s routes. Unresolvable leaves it unchanged. Unknown condition types are ignored entirely and never subtract. If subtraction empties the set, fall back to [“list”].

- **exclusive only governs behaviour INSIDE the radius.** Beyond it, exclusive and non-exclusive are identical: survey is subtracted either way.

- **Store the measurement, not the conclusion.** agent_runs.distance_miles (numeric) and distance_status (text: resolved / **resolved_approx** / no_depot / no_postcode / lookup_failed / null — resolved_approx added 2026-08-17 by S196 part 1 for address-derived distances; the column is plain text with no check constraint, so this needed no migration). **No is_local boolean** — the threshold can change, and a stored boolean computed against an old threshold is silently wrong with no way to detect it. Miles are a derived measurement rather than Google’s coordinates, so the 30-day caching restriction does not apply to storing them.

- **AMENDED 2026-08-17 BY S196 — THE POSTCODE-ONLY CLAUSE BELOW NO LONGER HOLDS.** Distance now falls back to the full street address when no postcode was extracted, and records distance_status: resolved_approx when it does. **The reasoning below survives for TOWNS and is why the fallback is safe:** the field mask has no match confidence, so Google resolves vague input to something plausible — but STRICT extraction already excludes towns, areas and bare postcodes, so the address string reaching this code is a real street address. **84% of enquiries that captured an address captured no postcode inside it, so the original gate was discarding almost all of them.** regionCode: “GB” is now sent against same-name ambiguity. Original clause follows.

- **Gate on STRICT acceptance and use the postcode, not the address.** The field mask requests distance and duration only, with no match confidence, so Google resolves a vague address to something plausible and returns a confident number. On the quote panel a human catches that; on the inbound path nobody does.

- **Unresolvable distance PERMITS the route.** Over-offering is visible to the mover and correctable; under-offering is invisible and they never learn what they lost. Same asymmetry as blank-means-no-limit in DEC29. **AMENDED 2026-08-17 BY S196 PART 2 — THIS CLAUSE STANDS AS ROUTE-RESOLUTION SEMANTICS AND WAS DELIBERATELY NOT CHANGED, BUT ITS REASONING IS NOW BOUNDED.** The asymmetry holds while a human reads every draft. **Under auto-send an over-offer has already reached the customer**, and a retracted free survey is reputational damage to the tenant — the unrecoverable class, not the recoverable “failed to help” class this argument assumed. So route resolution still permits the route, and **auto-send now holds the draft** when a survey is among the offered routes and distance did not resolve. See S196 part 2 and the DEC31 candidate. **Inverting the resolution semantics was considered and rejected:** it would silently withdraw the survey offer from every genuinely local customer who did not type an address, which is precisely the invisible under-offer this clause exists to prevent.

- **No radius UI existed when this was locked**, so there were no saved thresholds to migrate and no tenant expectations to break. The map-circle framing came from F10’s original description, not from shipped code. Free that day, breaking once a tenant had saved a value.

**DEC29. Survey distance capture and surfacing — locked 2026-08-13, shipped the same session.** How the DEC28 threshold gets set, and what happens when it is not. **REAFFIRMED 2026-09-16 (F51):** the onboarding + Local Rules survey-distance field carries a PLACEHOLDER, never a committed default value; untouched = blank = no-limit. A committed `"15"` shipped briefly and was removed — it read as a chosen cap while being treated as blank, wasting test cycles and risking a silent under-offer.

- **Blank means NO LIMIT, not**** ****“****never****”****.** A mover who clears the field is choosing to offer surveys at any distance. Not 0, not the prefilled 15. **The reasoning is the failure-mode asymmetry:** over-offering a survey is visible on the enquiry and the mover can walk it back; silently not offering surveys is invisible, and they never learn about the jobs they did not win.

- **A blank threshold is warned about, twice, and neither warning is dismissible in a way that hides a live problem.** Inline in onboarding as a bordered amber caution, and as a self-clearing banner on Dashboard and Agents › Rules. Both disappear the moment a distance is saved — setting the value IS the dismissal. **The Dashboard banner has a session-only X; the Rules banner has none.** Dashboard is where a mover is doing something else, so it earns a dismiss; Rules is where the setting lives, so the banner is context rather than interruption. **Dismissal is never persisted** — a mover who clicks X in week one must not still be silently over-offering six months later with nothing on screen.

- **The onboarding distance step is gated on survey being among the selected routes.** Not on route count. A mover offering only surveys still needs a limit; a mover offering photos and list needs no distance at all.

- **The exclusivity step is gated on survey PLUS two or more routes**, and deliberately NOT on a distance having been set. **The gate would not hold:** a mover can set 15, answer the exclusivity question, go Back and clear the distance in three clicks. Gating on something reachable by back-navigation is theatre. The blank-distance warning covers that state instead.

- **“****Within your survey distance****”**** ****stays in the exclusivity question’s wording even when no distance is set.** Considered and rejected: rephrasing to avoid assuming a radius would read as contradicting the job-assessment question two steps earlier, where the mover just ticked three boxes. Framing it as a condition is clearer than framing it as a contradiction.

- **The threshold is editable after onboarding, in Agents › Rules.** Added in phase 2d because 2c’s banner pointed at Rules where nothing could set one. The card collapses by default when a distance is set and stays expanded when it is not — **the mover the banner is shouting at must never have the fix hidden behind a toggle.**

- **Which quote routes a tenant offers is still NOT editable after onboarding.** See F10 phase 2e and S163. This decision covers the threshold only.

**Decision candidates (raised 2026-08-01, NOT yet locked)**

- **DEC31 CANDIDATE (raised 2026-08-17, NOT yet locked). Auto-send holds a survey offer when distance did not resolve.** DEC28’s route-resolution semantics are unchanged: unresolvable distance permits every route. **What is bounded is the reasoning, not the rule.** “Over-offering is visible and correctable” holds only while a human reads every draft; under auto-send the over-offer has already been sent, and a retracted free survey is reputational damage to the tenant — the class this project treats as unrecoverable. **So: when a draft offers a survey and distance did not resolve, auto-send is held and the draft goes to the Review Queue with reason manual_hold and the badge**** ****“****Distance unknown****”****.** Route resolution is untouched. Shipped under S196 part 2 on this basis; recorded here so it is a decision rather than an accident. **NUMBERED DEC31, NOT DEC30 — the nav-badge candidate above already claimed DEC30 on 2026-08-16.** This is the same collision risk the S131/S133 note exists for; two candidates were briefly competing for one number.

- **THE GENERAL LESSON FROM THE DEC31 CANDIDATE, worth more than the fix itself.** A locked decision whose reasoning depends on a human being in the loop needs re-examining the moment any path becomes unattended. DEC28 was locked 13 August with auto-send already shipped, and **nothing in this document connected the two.** Worth a deliberate pass over the locked decisions asking which of them assume review.

- **DEC30 CANDIDATE (raised 2026-08-16, NOT yet locked). Dashboard is the only badged nav item.** Counts surface once, on a nav item visible from every page, and break down per widget on the Dashboard itself. Other nav items stay bare regardless of what they own. **Rationale: duplicating the same counts across two nav items makes both unreadable** — a mover seeing “Dashboard 5” and “Agents 3” cannot tell whether that is five things or eight. In-page and in-tab badges (S84) are unaffected; this governs nav only. Shipped under S61 and S187 on this basis; recorded here so it is a decision rather than an accident.

These came out of the 1 August inbound-email discussion. They are recorded here rather than as DEC entries because they were not explicitly locked. Each should be locked or rejected before the work it governs begins.

**CANDIDATE A — Reply-to stays the tenant’s own address. STILL NOT LOCKED. NARROWED 2026-08-09 LATE — read the header before locking this.** Only the reply-to clause itself is still live. The contact-form inbound clause is superseded for any tenant with a connected mailbox, and the volume figure below is void, because F15a slice 1 changed what inbound is. The lock, when taken, should say: reply-to stays the tenant’s address FOR LAUNCH, revisited when F15’s thread-check guard is live — not before, not never — and the two-inbox consequence is accepted as TEMPORARY rather than structural, because the collapse is now scheduled rather than hypothetical. Original text follows. This is the decision that makes launching without F15 viable, and it changes what launch means, so it deserves an explicit lock.

- The customer’s reply goes to the mover’s normal mailbox. The conversation continues where they already work. The app handles the first response and steps back.

- The alternative — reply-to pointing at our inbound address — would pull the whole conversation into the app and make the in-app inbox real. It was rejected for now on two grounds: it moves customer conversations out of a mailbox the tenant has used for years before the app has earned that, and without F15 there is no thread check, so a mover replying from their phone would double-reply to a customer.

- Consequence accepted: the tenant monitors two inboxes. That is structural and only F15 collapses it. Adding reply-in-app now does not fix it — it splits the thread earlier.

- Framing for tenants: the app answers first, fast, so nobody waits, then it is yours. Honest, and strong given that a first reply within minutes is most of what wins a removals job.

- Consequence for the roadmap: F15 is not launch-blocking. What IS launch-blocking from the same discussion is smaller — S23 (done), F11 subject line and threading, and quoting the original enquiry under every reply.

- Inbound for launch is website contact forms, which is a clean path rather than a consolation prize. Most forms accept multiple recipients, so the tenant keeps their normal mailbox copy and the app gets one too. No absurd inbound-{uuid}@chualexua.resend.app address on promo material, no forward rule to break silently. Phone enquiries are out of scope entirely, which also means per-tenant email volume is low — dozens a month, not hundreds — so the in-app inbox does not need to scale before it needs to be good.

**CANDIDATE B — Mailbox writeback safety rules (governs F15). HALF LOCKED 2026-08-09.** The credential rule below is now DEC24 and DEC25 and is shipped. **The writeback rule is STILL OPEN and is now the next thing to lock**, because it governs the slice that moves mail inside a real business’s mailbox, and it was free to lock while it was on screen and was not. Original text follows. Two hard rules that should be locked before any adapter is built, because both are cheap to honour up front and expensive to retrofit.

- We are operating inside a real business’s live mailbox. Only ever move a message we hold an agent_run for. One at a time. Never a batch operation. Never a delete.

- Credentials: IMAP means holding a working password to the tenant’s mailbox. Encrypted at rest, service-role only, never rendered back to the UI, not even masked.

**ALSO SETTLED IN THE SAME DISCUSSION, no decision entry needed but recorded so it is not re-litigated:**

- Auto-send stays ON by default. Raised as a risk and withdrawn: with reply-to set to the tenant’s address the app usually replies within a minute or two, so the mover rarely beats it, and turn two onwards is theirs anyway. Sensitivity keywords escalate the cases that matter and are confirmed working (S47).

- Replying from within the app is acceptable. Initially argued against, then conceded — the only real gap was the tenant’s Sent folder missing a copy, and outbound already BCCs tenant_config.contact_email (verified 15 July). Still needs F11 first.

- Escalated should become a worklist rather than a log. A row already dealt with currently looks identical to one that has not been, so the list only grows. Replying from the app would clear rows naturally, which is the better answer than a bare mark-as-handled button. The app cannot detect a tenant’s own reply without F15 — Make can do it because Make logs into the mailbox; we only see mail arriving at our address.

**CANDIDATE C — RAISED 2026-08-04, NOT RESOLVED. Classify-then-template versus a freehand LLM responder.** Recorded so it is not lost, and because the operator has now raised it directly after watching the classifier misfire on the F12(a) card.

- The question: would a freehand LLM responder, given enough company and job context, get replies right as often or more often than the current classify → select template → substitute missing-info-block machinery?

- The case for freehand: it would never send a house-move template to a camel enquiry. Nuance is where the current model is weakest, and every fix so far (S59, S64, DEC17, the reasoning-pill inconsistency) is a symptom of coarse categories rather than a bug in isolation. It also removes an entire layer of configuration the target user does not want to own.

- The case against, and it is the reason the current architecture exists: templates are auditable and controllable. A tenant can read their template and know what their customers receive. With freehand they cannot, and cannot check before it goes out. Freehand also invents — prices, availability, promises — which is precisely the failure class recorded elsewhere in this document as unrecoverable: an app that fails to help is survivable, an app that damages a tenant’s reputation with their own customer is not.

- The current system is already a middle position: the LLM classifies and extracts, templates control the words that reach the customer.

- **UPDATE 2026-08-04 (evening). DEC17 IS NOW RESOLVED, so the**** ****“****resolve DEC17 first and see what is left****”**** ****instruction has been carried out. What is left is smaller than it looked.** The camel failure is fixed by the spine’s safety rules without any move toward freehand — the model now escalates anything it does not recognise instead of forcing a fit. And the correction loop means nuance improves through use rather than through configuration, which was freehand’s main claim. What freehand would still buy is a reply that reads as if it addressed the specific enquiry rather than selecting a template. Weigh that against S59: the classifier is currently non-deterministic, and a freehand responder inherits that variance in the words sent to a customer rather than merely in which template is chosen. That is a much worse place for it. **Settle S59 before revisiting Candidate C at all.** **UPDATE 2026-08-05: S59 is settled, so that gate is now open.** Note what settling it revealed: the variance was caused by underdefined category descriptions, not by the model. A freehand responder has no categories to define, which removes that failure mode entirely — and replaces it with the same class of ambiguity operating directly on the words sent to a customer, with no template to bound it. Read S110 and S113 before reopening this: both are about who owns the definitions, and freehand’s real claim is that nobody has to.

- **UPDATE 2026-08-09 EVENING — REFRAMED, AND THE REFRAME IS THE USEFUL OUTPUT. THE QUESTION IS NOT FREEHAND VERSUS TEMPLATES.** Two things were established this session. **(1) THE MAKE SCENARIO IS STATIC TEMPLATES PLUS ROUTING** — the same shape as the app, minus the dynamic parts, with routes covering missing addresses, missing item lists and so on. **It is not freehand.** So the months of unattended real-world evidence that would have counted heavily FOR freehand do not exist; the evidence is for classify-then-template, which is what is already built. **(2) THE DYNAMIC LAYER CAME FROM DEC20 AND DEC21, DELIBERATELY.** DEC20 removed the separate incomplete-quote-request category and DEC21 locked per-field extraction as a post-classification call; that pair is the moment “a route per missing-info combination” became “one template plus a dynamic block”. It was a decision, not drift.

- **AND THE REASON IT WENT THAT WAY IS THE NUMBER OF SITUATIONS.** Make has essentially one situation with a handful of routes — a small enumerated set, where static-per-combination is entirely viable. The app has seven. Static-per-combination across seven situations by missing-info states by noun and clause shapes is the same combinatorial trap D5 was rejected for. **SO THE REAL OPEN QUESTION IS WHETHER SEVEN SITUATIONS WAS THE RIGHT NUMBER.** Fewer situations makes static viable again, and that is a far smaller and more answerable question than freehand-versus-templates. **UNDECIDED. Own session, chat only, no code. Do not tangle it into F15.**

- **WHERE THE FREEHAND INSTINCT IS RIGHT, RECORDED SO IT IS NOT DISMISSED.** Almost none of the last month’s work has been classification — S64, S51 rule C, S53, S70, D5, DEC17’s merge question and S69 are all copy-assembly plumbing, the cost of making templates behave under combination. **S64 is a bug that cannot exist in a freehand responder.**

- **WHERE IT DOES NOT HOLD.** “Enough sensible rules in the prompt” is the same claim descriptions make, and S59 is the record of how that went. Freehand does not remove the definition problem; it moves it into one large prompt where nobody owns it and nothing checks it — a bad description is visible and editable by the tenant, a bad prompt clause is invisible, ours, and global. **And freehand plus auto-send-on-by-default is the genuinely dangerous combination**, because auto-send-on was settled deliberately under Candidate A and freehand invents prices, availability and promises with no human between the invention and the customer. **Templates are not valuable because they are better writing. They are valuable because they bound what can be said on an unattended path.**

- **THREE THINGS ARE SEPARABLE AND PROBABLY SHOULD STAY SEPARATE HOWEVER THIS LANDS.** Routing (which situation is this — keep it; it drives escalation, and escalation is the safety valve). Wording (where freehand would actually earn its keep). Facts — price, availability, route policy — never generated, always retrieved, with a hard rule that no number, date or commitment appears unless it was supplied.

- DO NOT DECIDE THIS IN A TIRED SESSION OR AS A SIDE NOTE. It is DEC-level and it interacts with DEC1, DEC3, DEC12, DEC17, DEC20 and DEC21. It also partly overlaps DEC17’s “more nuanced enquiry types”, which may deliver most of the benefit without giving up control — resolve DEC17 first and see what is left over.

**CANDIDATE D — RAISED 2026-08-09 EVENING, NOT RESOLVED. Billing at launch.** Settings carries a Billing tab. No payment path appears anywhere in this document — D9 mentions “retain-data billing wiring” and nothing else. Launching as a manual-invoice pilot is a perfectly good answer, and so is building a payment path, but **neither has been decided and the absence has been reading as an oversight rather than a choice.** Decide which, record it, and if it is the pilot, record what triggers the switch.

**CANDIDATE E — RAISED 2026-08-09 EVENING, NOT RESOLVED. Was seven situations the right number?** See the 2026-08-09 update under Candidate C, which is where the reasoning lives. Recorded separately because it is answerable on its own and Candidate C is not. Chat session, no code. It decides how much of the copy-assembly layer is worth keeping and therefore how much of S64, S68, S69 and D5 is worth paying for.

**CANDIDATE F — RAISED 2026-08-09 EVENING, NOT RESOLVED. Cornwall cutover, and shadow mode.** Should the operator’s own firm move off Make, spreadsheets and the WordPress Quote Engine and onto the app now? **Answer taken this session: email no, everything else yes.** Email no, because Make reads the mailbox directly, moves messages to folders, sees the manager’s own replies, and sends from the firm’s own domain — four things the app cannot do until F15 and F19 land, so switching would be a downgrade. Everything else yes: quotes, jobs, leads, fleet, team and reports touch none of that gap, and it is where real dogfooding pressure lands on surfaces only ever eyeballed on test tenants. **BLOCKED ON ONE UNVERIFIED THING — see S127.**

- **[SUPERSEDED 2026-08-24 — SHADOW MODE IS NOW DONE; see header. Original entry retained below for reasoning.]** **SHADOW MODE IS THE FREE MIDDLE OPTION AND IS NOT YET DONE.** Point the website contact form at BOTH the Hostinger mailbox and the app’s inbound address. Make keeps replying exactly as now; in the app, auto-send off, so it classifies, drafts and queues and sends nothing. Yields real enquiry volume, real classification accuracy, real escalation rates, and drafts comparable side by side against what Make actually sent. Zero customer risk, costs one form field. **It also unblocks three items currently waiting on data that does not exist** — F18’s volume question, and the D13 and D14 revisit triggers, both of which say to watch escalation reasons once there is real traffic. There is no real traffic anywhere in the system today.

- **CORRECTION TO A FRAMING THAT WOULD OTHERWISE MISLEAD THE LAUNCH BAR.** The inference “if we cannot use it as efficiently as our current setup, nor can a paying user” does not hold. **Cornwall is not a representative tenant.** Its baseline is a tuned Make scenario running the full mailbox loop; a new signup’s baseline is a mover reading email on their phone and typing replies by hand. App-versus-nothing is a clear win, app-versus-Make is a loss. Do not let Cornwall’s unusually high floor set the launch bar.

**ITEMS RAISED 2026-08-25 (F19b test + merge session)**

- S207. **NEW + SHIPPED 2026-08-25 (fix session, commit 94ec540). Generic-send crash-handling.** Scenario C crashed the whole Agents page with an uncaught 500 because RESEND_FROM_EMAIL was unset (not in Vercel, not local). requireGenericSendFromAddress() throws when the var is absent, and the throw propagates to the page-level error boundary rather than returning {error} to the review queue. **Immediate trigger fixed** by setting RESEND_FROM_EMAIL = noreply@richardstowell.com (placeholder — see S209/DEC33). **The defect that STANDS regardless of the env value:** the unverified+acked generic path is the only send path that throws uncaught instead of erroring gracefully like every other block (B returns a clean inline error). Any future unresolvable-generic state — var removed, Resend rejects the from-domain, a transient failure — still white-screens the Agents page for a reachable production tenant state. **Fix: wrap generic-from resolution and the Resend send so failures return a graceful inline error, never an uncaught throw.** Found by C because the generic path had never been exercised end-to-end before this session. **SHIPPED:** wrapped generic-from resolution + the Resend send so an unresolvable generic state returns a graceful inline error to the review queue instead of an uncaught throw; Agents page stays rendered. Background auto-send path degrades to auto_send_failed on the queue/run row. Verified end-to-end on S146 (RESEND_FROM_EMAIL unset → inline error in queue, run row manual_send_failed, outbound_email_id null, page never white-screened).

Manual-send block error names two exits and links only one.** The inline error reads “Verify your sending domain or acknowledge generic sending in Agents before sending replies.” “Verify your domain” is now a link (shipped this session); “acknowledge generic sending” is not. A user who wants the faster path — acknowledge and send now — is told it exists but has no inline route to it, and is sent to Agents to hunt for it. Options: add a second inline action that triggers the consent modal in place, or adjust the copy so it stops implying both routes are equally reachable here. Same family as the link fixes shipped this session (79493f5, 8758ae8). **SHIPPED 2026-08-25 (commit e8a3358):** A-lite — second inline action opens the shared GenericSendingConsentModal (deliberate consent preserved, no auto-send, no silent ack), verify link unchanged; lead copy trimmed to “This reply can’t be sent yet. Choose from the following:”. Modal extracted to a shared component now also reused by the Overview auto-send toggle.

GracePeriodBanner + LockedBanner body strings — "Billing issue — resolve within…" / "…paused — reactivate…" — confirmed still present 2026-09-07; the new captured-row "No draft. Billing issue." pill + note were written dash-free). Grep every user-facing string for the em-dash character and replace app-wide — its own reviewable diff. **Fold in the misleading folder path while here:** SendingIdentityCard.tsx lives under app/(app)/settings/general/_components/ but renders on Agents Overview after E’s move (it is a shared component, one import — the F spacing fix confirmed this). Cosmetic tech-debt, zero functional impact.

- S212. **NEW + SHIPPED 2026-08-25. Reversible generic-sending consent.** autosend_consent_ack was a one-way flip with no UI to withdraw. Added a Turn off control on the sending-identity card (shows for unverified+accepted tenants only; hidden when verified). Turn off sets autosend_consent_ack=false and autosend_consent_ack_at=null, AND cascades to disable tenant_agents.auto_send_enabled — without the cascade an unverified tenant could sit auto-send-on with no valid consent, and every auto reply would fail. Turn-off is the safe direction, so no consent modal. Also fixed a stale-toggle defect (OverviewTab held auto_send in local useState that wasn’t re-syncing from fresh server props after the mutation; revalidatePath was already firing — the sync effect now resets local state from agent.auto_send_enabled) and renamed user-facing “acknowledge” → “accept” app-wide (DB columns / code identifiers unchanged). Commits db688156 (build) + d7acc848 (toggle + copy). Verified on S146: DB cascade confirmed by query (ack=false, ack_at=null, auto_send_enabled=false), toggle flips off live with no refresh, block copy correct, S199 verified-tenant shows no Turn off control.

Implements DEC31.** sendReplyEmail gained an optional replyTo passed through to Resend; resolveSendFrom now returns contactEmail SEPARATELY from from; all three send callers (manual approve, edit+send, auto-send) pass tenant_config.contact_email as Reply-To. **DECISIONS 2026-08-25**

- **DEC32 — tenant_sending_identity is its own table.** Chosen over bolting columns onto tenant_config, for cleaner future change. Locked when F19 slice A shipped 2026-08-24; logged here 2026-08-25.

- **DEC33 — the app’s generic sending identity is an app-owned domain, TBD/to-provision.** noreply@richardstowell.com is a PLACEHOLDER for development only. **HARD LAUNCH GATE: RESEND_FROM_EMAIL must be replaced with an address on an app-owned, Resend-verified domain before any real unverified tenant goes live.** Never use a tenant’s own domain (cross-tenant leak); never ship the personal placeholder to production. Implementation is one env-var value plus a redeploy once the domain is verified — the send code already reads RESEND_FROM_EMAIL. See S209.

- **DEC31 — CONFIRMED + IMPLEMENTED 2026-08-25 (v2 session).** Reply-routing model: customer replies land in the tenant’s own inbox (Reply-To = contact_email), NOT routed into the app by default. Confirmed after the reply-to investigation established two facts: (a) no Reply-To was set on any outbound send, so generic-path replies (From = generic noreply) went to a void nobody owns; (b) the app also ingests via IMAP mailbox sync (F15a shadow rig), so a mailbox-connected tenant’s replies come back into the pipeline regardless — the earlier “app only listens to its inbound address” claim was wrong, corrected in-session. Chosen over app-managed routing because the app cannot yet hold a conversation (thread replies escalate, quote calc untested) and sole custody of customer threads carries data-loss / missed-enquiry risk before it’s earned. Progression: a future per-tenant “app manages conversations” opt-in flips Reply-To from contact_email to inbound_address — one header value, no rearchitecture. Implemented by S214. **NEW ITEMS 2026-08-26 v2**

- S217. **CLOSED 2026-09-02 — NOT REPRODUCED, DROPPED FROM LAUNCH GATES.** Attempted repro on S199 (live prod URL, not preview): added a calendar entry, it appeared, persisted after hard refresh, DB row confirmed present; a second new-calendar creation test was also clean (Main/Vehicles/Team Hours + two events rendered). The original demo failure was most likely a stale-preview view or a mis-step, not a prod bug. D, still open — gates whether there is a pricing page and what it says), and the domain being on Vercel (S43). Still LAUNCH-BLOCKING; design half done, build + cutover + legal-fill remain.

- F22. **NEW 2026-08-26. Shared-inbox reply tracker for the generic sending address. Feature. NOT launch-blocking (post-launch).** Closes the “void” left by the generic send path: mail sent TO hello@send.moversapp.app is currently read by nothing, so a customer who replies to From (instead of the Reply-To=contact_email that S214 sets) is lost silently. Would set up a genuine inbox on the generic address, feed replies into the existing inbound pipeline (webhook → classify → route → queue, most of which exists), and kick each reply back to the owning tenant. **The hard part is attribution, not ingestion.** hello@send.moversapp.app is ONE shared address across ALL tenants; the address carries no tenant marker (the clean-address win from S209/DEC34). Only clean signal is the **In-Reply-To header** (customer’s reply carries the original outbound’s Message-ID → look it up → tenant/run). **Prerequisite: F11(d)** — outbound is persisted NOWHERE today (established at S214), so there is no Message-ID → tenant map to match against. This stacks ON TOP of F11(d). **Must-have fallback:** a reply with no In-Reply-To (plain-text client, stripped, forwarded) is unattributable → needs a triage surface a human clears, else it voids in the app’s box instead of nowhere. **Data-surface caveat:** a shared monitored box means the reader sees replies for all tenants → cross-tenant data surface, GDPR-adjacent; design read access accordingly. **Do NOT** implement as a Porkbun forward on hello@ — collides with the Resend MX already on send.moversapp.app. Insures a small slice (generic-path tenant × client ignores Reply-To × In-Reply-To present) of an already-small slice.

**DECISIONS 2026-08-26 v2**

- **DEC34 — brand / product name / domain LOCKED = moversapp.app.** Registered at Porkbun 2026-08-26. Resolves the brand decision that gated S43, the privacy policy, the homepage (F21), and the entire Google OAuth track. Sending is on the subdomain send.moversapp.app (Resend, eu-west-1); app hosting and inbound will sit on the apex / other subdomains via S43. Localpart for generic sending is hello@ (not noreply@) — a reply-ability/trust choice: S214 already routes replies via Reply-To = contact_email, so the From localpart is cosmetic to routing but not to how replyable the address LOOKS to a customer. See S209 (DONE) and the session summary’s reply-ability note.

# 2026-08-27 SESSION — AMENDMENTS + NEW ENTRIES

**These entries are authoritative and supersede earlier conflicting text on S43, S42, DEC31, and F15 mailbox auto-send. No code shipped this session — infrastructure (operator dashboards) + locked decisions only. main unchanged at e3fdbf0.**

## Decisions locked

- **DEC35 — MAILBOX AUTO-SEND IS IN LAUNCH SCOPE. Supersedes DEC31’s “mailbox-sourced runs held for review until the race guard ships (post-launch).”** The mechanism that makes mailbox auto-send safe is NOT the Sent-folder race guard (F15 Problem B) as previously assumed — it is **safety-by-separation via folder writeback** (the Make model): the app auto-replies confident runs and FILES them into a “responded” folder; everything else escalates untouched to the human. App-handled and human-handled become **disjoint piles**, so the double-reply race DEC31 held auto-send off for does not arise. **Residual (accepted for launch):** a narrow window between a fresh enquiry landing and the app filing/sending it (≤ sync tick + auto-send delay) during which a human working the inbox could grab it first — Make has this too and it is tolerated. Mitigated by a short default auto-send delay for inbox-only mode + a copy pointer near the delay timer (S218). **F15 Problem B (Sent-folder race guard) is DEMOTED to post-launch insurance** for this residual window, built only if real usage shows it bites. App-only mode has no race at all (only the app watches the letterbox).

- **DEC36 — SINGLE-MODE INBOUND MODEL. A tenant picks ONE of: app-only (letterbox) OR inbox-only (own mailbox) — not both.** Both-active = duplicate ingestion / mess. Mode chosen via an onboarding survey (“How do most enquiries reach you?” → Direct email / Website form; social media is a future third option, no inbound integration built yet). A large in-app toggle controls the mode and gates which config surfaces show, so tenants never face the both-config junction (driving-vision alignment: kill the config junction, show only what’s relevant). **The raw inbound UUID address (inbound-{tenant}@…) is NEVER shown to tenants** — app-only mode presents it behind a friendly frame; inbox-only tenants never see it. **Guardrails:** (a) each mode has a precondition — app-only needs a form pointed at the letterbox, inbox-only needs a working mailbox connection — and the toggle must guide/block when unmet, or a tenant silently receives nothing; (b) switching mode after setup must tear down the old mode cleanly, not strand in-flight enquiries; (c) inbox-only needs a LOUD “mailbox stopped syncing” signal — Errors & Health on the dashboard exists but inbox-dwelling tenants don’t visit it, so it needs a louder channel (ties to F14 push). **Onboarding placement:** foundational (it decides which config even shows) so it belongs early, but onboarding is near-shell (F9) — at launch this is a first-run settings choice with a sensible default; the plain-language Manager-Agent version is post-launch (F6).

- **DEC37 — INBOUND DOOR IS DERIVED FROM MAILBOX STATUS; THE TOGGLE IS A MANAGEMENT-SURFACE CHOICE, NOT THE DOOR. Refines DEC36 after slice-2 build surfaced two fused concepts.** Two independent axes, not one: **(1) DOOR** = where enquiries enter the pipeline. NOT a preference — derived. A tenant with ≥1 verified mailbox (`tenant_mailboxes.status='ok'`) is on the **mailbox door**; the letterbox goes DORMANT for them (mail sent there is recorded but not processed). A tenant with no verified mailbox is on the **letterbox door**. Gate is one-directional: only the letterbox is ever blocked, and only when a mailbox is live — there is no “mailbox dormant” path. **(2) TOGGLE (`inbound_mode`)** = where the tenant *manages* (app vs their own inbox). This is the real choice, and it only bites once auto-send is decided: with auto-send OFF, drafts exist only in the app, so approval happens in the app regardless of toggle. Mailbox-managed therefore = mailbox door + auto-send on; it is not a separate stored surface. **We do NOT auto-flip auto-send when a mailbox connects** — that is the tenant's call; the mailbox toggle position shows a one-line “drafts are approved in the app; turn on auto-send to work fully from your inbox” note instead. **Data responsibility (why both doors still leave a full copy):** managing from one place must not mean data lives in only one place. App-managed tenants still get everything in their own mailbox (S226 Sent-append + writeback filing); mailbox-managed tenants have the full record in the app. **Why the letterbox still exists at all** (post-mailbox-sync): it is the door for tenants whose mailbox we CANNOT read yet (Gmail/Outlook, pending F15 OAuth adapters) or who won't hand over creds — and it is a genuine selling point (a tenant can run with no external mailbox at all). The mailbox door is a superset for anyone syncable; the letterbox is not redundant, it is the fallback + no-mailbox path. **`app_only`/`inbox_only` naming is now a slight misnomer** (`app_only` really means “no mailbox / letterbox door”) — rename deferred, not done. **Wrong-door mail** (real enquiry hitting a dormant letterbox — only happens if a form is mis-pointed) is recorded under its own status `letterbox_dormant` (NOT `ignored`) so it stays recoverable and distinct from the classifier's spam-`ignore`; surfacing it in-app is an S224 requirement (slice 3). **C (re-route dormant-letterbox mail into the mailbox pipeline) was REJECTED** — it can't tell “only copy” from “duplicate of what's also in the mailbox”, so it re-opens the DEC36 double-reply. The fix is visibility, not re-routing.

- **DEC38 — RECOVER RELIES ON THE PIPELINE'S EXISTING `message_id` DEDUP TO PREVENT DOUBLE-REPLY; THE UI BADGES THE LIKELY-HANDLED CASE PRE-CLICK; FUZZY MATCH DEFERRED. LOCKED 2026-08-31 v2 (F23 slice 3a-2).** When a form feeds BOTH doors, the mailbox drafts the enquiry AND the letterbox records a `letterbox_dormant` ghost; recovering the ghost must not produce a second reply. **Guard (already live, NOT new):** `processInboundRun` calls `isDuplicateMessage(tenant, message_id)` as its first step, matching across ALL sources — so a dormant ghost whose `message_id` equals a `source='mailbox'` sibling resolves to status `duplicate` with no draft. Recover reuses this; nothing extra was built for the strong-match case. **Visibility (new this slice):** the Filtered fetch flags dormant rows that have a same-tenant same-`message_id` `mailbox` sibling and shows an amber "Likely already handled via your mailbox" badge BEFORE the tenant clicks. **Badge only — never auto-hide:** auto-hiding could bury a genuine second enquiry (the DEC37 "C rejected" principle — flag, don't re-route — resurfacing at the action layer). **Strong match only:** dedup is exact `message_id` (one physical email hitting both doors, e.g. a form To/Cc'd to both). The **fuzzy case** (two separate submissions, different `message_id`, matched by from+subject+time window) is DEFERRED — not built, may not be worth it. Gotcha for if it ever is: `from_address` format differs by door (`mailbox` stores `"Name" <addr>`, `resend_inbound` stores bare `addr`), so a from-match must normalise to the bare address or it silently never fires.

  - **AMENDMENT 2026-09-03 — UPGRADED TO LAUNCH-BLOCKING + RE-SCOPED. The "not a launch blocker" verdict is REVERSED (operator decision).** Own-domain sending is essential from launch — a mover must be able to send as their own domain day one, not post-launch. That makes the cap a launch gate, and reveals the real problem is Resend's pricing MODEL: it prices per verified DOMAIN. Resend tiers: $20/mo = 10 domains, next ~$90/mo = 1000 domains — you pay for domain slots before sending anything, which scales badly for multi-tenant own-domain. LIVE SYMPTOM (found this session on Sep Movers): the Sending-identity "Add domain" field is exposed to every tenant but returns a raw `403 {"statusCode":403,…"You have reached the domain limit of your plan"…}` — the three Resend slots (`inbound.moversapp.app`, `send.moversapp.app`, `cornwallmovers.co.uk`) are full. That raw error to the tenant is S220's leak, live. The wiring WORKS (the app did call Resend's domains API; refused on quota, not missing code). **FIX DIRECTION (vendor NOT locked): switch the own-domain path to a per-EMAIL transport.** Amazon SES is the frontrunner — up to 10,000 verified domain identities per region at NO per-domain charge, $0.10/1k emails; 1000 tenant domains = $0 for domains + ~$10–16 for 100k emails/mo vs Resend's $90 before any send. SES trade-offs: infrastructure not managed (no suppression UI / deliverability team, more AWS wiring); NEW ACCOUNTS START IN SANDBOX (send only to verified addresses until production-access granted — a PRE-LAUNCH GATE); new accounts default to "Essentials" $0.16/1k, switch to à-la-carte for $0.10; per-domain DKIM via Easy DKIM API (same DNS-publish UX F20 handles). PREFERRED SHAPE = HYBRID: keep the proven Resend generic path (`send.moversapp.app`, DEC33/34) untouched; route only the own-domain path through the per-email transport, so a misstep can't break the address every tenant already uses. Alternatives (Postmark / Mailgun / SparkPost — per-email + free domains + better managed UX than SES?) to be compared before locking. Ties S220 (raw error leak), S219 (send-gate button prominence), S208 (dead `from_email` — becomes correct once own-domain works via a real transport). **MUST be decided pre-launch.**

- **DECISION PENDING (pre-launch — will lock as DEC42 once the vendor is chosen). Own-domain send transport.** Resend's per-domain pricing model is REJECTED for the own-domain path. Launch will use a per-email-priced transport for own-domain sending, HYBRID with Resend retained for the generic path. Specific vendor (SES vs Postmark/Mailgun/SparkPost) NOT yet chosen — pending a comparison discussion (operator wants it, but not this session). Not assigned a DEC number yet because a DEC = a locked decision and the vendor is open. Once locked, run the **S241** auto_send/verified check on the first genuinely-verified own-domain tenant this transport makes possible.
