# 2026-08-31 v3 SESSION — F23 3a-2 (finished) + 3b-1 + 3b-2 + 3c + tsc fix

**Six ships to main (db3c566). No launch-plan restructure. One migration (`forwarding_note_dismissed_at`) + one reconcile migration (`inbound_mode`), both applied via MCP. Full narrative in the 2026-08-31 v3 session summary.**

## Completed

- **F23 slice 3a-2 — DONE (both halves).** Recover-dormant half was DONE 2026-08-31 v2 (PR #78). **Ignored-override half DONE this session (DEC39):** an override action on `ignored` Filtered rows flips `ignored`→`escalated` for manual handling (NOT a draft). Owner/admin-gated, `.eq("status","ignored")` idempotency guard, no pipeline re-run, no dedup guard (human-gated). DB confirmed an ex-`ignored` escalated row is shape-identical to a normal escalation (classification present, category null), so the Escalated tab renders it unchanged; no migration. Verified on prod (creditcontrol + eBay test rows escalated clean, then reverted to `ignored`).

- **F23 slice 3b-1 — DONE (DEC40).** Inbound door now follows `tenant_agents.inbound_mode`, not mailbox presence. `getInboundDoor` returns `mailbox` only when `inbox_only` AND ok mailbox (else fail-safe letterbox); `syncTenantMailbox` skips ingest unless `inbox_only` (keyed on MODE, so a broken inbox_only mailbox still retries). Reconcile migration flipped the one `app_only`+ok-mailbox tenant (`99c73f1b`) → `inbox_only` = behaviour-neutral on merge. Verified on PROD (preview couldn't — masking cron, see quirks). PRs #79/#80.

- **F23 slice 3b-2 — DONE (PR #81).** In-app segmented toggle "How do you want to manage enquiries?" (In the app / In my own inbox); owner/admin `setInboundMode`; letterbox inbound-address hidden in inbox mode, MailboxCard hidden in app mode (UI-only, config persists), soft-warn when inbox_only without ok mailbox, DEC37 note under inbox mode.

- **F23 slice 3c — DONE (PR #82).** Dismissible amber app-mode forwarding note (persisted via new `forwarding_note_dismissed_at`); re-arms on a real switch into app mode, no-ops on same-segment re-click; note at top of tab; stray "Add more paths using AI" button removed; **Overview tab renamed "Setup"** (label only, `overview` key kept); inbound-address helper text sharpened. 3b-3 guardrail slice retired (covered/moot).

- **S229 — DONE (direct to main).** Type-corrected the mailbox test fixtures/mocks added in 3b-1 (`vi.mocked()` wrap + missing `tenant_mailboxes` Row columns) so `tsc --noEmit` is clean. Test-only, orthogonal → direct to main per branching rules.

## Decisions locked

- **DEC39 — OVERRIDE OF AN `ignored` FILTERED ROW = ESCALATE-TO-QUEUE FOR MANUAL HANDLING, NOT FORCE-DRAFT. LOCKED 2026-08-31 v3 (F23 3a-2).** Re-running an `ignored` row through the pipeline just re-ignores it (same body, same classifier, same settings → same verdict), and an ignored run carries no category → no template → nothing to draft from. So an "override" cannot mean "make a draft"; the only coherent meaning is **escalate the row to the Escalated queue for a human**. Implementation is a pure `ignored`→`escalated` status flip (owner/admin-gated, `.eq("status","ignored")` guard = idempotent, no reprocessing). **No dedup guard needed** — unlike Recover, escalate runs no pipeline and sends nothing; it's inert and human-gated, so the worst case is a human opening an already-handled item and closing it (annoyance, not a double-reply). **No reason column exists** (`agent_runs` has no `escalation_reason`) and none was added — an ex-`ignored` escalated row is shape-identical to a normal escalation (classification present, category null; the Escalated tab already renders 115+ of these), so it needs no special-casing. Do NOT gate the escalate action on body-present (unlike Recover) — no reprocessing happens, so a null body is harmless (human still sees from+subject).

- **DEC40 — INBOUND DOOR FOLLOWS THE `inbound_mode` TOGGLE; MAILBOX IS A CAPABILITY/PRECONDITION, NOT THE DECIDER. AMENDS DEC37. LOCKED 2026-08-31 v3 (F23 3b-1).** DEC37 had derived the door from mailbox status, which silently demoted the toggle: a tenant with a connected mailbox who flipped to app_only found the door didn't move (mailbox-presence, not the toggle, picked the door — the toggle became a label). Corrected: **`inbox_only` → mailbox door (requires an ok mailbox as a precondition; no ok mailbox → fail-safe to letterbox so mail is never dropped); `app_only`/null → letterbox door, and any connected mailbox stays connected for writeback but is NOT ingested.** Exactly one door ingests per tenant → DEC36's no-double-reply guarantee is preserved, now enforced by the toggle instead of by mailbox-presence. Two code sites, keyed differently on purpose: `getInboundDoor` (routes a specific inbound; needs a live mailbox → checks mode AND ok-status) vs the `syncTenantMailbox` guard (whether to attempt ingest at all; keyed on MODE only, so an `inbox_only` tenant with a temporarily-broken mailbox keeps retrying and can recover — keying it on `getInboundDoor` would compute `letterbox` and strand it). The `app_only`/`inbox_only` naming is now doubly a slight misnomer (rename still deferred). **Related: when an inbox_only tenant later switches to app_only, mail landing DIRECTLY in their business inbox is silently not captured (the mirror of `letterbox_dormant`) — DEC37's "no mailbox-dormant path" still stands; surfaced only via the 3c forwarding note (forward business email to the inbound address, or switch modes). Promote to a real capture surface only if it bites (see F27 / F22-adjacent).** **REVERSED 2026-10-09 by DEC64 (F78).** A healthy mailbox is read in both modes and decides the door; the toggle is where the tenant manages, not whether the mailbox is read. The DEC40 problem (flipping the toggle did not move the door) cannot recur because the toggle no longer moves the door at all.

## New items

- **F27. NEW 2026-08-31 v3. Promote the app-mode forwarding note to a cross-tab / dashboard banner. Feature. OPEN — PENDING DESIGN DECISION (not decided).** The 3c forwarding note lives inside the Setup tab, so a tenant who works in the Inbox tab (or on the Dashboard) never sees it — and it's the sole in-app flag for a real correctness gap (app-mode tenants silently miss direct business email; DEC40). Idea: lift it above the Agents tab-strip and/or add it to the Dashboard (which already carries the DNS "generic sending" banner). **Deliberately NOT decided — open questions to settle when picked up:** (a) above-tabs-only vs dashboard too; (b) per-surface dismiss vs one shared flag; (c) whether it earns *stacking* with the DNS banner (two amber action-bars trains people to ignore both, including the DNS one that gates sending); (d) it's a refactor not a move — mode state currently lives inside OverviewTab, so lifting above the tabs means hoisting mode state to the AgentsTabs level. Same "build properly if it bites" posture as the DEC37 deferrals. Chunky enough to want design first.

# 2026-09-02 SESSION — F28 (mailbox auto-send parity) + S230 + S217 close + S218 + F23 3b-4 close

## Completed

- **S217. CLOSED 2026-09-02 — not reproduced, dropped from launch gates.** See item entry above (moved-up in-place). Add/refresh/DB all confirmed calendar working on live prod; original demo failure was stale-preview/mis-step.

- **F23 slice 3b-4 (mode-switch teardown). CLOSED 2026-09-02 — discovery verdict = NOOP + DEC41, no build. F23 IS NOW FULLY CLOSED.** Discovery pass (read-only, code + live DB) found an explicit `app_only`↔`inbox_only` switch strands nothing that isn't already accepted: (1) **cursor replay** — switching inbox→app→inbox resumes the mailbox sync from the frozen `tenant_mailboxes.sync_cursor` (via `fetchSinceUid`, uid >= cursor+1), replaying backlog capped ~50/run, but the pipeline's cross-source `message_id` dedup (`isDuplicateMessage`) prevents any double-reply — worst case is wasted replay work, not a double-send; (2) **parked `letterbox_dormant` rows** — only S199 had any (4, 3 unresolved); a switch doesn't auto-clear them but they're visible + manually actionable in Filtered (Recover / Mark handled), not orphaned; (3) **in-flight runs at the flip** — finish under pre-flip semantics, a small race in the same class as the DEC35 residual window already accepted. **REJECTED the one "build" the discovery suggested** (make `syncTenantMailbox` gate on `getInboundDoor` instead of raw mode "so sync and webhook agree") — that would REGRESS DEC40: the asymmetry is deliberate (sync keyed on MODE so a broken inbox_only mailbox keeps retrying and can recover; keying on the door would compute letterbox and strand it). See DEC41 + quirks.

## Decisions locked

- **DEC41 — DEC35 IS DELIVERED (via F28); F23 3b-4 MODE-SWITCH TEARDOWN = ACCEPTED SEMANTICS, NOT A BUILD. LOCKED 2026-09-02.** Two things: **(A)** DEC35's "mailbox auto-send made safe by folder-writeback separation" was assumed built but wasn't — F28 actually wired it (mailbox runs can auto-send; auto-send files to Responded, send-first, non-fatal writeback). DEC35 is now real code, not an assumption. **(B)** An explicit inbound-mode switch requires no teardown build. The mode-keyed sync guard vs door-keyed routing asymmetry is INTENTIONAL per DEC40 (do not "reconcile" them — it strands broken-mailbox recovery). Cursor replay on switch-back is dedup-covered (wasted work only, no double-reply). `letterbox_dormant` rows remain a manual queue by design (auto-cleanup on switch would risk burying a genuine enquiry — the DEC37 "C rejected" principle). In-flight-at-flip is the same accepted residual-window class as DEC35. The send→file "sliver" (F28) and the app_only-direct-mail-not-captured gap (DEC40) both remain "build only if it bites" — F15 Problem B and F27/F22-adjacent respectively.

- **DEC42 — EMAIL TRANSPORT: POSTMARK FOR OWN-DOMAIN, RESEND FOR GENERIC (HYBRID); SES = DOCUMENTED FALLBACK. LOCKED 2026-09-03 (v2).** Own-domain tenant sends + all own-domain verification go through **Postmark** (Platform plan = unlimited sending domains — the reason to pick it; Basic=5/Pro=10 both reproduce the Resend per-domain cap). Generic app-domain sends stay on **Resend** (`hello@send.moversapp.app`, S209), unchanged. Dispatch is keyed on the existing verified-state derivation (`getTenantSendingIdentityStatus`), not a new flag — see `lib/agents/replySender.ts`. **Why not Resend for own-domain:** Resend prices + caps per verified DOMAIN (403 on the plan cap = the S220 leak) — wrong model for multi-tenant. **Why not SES (the runner-up):** ~10× cheaper at high volume, but requires self-built SNS bounce/complaint handling + suppression + reputation monitoring + a sandbox→production-access approval — more infra to run, which cuts against the "less to manage" principle that drove the choice. SES stays the DOCUMENTED FALLBACK if Postmark cost-at-scale ever bites (~$126/mo at 100k emails/1000 tenants vs SES ~$10) — a deliberate cost-vs-build trade, not an oversight; note that switching re-publishes DNS for every verified tenant, so it gets more expensive to switch as tenant count grows (decided now while near-zero real verified tenants exist). **Column:** own-domain provider id is stored in `provider_domain_id` (renamed from `resend_domain_id`, vendor-neutral, survives a future switch). **Consequences/owed:** Postmark account must reach PLATFORM plan + clear manual approval before real sending (S243); bounce/complaint webhook + suppression must be built before heavy sending (S244). Verified live end-to-end this session (both paths). Ties/closes the S221 transport-pending line + folds S220.

- **DEC43 — BILLING: STRIPE SUBSCRIPTIONS AT LAUNCH — 30-DAY FREE TRIAL, CARD UPFRONT, AUTO-CONVERT; READ-ONLY LOCK ON NON-PAYMENT. LOCKED 2026-09-04 (v2).** Reverses the Cand. D "manual-invoice pilot = zero code" assumption — automated billing is IN launch scope from the start. **Shape:** signup → Stripe Checkout (card captured) → 30-day free trial → auto-converts to paid, no re-prompt. Non-payment handled Stripe-native (smart retries + dunning) → tenant goes READ-ONLY after N days overdue (N = config, not code; set at build). **Integration = Stripe hosted Checkout** (pre-built form; lowest build, less PCI surface, native trial support) — NOT Payment Links (manual) or Elements (custom, more build). **Pricing:** THREE tiers **£29 Starter / £44 Pro / £99 Scale**, seat-split **≤4 / 5–10 / ≤20**, target avg ~£40/tenant. Differentiator = SEATS ONLY for launch; what else each tier includes (AI caps, own-domain, SMS) is deferred to F21 and does NOT gate this DEC (price objects don't care). **DB:** new `tenant_billing` one-to-one table, service-role-write-only (DEC25 posture, NOT tenant_agents — an owner-writable billing row would let a tenant self-unlock; caught in F30 slice-1 review). **Why Stripe-at-launch over the pilot:** operator optimises for TIME + zero recurring admin over signup volume — manual invoicing IS the overhead being designed out. **Why card-upfront over no-card trial:** (a) launch is concierge (first ~20 hand-walked) — a personally-set-up tenant doesn't bounce at a card field, so "no-card = more signups" only pays at anonymous self-serve, which launch isn't; (b) every trial user consumes solo-operator support time — card upfront filters tyre-kickers; (c) cheaper build (one flow, Stripe auto-converts) vs no-card's extra lock/chase/prompt path; (d) reversible with data once live testing proves conversion. **Rejected:** manual-invoice pilot (recurring admin), no-card trial at launch (more build + chase + tyre-kicker load). **No-card trial revisited post-live-testing** once the product is proven and launch moves off concierge → self-serve (that's the trigger). **Trial-eligibility (repeat-trial abuse):** trial is currently hardcoded 30d, always — NOT eligibility-checked. Case A (same tenant re-subscribes) → check `tenant_billing` history, set `trial_period_days:0` if used; folds into F30 slice 2 (webhook writes trial state anyway). Case B (same person, new signup/tenant) → the real leak; Stripe Radar free-trial-abuse control (dashboard toggle, free, catches card-reuse) + optional cross-tenant dedup = S251, "build if abuse appears." **Marketing site advertises the trial; the app SERVER decides eligibility at checkout-session creation** (only the app knows tenant_billing history) — keeps eligibility in one trusted place regardless of where the "Start trial" button lives. Where in the funnel checkout fires (signup vs post-onboarding) stays an open placement decision, doesn't gate the eligibility logic. **Operator prereq (S250):** legal entity = **Future Apps LTD** (fresh, not the dormant co — Stripe binds to the entity, switching later ≈ new account); bank = **Monzo Business**; Stripe account live + sandbox products created. Only payout-verification-cleared tail remains. **Reversal cost is LOW** (config + one flow to swap manual↔Stripe or card↔no-card) — this is a DEC because it re-scopes launch and reverses a recorded zero-code assumption, not because it's hard to undo. **Delivered by F30** (slice 1 shipped ce57cb9/PR #88: tenant_billing + Stripe client + checkout action + interim selector + success/cancel, verified live sandbox; slice 2 = webhook/state-sync + trial-eligibility Case A; slice 3 = read-only enforcement).

- **DEC44 — READ-ONLY ENFORCEMENT MODEL: "CAPTURE WITHOUT COGNITION". LOCKED 2026-09-04 (v3).** Defines what a non-paying / cancelled / past-due tenant can and cannot do. The lock protects the LLM-powered flagship value; it does NOT punish the mover's END customer, and it keeps the cheap convenience surfaces open to encourage reactivation. **Fully OPEN under lock (all DB reads/writes, fractions of a penny):** Settings (MUST stay — Billing lives here so they can pay), Vehicles, Jobs core, Calendar (incl. new entries), Dashboard. **BLUR-WALL (a reusable "reactivation wall" — blurred page + centred "Reactivate to keep using" → Billing):** ONE reusable component in the app layout, wraps `/quote` first; Reports (`app/(app)/reports/page.tsx`, IS built) and Video Quote (`/quote?tab=video`) inherit it. **Blur is COSMETIC — the server-side gate is the real lock** (a blurred page still has live data + working buttons underneath; enforcement must be server-side, never the blur). **INBOUND under lock = capture, then gate before cognition:** the customer's email is still received + stored + placed in the Review Queue as a RAW enquiry (zero LLM) — no enquiry silently dropped (the S194 harm), and the raw-queue pile is a visible carrot — but the classify + draft LLM calls are SKIPPED (guard in `pipeline.ts`, after run-row creation, before `computeDraftForInput`). **On reactivation:** raw enquiries stay raw; tenant manually triggers a draft PER item; NO bulk-draft button (200 clicks = human-rate-limited, no token spike; and it's deferred normal spend by a now-paying tenant, not a leak). **Enforcement architecture (discovery: NO single mutation chokepoint — ≥6 side-effect entry points + 7 more LLM action paths, each with its own local membership check):** the near-universal chokepoint is the shared LLM client, so a **`guardLLM(tenant_id)` wrapper at that client** covers ~10 LLM entry points at ONE site and is simultaneously the LLM-half of read-only AND the per-tenant abuse cap (S252). Non-LLM guards remain per-site: auto-send cron, `approveQueueItem`, `editAndSendQueueItem` (all currently on local `requireOwner`, not a shared gate). Shared `isReadOnly(tenant_id)` helper lives in `lib/tenant/active.ts`; `read_only_at` maps from `status` (stamp on past_due/canceled/unpaid, clear on active/trialing) — NOTHING sets `read_only_at` yet, that mapping is slice-3 work. Reactivation wall needs billing state fetched into the app layout (pages don't receive it today). **Sequencing:** the cancel path (slice 2.5, DONE) precedes enforcement — a tenant must be able to exit cleanly before being locked out. **Delivered by F30 slice 3** (3a = isReadOnly + guardLLM + counter + read_only_at mapping; 3b = send/inbound guards + wall + billing-state-into-layout). **Reversal cost LOW** (guards are additive conditions). This is a DEC because it defines the launch billing behaviour + the cross-cutting LLM chokepoint that also carries abuse protection. NOTE: this claims **DEC44**, which S248 had floated as a CANDIDATE — S248's candidate DEC now floats to **DEC47** if still wanted (still a candidate, not locked; **DEC45** + **DEC46** consumed 2026-09-06 — see the 2026-09-06 Decisions).

## New items

- **F29. NEW 2026-09-02. New-tenant auto-send consent/sender gate loops — a fresh tenant CANNOT enable auto-send. LAUNCH-BLOCKING. OPEN.** On a brand-new tenant (Cornwall/Sep Movers), toggling Auto-send on triggers the "Use generic sending address?" consent modal; clicking Accept and enable does NOT clear it — "No sender email configured" persists and the toggle never sticks, an unbreakable loop. `tenant_agents` has NO sender or consent column (confirmed via information_schema); the auto-send gate reads a sending identity / consent that lives in OTHER tables (F19 put consent in `tenant_users.ui_preferences`, sending identity in `tenant_sending_identity`), and a fresh tenant has ZERO rows in those — so accepting generic consent never produces the thing the gate checks. **This is the S201 hole, live and confirmed.** Ties S208 (the from_email/contact_email split-brain is adjacent). **Also blocks F28's auto-fire verification** (can't test mailbox auto-send end-to-end until a tenant can turn auto-send on). Needs its own scoped session: trace the auto-send gate (send-gate + consent-write + sending-identity provisioning for a new tenant) — do NOT scope blind. Likely fix: generic-accept must WRITE the consent/identity the gate reads, and/or a new tenant must be provisioned with a usable default sending identity.

- **S231. NEW 2026-09-02. STANDING RULE (process, not code): JSX/import/hook-touching briefs must verify with `npm run build`, not just tsc+test.** Codex verifies with `tsc --noEmit` + vitest; NEITHER runs ESLint. Vercel's `next build` DOES run ESLint. Three Vercel build failures this session (an unused-import `Link`, and two `react/no-unescaped-entities` raw-apostrophe errors) all passed tsc+vitest and only failed at deploy. Every brief that edits JSX text, imports, or hook dependency arrays MUST end its verification with `npm run build` before push. This is why deploys "kept failing recently" — a verification gap, not bad luck. (Half-logged in quirks from the earlier `Link` incident; promoted to a standing rule after the third hit.)

- **S232. NEW 2026-09-02. Desktop unsaved-changes toast to match mobile. Cosmetic. Folds into the existing save-consistency cluster (see the ~line-1185 design note: instant-save controls vs explicit-save fields on Agents Overview). Not a gate — the glow + mobile toast are considered enough signal for launch.

After F28 this is WRONG — mailbox runs now auto-send when the tenant has auto-send on. Update the copy to reflect auto-send parity.

Non-blocking (warning, not error) and appeared on nearly every build this session. Tidy it so it doesn't become the noise that hides a real warning later — either add the dep or intentionally document the omission.

- S249. **DONE 2026-09-15 (PR #119, merge `c72fce9`). no_depot survey over-offer closed.** **Shipped as HOLD-not-strip**, deliberately different from this item's parked 'strip survey when `distance_status` ∈ {no_postcode, no_depot, lookup_failed}' shape: stripping removes the offer silently, holding keeps the offer, stops the send, tells the tenant why, and preserves manual send. `no_depot` added to the auto-send hold set; review-queue label 'No depot set'. **The structural half:** the auto-send cron and the review queue kept SEPARATE hold-reason lists, which is why this class of bug existed — unified into one shared `surveyDistanceHold()` in `surveyDistanceStatus.ts` that both read. No send-anyway override (a knowing override would keep a distance limit alive while silently not enforcing it; the coherent exits are add a depot, remove the limit, or send by hand). The parked lookup_failed sub-decision resolves as Choice B in effect: all three statuses now hold rather than send. NOT exercised live — no tenant could demonstrate it (S199 Movers has auto-send OFF so everything holds as `auto_send_off`; only S146 Mover has it on) — accepted on branch unit tests + a live-proven pipeline by operator decision. **Full detail archived in movers_app_backlog_archive_APPEND_2026-09-15.md.**

- S252. **NEW 2026-09-04 v3. Per-tenant daily LLM TOKEN counter + guard. DELIVERED 2026-09-06 by F30 slice 3a** (guardLLM + per-tenant daily token counter, `845ad0c`; counter-accumulate fix — atomic `increment_tenant_token_usage()` RPC — PR #93 `0e7268c`; verified live). Design reference retained below.** Circuit breaker against LLM-cost abuse (a trial account has a valid £0-charged card + full access; a bot could smash Quote/inbound/redraft in a day before cancelling). **Count TOKENS not calls** — a token-smash is few calls with giant payloads, which a call-counter misses; `usage.total_tokens` is on every OpenAI response so summing it is ~free. Storage = tiny `tenant_llm_usage` (tenant_id + date + count) or counter column, upserted. **ONE generous ceiling** = N× the heaviest plausible honest day (a breaker, NOT cost-pro-rata — pro-rata is too restrictive, usage varies wildly across tenants; onboarding fires several calls in a row so the ceiling must clear a legit onboarding day). **HARD block, "resets midnight."** Lives in the **`guardLLM(tenant_id)` wrapper at the shared LLM client** (same wrapper as the DEC44 read-only LLM lock — one site covers ~10 LLM entry points). Number = a 5-min cost-model calc at build (ties the £29/£44/£99 + per-tenant cost assumption). Backed by S253 (the account-level hard cap). Fair-use clause goes in ToS (F21).

- S253. **DONE 2026-09-25 v2.** LTD OpenAI account (Future Apps LTD, Monzo card), project "Movers App", service-account key, `OPENAI_API_KEY` swapped in Vercel prod+preview+local, redeployed, F12(a) draft verified, crons `ok` after deploy, no operator alert. Prepaid: $20 initial, auto-reload $5->$20, **Monthly reload limit ON at $120 (the real money ceiling)**, org spend limit $120 with "Enforce a hard limit" ON, alerts at 40/80/100%. Old personal key: revoke after 24h. Original entry: **NEW 2026-09-04 v3. OpenAI account/project HARD spend-cap — OPERATOR task.** Dashboard budget limit + alert at the OpenAI account/project level. Backstops EVERYTHING regardless of app code — trial abuse, bot, runaway bug, our own accidental loops — bounding the worst-case bill to £X/month. ~5 min, independent of any build. This is the "no pattern can bill us beyond the cap" insurance; S252 is the finer per-tenant/fairness layer on top. Both wanted. **REFRAMED + PARKED on the Monzo card 2026-09-07.** Set on the **LTD OpenAI account**, not personal. Current personal-org 30-day spend = **$0.60** (Cornwall Make + all app testing), so a **$120/mo hard cap ≈ 200× honest load** = a fine catastrophe ceiling (trips only on a runaway bug / leaked key, never honest load; set it HIGH — a tight org cap is the only thing that makes the shared-fate DoS below real). **Post-2026 OpenAI change: the $ number alone is ALERTS-ONLY — must toggle "Enforce a hard limit" ON** (org level, covers all projects/keys), + add a spend alert ~$50–80 below. **Now bundles LTD OpenAI provisioning:** create LTD account (LTD email, Monzo LTD card) → project "Movers App" → migrate `OPENAI_API_KEY` (Vercel + local) → re-verify a live Cornwall draft end-to-end → then the cap. BLOCKED on the Monzo **debit card** (the LTD bank account itself is live — that's S250, now closed). **Launch-time design note (DEC candidate):** an org-level hard cap is shared-fate — a coordinated trial-abuser burning many trials to their S252 caps could aggregate to the org ceiling and 429 ALL tenants pending a manual raise. Mitigations: keep the cap HIGH, S252 per-tenant counter, DEC43 card-upfront (traceable/blockable). Structural fix to eliminate shared-fate = per-project caps (one Stripe/OpenAI project per tenant) — heavy plumbing, overkill pre-launch. Revisit at self-serve scale. **AMENDMENT 2026-09-17:** NOT blocked on the card (card arrived). The LTD OpenAI account was closed by OpenAI after repeated failed card attempts. Prod `OPENAI_API_KEY` is on the operator's PERSONAL account, ran dry 2026-09-15 (silent 429s), topped up $10. Path: re-register an LTD OpenAI account -> set hard cap -> migrate key. Still OPEN, still before paying tenants.

- S254. **NEW 2026-09-04 v3. Seat-cap enforcement on plan-switch/downgrade. OPEN.** The Stripe Customer Portal (slice 2.5) lets a tenant self-switch plans and Stripe just swaps the price — **Stripe knows nothing of the seat bands** (Starter ≤4 / Pro 5–10 / Scale ≤20). So a 15-seat tenant could self-downgrade to the £29 ≤4 plan and Stripe won't stop them. **HALF A ✅ DONE 2026-09-07 (main f87ab5d, direct).** Seat-ADD cap: numeric `maxSeats` (starter 4/pro 10/scale 20) + `label` added to `billingTierDetails` (was display-copy only); `inviteMember` blocks a new seat when the tenant is at its tier cap — counts active+pending+invited (role-agnostic, service-role), grandfathers no-billing tenants (null tier → uncapped). Guard sits after the existing-member early-return (re-invite unaffected), before both insert paths. Proven live on S199 (filled to 4 via 3 borrowed mailinator test users → 5th invite blocked with "Your Starter plan allows up to 4 team members…" → filler removed). **HALF B DONE 2026-09-23 (PR #140 `d6f8394`), S254 CLOSED.** Three Stripe portal configurations (ALL / PRO_UP / SCALE_ONLY, ids in `STRIPE_PORTAL_CONFIG_*`) chosen at portal-session creation by live seat count, so a downgrade below the band is not offered; over-band computed at render (invite block, banners, console Trouble panel, webhook warning), nothing stored. Detail in movers_app_backlog_archive_APPEND_2026-09-23.md. Original Half B note follows. *Was:* downgrade-below-seats, WARN-ONLY not cut: a portal downgrade is a fait accompli (Stripe swaps the price instantly); hard-cutting seats would log out real people/owners mid-job. So compute over-seat on read (grace-banner model) + a nudge banner "remove N or upgrade" — no forced removal. Needs `app/api/stripe/webhook/route.ts`. Lower stakes pre-launch (Half A stops growth past the band). **KNOWN LIMITATION (Half A): TOCTOU race** — count-then-insert isn't atomic, two simultaneous invites could both pass at cap-1. Human-rate/near-zero concurrency pre-launch; harden with a DB-level check only if it ever matters. (Codex flagged it correctly rather than silently "fixing".)

- S255. **NEW 2026-09-04 v3. Non-current tier cards → "Switch to X" via portal. OPEN — UX polish.** In slice 2.6, when a tenant is subscribed the non-current tier cards render as dead cards that still hover-highlight but have no action (deliberate — the double-billing guard removed their checkout buttons). Confusing. Turn them into working upgrade/downgrade affordances ("Switch to Pro →") that route to the Customer Portal (already configured for plan-switch + proration). Pairs with S254 (seat-cap check on the switch). Tier-3 polish.

- S256. **NEW 2026-09-04 v3. Verify webhook WRITES `cancel_at_period_end` on a real cancel event. ✅ DONE 2026-09-07 — proven live. S199 Stripe portal "Don't cancel subscription" → `customer.subscription.updated` webhook → `cancel_at_period_end` flipped true→false in the DB (`updated_at` matched the click, not MCP-set). Webhook WRITE of this field confirmed, and confirmed reaching a live endpoint.** Slice 2.6's cancel-ending banner was proven DISPLAY-ONLY: S199 was cancelled earlier in the session before the `cancel_at_period_end` column existed, so the webhook that synced that cancel had nothing to write there (`updated_at` predates the migration); the flag was set directly via MCP to prove the banner renders. Low risk (one field added to an upsert seen working 3×) but the webhook WRITE of this specific field is unproven. Re-cancel S199 fresh (renew → cancel) OR verify on the first real tenant cancel — the `customer.subscription.updated` event should set the flag true.

The drift is worse than "ledger rows missing": LIVE DB FUNCTIONS CAN SILENTLY DIVERGE FROM COMMITTED REPO FILES.** S230 proved it — the repo's 10 July migration contained the `seed_tenant_defaults` call, but the live `create_tenant_with_owner` did not (an out-of-band CREATE OR REPLACE overwrote it). This session added orphan #~25 (S230 seed restore: file committed + run via MCP, no ledger row). The working apply-process remains "commit the .sql + run it live via MCP `execute_sql`"; it does NOT write a schema_migrations row unless done manually. # 2026-09-06 SESSION — F30 SLICE 3 BUILD RUN (3a + counter fix + 3b-1 + grace fix + 3b-2a + 3b-2b SHIPPED; 3b-2c remains)

## Completed
- **F30 slice 3a — DONE (`845ad0c`, verified live).** Per-tenant daily LLM token cap + counter + `guardLLM` wrapper at the shared LLM client — the DEC44 chokepoint that is simultaneously the LLM-half of read-only enforcement AND the abuse cap, at one site (~10 LLM entry points). **Delivers S252.**
- **F30 counter-accumulate fix — DONE (PR #93, `0e7268c`, verified live).** Replaced the overwrite upsert with an atomic `increment_tenant_token_usage()` RPC (a DB-side function) so concurrent LLM calls accumulate the daily count instead of clobbering each other.
- **F30 slice 3b-1 — DONE (`3ffeb19`, ⚠ pushed direct to main).** Backend read-only enforcement — capture-without-cognition (inbound stored as a raw enquiry, classify/draft LLM calls skipped under lock) + send guards. Superseded/completed by the grace fix below.
- **F30 read-only grace fix — DONE (PR #94, `0496347`, verified live).** `reason` CHECK constraint + 7-day past_due grace window, computed on read. See DEC45.
- **F30 slice 3b-2a — DONE (PR #95, `b0f6cdf`, verified on preview).** Billing state fetched into the app layout + grace banner + countdown.
- **F30 slice 3b-2b — DONE (PR #96, `61c245f`, verified on preview).** Locked-state banner.

## Decisions locked
- **DEC45 — READ-ONLY GRACE MODEL. REFINES DEC44. LOCKED 2026-09-06 — implemented + verified live.** The read-only lock is **computed on read** from `tenant_billing.status` + `grace_started_at`, NOT stored: **`active`/`trialing`** → not locked; **`past_due`** → 7-day grace (`GRACE_PERIOD_DAYS`) from the first past_due, locked only once that expires; **`canceled`/`unpaid`** → locked immediately; **no billing row** → not locked (grandfathers Cornwall / pre-billing tenants). **Rationale:** locking on the first failed payment (past_due) is too aggressive — an innocent card glitch would cut service instantly; the grace window gives the dunning period an app-wide countdown banner ("resolve within N days"), so the lock is always warned, never silent. **Compute-on-read chosen over a cron** because nothing fires an event at the 7-day mark. The `read_only_at` column is retained but is **no longer authoritative** (DEC44 had it mapping from status; grace supersedes that as the lock source of truth).
- **DEC46 — config_change_log IMPLEMENTATION. CANDIDATE — decision pending build, NOT locked. Logged 2026-09-06.** Investigation finding: config writes are **scattered** (25+ sites across ~6 files, no shared write helper; all go through server actions so auth is clean, but there is no single logging chokepoint) — so config_change_log is **not** a cheap freebie. **Pure DB triggers** = reliable capture but **cannot** populate `source` (manager/manual/generator), `reason`, or `conversation_id` — the fields that make it the Manager's foundation → trigger-only = a generic audit log only. **Hybrid** (triggers for guaranteed capture + transaction-local session vars / GUCs [Postgres per-session config variables] set in the write path to supply source/reason/conversation_id) = the real Manager-foundation version. Service-role writes mean `auth.uid()` is likely NULL inside a trigger, so even "which user" needs app-supplied context. **Recommendation:** if built for the Manager (F6), do the **hybrid**, or don't start — pure-trigger has the cost without the strategic payoff. **Timing:** top of F6, unless wired during launch.

# 2026-09-07 v2 SESSION — F21 MARKETING SITE (DESIGN PHASE)

Feature content grounded in launch-BUILT features only (roadmap items — customer portal, SMS, call transcription, Open Banking, photo→inventory — deliberately left OFF to avoid overselling). Files in `/mnt/user-data/outputs` (session scratch) → operator to download + upload into the Project.

## Decisions locked
- **DEC47 — MARKETING SITE ARCHITECTURE: SEPARATE SITE ON THE APEX; APP ON A SUBDOMAIN. LOCKED 2026-09-07 v2.** The marketing site is built + deployed SEPARATELY from the Next.js app (not same-repo route-group), served on the apex `moversapp.app`; the app moves to a subdomain (`my.moversapp.app` RECOMMENDED — short, "my-product" convention, avoids "app.moversapp.app" triple-app — NOT yet locked). **Why separate:** a copy tweak shouldn't redeploy (or risk) the app; the split matches the apex/subdomain layout; marketing can be near-static so the mockups are ~the site → fastest to live. Same-repo route-group would couple deploys and fight the split. **OPEN sub-decision — the site PLATFORM/editability:** static-via-Codex now (fast, unblocks launch, but copy edits go through Codex — no non-dev editor) vs a visual builder (Framer/Webflow — self-editing from day one, custom domain, but the design gets rebuilt in-tool, a separate platform + monthly cost, and Codex can't build it). Operator UNSURE — wants "just get it up" AND "edit pages myself later," which pull opposite ways. Leaning static-via-Codex now, migrate to Framer later IF self-editing becomes a priority. NOT locked → part of DEC48-adjacent open work; resolve before the F21 build brief.

## Candidate decisions (NOT locked)
- **DEC48 — OPERATOR CROSS-TENANT CONSOLE: LAUNCH-SLICE vs POST-LAUNCH. LOCKED 2026-09-18 (was CANDIDATE, logged 2026-09-07 v2).** **Decision: a read-only operator console slice ships BEFORE launch, and it has shipped (F31 PR 1 + PR 1b, `/admin`).** Rules locked with it: (1) the gate is a fail-closed allowlist of Supabase user ids in `OPERATOR_USER_IDS` (unset = nobody), non-operators get 404, logged-out users go to `/login`; (2) the gate is enforced in the `/admin` layout AND as the first line of every console data function and server action, never layout-only; (3) no `/api/admin/*` routes, because middleware passes `/api/` through with no auth gate; (4) reads use the service-role client and reuse the existing per-tenant computations (billing state, tier, sending identity, system health) so the console cannot disagree with what the tenant sees; (5) v1 shows no customer content (no enquiry bodies, draft bodies, customer names or addresses), with one exception: the assembled prompt view, collapsed by default, because worked examples are tenant-written evidence; (6) write surfaces arrive one at a time behind the proven gate: Prompts tab (PR 2b), then operator edits of tenant descriptions (PR 3) which must go through the same save path the tenant UI uses and must log before / after / who / reason. Original candidate text follows. F31 (below) is a real backlog gap distinct from F2. The question this DEC will settle: does a thin read-only operator-visibility slice ship AT launch, or does the console wait until just-after? Today the DB/SQL (via Supabase MCP) IS the operator console and holds at concierge scale (a handful of hand-walked tenants), so it is NOT a hard launch gate — but running everything through raw SQL gets slow and risky (one bad cross-tenant UPDATE) the moment stranger tenants sign up. Decide the MINIMUM operator-visibility slice needed to run real tenants without living in SQL. Not locked — pending the operator's call.

CANDIDATE, logged 2026-09-09 (v2).** DEC43 locked card-upfront + 30-day trial but explicitly PARKED the funnel placement: whether in-app Stripe Checkout fires right after signup (+ email verify) or after onboarding. Never resolved; recorded nowhere until now. Not a regression that it is missing today: the marketing CTA lands on a bare `my.moversapp.app/signup` (no tier carried, no auto-advance into checkout) because slice 3 is unbuilt, and DEC45 grandfathers a no-billing-row tenant as not-locked, so a fresh signup can use the app with no card. ## New items
- **S260. NEW 2026-09-07 v2. Privacy Policy + Terms of Service — solicitor review + fill [bracketed] fields. OPEN. Gates FUNCTIONAL launch.** The drafts are best-practice (UK GDPR / Data (Use and Access) Act 2025 / AI-SaaS), NOT legal advice. Before publish: a UK SaaS/data-protection solicitor reviews both, and every `[bracketed]` field is filled — ICO registration number (+ actually register + pay the fee at ico.org.uk), Future Apps LTD company number + registered office, the real sub-processor list with regions, retention periods, contact emails, DPA link. Also VERIFY (or delete) two claims that must match the actual OpenAI API config: the "we don't train on your data / zero-retention" line and any encryption-at-rest / certification claim. An informational page can go up without this; taking real signups (card + PII) cannot. The privacy policy also gates the Google restricted-scope OAuth track.
- **S261. NEW 2026-09-07 v2. Tenant-facing Data Processing Agreement (DPA). OPEN. Required before real stranger tenants.** The privacy policy references a DPA we don't have. Tenants (removals firms) are controllers of THEIR customers' personal data; we process it on their behalf, so under UK GDPR Art 28 they legally need a DPA from us as their processor. Separate legal doc from Privacy/Terms. Not blocking the marketing site going up, but required before onboarding real stranger tenants for pay. Solicitor-drafted or a reviewed template; names our own sub-processors (OpenAI, Supabase, Resend, Stripe, Google) with the general-authorisation + object-on-change mechanism.

## Completed
- F14, F54, F35, S248, S267, S300, S301, S302, S303, S304, S305, S306, S307, S308, S309, S310, S311, S158, S208, S77, S201 (check only), S165. Detail in the archive append and the session summary.

## Decisions locked
- **DEC55 — PUSH RECIPIENTS RESOLVE BY TENANT MEMBERSHIP; A SUBSCRIPTION IS A DEVICE. LOCKED 2026-09-17.** `push_subscriptions` rows are keyed on the browser endpoint (one row per device) and record the user who last pressed Enable on that device; `tenant_id` on the row is informational only. Event fan-out = `tenant_users` where role in (owner, admin) and status = active, then every subscription for those users. Why: an operator or multi-tenant owner subscribes once per device and must receive pushes from every tenant they own; filtering by the subscription's tenant silently dropped pushes (observed 2026-09-17 morning). Corollary (original 2026-09-17): the Notifications card re-POSTs the existing browser subscription on mount so the row follows the logged-in user. **AMENDED 2026-10-02 v3 (S441 + S444):** that corollary was the only re-key path and it was not enough; a device whose row still named the previous login kept receiving that login's pushes until Settings was opened (observed 2026-10-01). Now: (a) logout deletes this device's row before `signOut`; (b) the authenticated shell (`PushRegistrationSync` in `app/(app)/layout.tsx`) re-POSTs the browser subscription on every mount, no sessionStorage guard, because a remote sign-out never runs local cleanup; (c) `/api/push/subscribe` DELETE matches endpoint only, so logout-as-B clears a row that still says A; (d) "Sign out of all devices" deletes every row for the user before the global sign-out, because a push row is not tied to a session. `tenant_id` on the row stays informational. Crew excluded from all four triggers.

## Decisions locked
- **DEC48 LOCKED** (see entry): read-only operator console slice ships before launch; console rules.
- **DEC23 AMENDED** (see entry): code default plus operator override layer, spine included; draft, test, publish, history, revert.
- **DEC56. LOCKED 2026-09-18. Per-tenant prompt customisation is ADDITIVE, never a fork.** There is one master prompt per key. A tenant never gets a copy of it. When a tenant needs something the existing per-tenant levers cannot express, the operator writes a tenant note that is injected into a marked slot of the master for that tenant only (empty slot = nothing added, prompt byte-identical). Master improvements therefore reach every tenant, including tenants with notes. **Rationale:** forks drift; every master fix would have to be hand-merged into every fork, and forked tenants silently miss fixes. This is the same failure DEC23 removed on 2026-08-04 (per-tenant stored prompts). **Tool order for a "my drafts are wrong" ticket, mildest first:** (1) fix the enquiry-type description, (2) add a worked example (STEP 0 of the spine outranks every other step), (3) tenant note, (4) change the master (affects everyone). Notes are operator-only and never tenant-visible (DEC1 / DEC12). Same draft, test, publish, history, revert workflow, scoped per tenant. `prompt_versions.tenant_id` / `prompt_publications.tenant_id` are nullable for exactly this; tenant-scoped rows are not resolved until F31 PR 2d. True forks stay possible on the same tables if a real case appears (for example a tenant in a different trade) but are not built.
- **DEC57. LOCKED 2026-09-18. Retry schedule, stranded runs, operator alarm policy (S318).** (a) Every thrown LLM error is classified to a cause: quota, auth, model_config, rate_limit, provider_down, timeout, token_cap, other. Quota is recognised by provider code, type OR message wording (the real 2026-09-15 message was "429 You have no credits remaining", not the classic insufficient_quota shape). (b) quota, auth and model_config bucket as `our_end`; rate_limit, provider_down and timeout stay `transient`; token_cap stays `token_cap`. (c) LONG retry schedule for every cause except other: by run age, every 10 minutes under 30 minutes, every 30 minutes under 3 hours, hourly under 24 hours, with a 1 minute tolerance for cron jitter; no count cap. SHORT schedule for other / null cause: existing behaviour, cap 5. (d) An exhausted run (long schedule at 24h, or short schedule at the cap) is marked `stranded_at` once, gets a support report if none exists, and raises one alarm. (e) Operator alarms go through ONE throttled sender: at most one email per cause per hour, the rest counted in `operator_alert_throttle`; a failed send releases the slot; alarm code never throws into the pipeline. Alarms fire at the single LLM call point for quota / auth / model_config, from the pipeline for `our_end`, and for `run_stranded`. (f) The auto-replay candidate query excludes stranded and just-retried runs IN SQL and orders least-recently-tried first, so a backlog can never starve due runs. Manual Retry by the tenant is unchanged and always allowed. Not built, deliberately: circuit breaker, changes to the SDK's own retries.

## New items
- **S315. NEW 2026-09-18. Make it obvious that enquiry-type descriptions drive classification. OPEN. Pre-launch or soon after.** Descriptions are seeded from the library, the tenant never wrote them, and nothing in the UI says they are live conditions the AI matches against. A stranger tenant either never touches them (and misses the self-serve fix) or tidies them (deleting a "Not X" exclusion clause raises escalations). **Scope:** copy on the field saying what it does ("This decides which emails get this reply"); teach the IS / IS NOT pattern; say that narrowing a description means more emails come to you for review, never silence; link to the test panel ("Changed it? Test it here"); support triage line: wrong type = description, wrong wording = template, wrong survey offer = Rules. **Step 0 investigation, partly answered this session from a real assembled prompt:** (a) a description is substituted verbatim as the condition of one decision-tree step ("if the enquiry matches: <description>"); exclusions inside it are honoured by the multi-match check by design; what order-style text ("do not respond if...") does is still untested, test on Testing Mover with the F12(a) panel, never on S199; (b) sensitivity keywords are a case-insensitive SUBSTRING match, both in the prompt text and in a code-side matcher, so "pet" trips on "carpet"; (c) a NULL description falls back to the bare label ("Storage.") as the condition (see S153); (d) STEP 0 of the spine refers to "WORKED EXAMPLES above" even when there are none, tidy at the next spine change; (e) descriptions have no length cap or sanitisation and go straight into the prompt; (f) is a tenant-facing "never auto-reply about X" slot a real gap.
- **S319. NEW 2026-09-18. `audit_log` table does not exist. OPEN. Small.** The tenant-switch route writes to `audit_log`, which exists in no schema on the live DB and has no CREATE TABLE migration. Switching works, so the failure is being swallowed. Either create the table or remove the write. Same silent-swallow family as S191. Related: DEC46 `config_change_log` is confirmed not built; F31 PR 3 creates its first slice.
- **S321. NEW 2026-09-18. Regenerate `lib/supabase/database.types.ts`. OPEN.** Known stale for `tenant_billing` (`grace_started_at` missing, `read_only_at` legacy); it has been hand-synced additively by several PRs. Do it alone: a full regeneration risks a wide diff and new type errors.
- **S322. NEW 2026-09-18. `server-only` guard on the service-role client. OPEN.** `lib/supabase/admin.ts` is protected only by a comment. The `server-only` package throws when imported outside the Next server, and the unit test runner and repo scripts import that file, so the guard needs a test-runner alias / mock designed in. If adding it breaks the production build, that means the key is reachable from browser code today: stop and report.
- **S323. NEW 2026-09-18. No quote route set = inventory ask silently dropped. OPEN. Product call. Extends S235 / S143.** S143 deliberately made the no-route fallback empty. Consequence seen live on Testing Mover: the `item_list` essential-info field is tied to the "list" route, the run resolves no route (`quote_routes` null), so the furniture-list ask vanishes from the reply AND the test panel reports "Missing essential info: None", while the reply still says "we'll work out a price". Extraction itself correctly marks the list as missing. Any tenant with no route set sends removals replies that never ask for the one thing needed to quote, and nothing warns them. Decide the default (fall back to asking for the list, or block / warn). Compounds the config-reachability gap (S163 / S134 / S175 + F10 2e): routes cannot be set after onboarding without SQL.
- **S324. NEW 2026-09-18. CI does not gate on unit tests. OPEN. Decision + small.** `main` carried a failing test (the S309 sweeper mock) for a day and nothing went red. Fixed in S318; suite is now fully green (407 at PR 2a). Decide whether CI should run and block on the unit suite.
- **S325. NEW 2026-09-18. Operator console polish. OPEN.** Trouble view has no table for manual send failures or stuck runs, so a tenant's problem count can exceed the rows shown (S146 showed 3 vs 2); highlight stranded runs; show `operator_alert_throttle` (last alarm per cause, suppressed count); stranded alarm email labels its detail "Provider message:", should say "Detail:"; old failed sends predate S248 and show no code; HTML-derived text uses a single newline between paragraphs (cosmetic).
- **S326. NEW 2026-09-18. Dedicated operator login + 2FA. OPEN. Before stranger tenants' data is in the console.** Operator access currently attaches to `richjstowell@gmail.com` (user id `21858b8d-9c42-4f9c-b8ba-4138f77631c1`), the daily-driver login. Env var is a comma-separated list, swap takes two minutes.

# 2026-09-29 SESSION: THE SUPPORT SURFACE (F64 slices A to D, S389)

## Completed 2026-09-29
- F64 slice A (PR #146 `097ab28`), slice B (PR #147 `dfc39b2`), slice C (PR #148 `3ab8b12`), slice D (PR #149 `c92ebae`), S389 (`16cee4b`). AGENTS.md added (`da89737`). All verified on preview/prod by the operator (Testing Mover `16e64782-f2ee-4cd9-b3c9-7426b2640750` and S199 Movers `8f31127f-50da-4d6b-9513-a983183bac57`); auto-close verified with SQL backdates from the chat. Bodies in movers_app_backlog_archive_APPEND_2026-09-29.md.

## Decisions (operational, not DEC-numbered)
- **Support surface = articles + assistant + tickets. No live human chat.** The "AI helper that knows the page" is F6 phase 1 from a global launcher, grounded in the articles. One assistant identity (2026-07-22 lock) holds; no separate help bot. Handoff chain: article -> assistant -> ticket.
- **Tickets are their own table.** `support_tickets` + `support_ticket_messages`, not `support_reports` (machine-raised, keyed on `agent_run_id`).
- **Internal notes are messages**, not a mutable field: append-only, `is_internal`, hidden from tenants by RLS and column grants, never in any email.
- **Category and severity stay separate.** Category = what kind of help; severity (tenant-set, default Minor) = how fast. "Something is broken" at Minor is legitimate.
- **Reply by app, not by email.** Notification emails carry the thread for context and say replies are not read. No inbound email parsing.
- **Status follows the conversation.** Operator reply -> replied; tenant message -> open; any non-internal message on closed reopens; Close/Reopen manual on both sides; auto-close at day 7 after two reminders; `closed_reason` records who.
- **Notifications are fire-once.** Email and push results stored on the message (`email_sent_at`/`email_error`, `push_sent_at`/`push_error`); email has a Resend button, push does not; reminders are only logged.
- **Unread = raiser only.** A teammate opening a thread does not clear the raiser's banner or badge.
- **Banner colours mean something.** Red = broken, amber = action needed on setup, blue = information. Blue = the existing Open-pill tokens; tenant theming does not touch status colours.
- **Nav badge: no timer.** Fetch on navigation, thread open, and tab focus/visibility with a 5-second gap stamped at attempt start.
- **Repo rules file.** AGENTS.md (Codex) carries: harness cleanup + clean typecheck before every commit (a non-clean typecheck is a stop); rename MCP-applied migration files to the recorded version; no em/en dashes in copy; no merges, migrations or pushes to main without an explicit instruction.

## New items 2026-09-29
- **F65. DONE 2026-09-29 v2** (PR #150 `72ffebf`, PR #151 `1b0b7d1`). Body archived in movers_app_backlog_archive_APPEND_2026-09-29_v2.md; current shape under Amendments 2026-09-29 v2.
- **S390. NEW 2026-09-29. Billing-locked tenants cannot reach `/support`. OPEN. Small.** `isBillingBlocked` holds them at the checkout page, and they are exactly the ones who need a billing ticket. Let `/support` (and `/api/support/*`, the thread actions) through the billing gate, or put a "raise a ticket" link on the checkout hold page. Before real paying strangers.
- **S391. NEW 2026-09-29. Support emails: HTML template. OPEN. Small.** All support mail is plain text through the admin sender: `earlier_messages:` renders as a field with a second "Earlier messages" heading in admin mail; tenant mail is bare; Gmail folds same-subject replies into one conversation so a second reply looks missing. One HTML layout for reply / reminder / close / admin notice with the new message boxed and earlier messages quoted, and a per-ticket `Message-ID`/`References` chain or a suffix in the subject so replies do not hide inside each other.

Not a truncation problem, a priority problem: show the highest-priority bar plus "N more" that expands. Needs a priority order across every bar (red > amber > blue) and a look at dismiss persistence per bar (only the sending-identity bar persists a dismiss today). S389 aligned the layout; this is the next step.

Vercel project -> Settings -> Deployment Protection -> generate the bypass secret; send it as `x-vercel-protection-bypass`. Until then crons are tested on production after merge.

# 2026-10-02 v2 SESSION: F69 DOMAIN CONNECT AUTO DNS (design, build, template submission)

## Completed 2026-10-02 v2
- **F69 DONE** (PR #161 squash `88757cd`, branch `codex/f69-domain-connect`, no migration, 37 new unit tests, typecheck + lint clean). Full body in movers_app_backlog_archive_APPEND_2026-10-02_v2.md. Upstream template merged: Domain-Connect/Templates PR #2108, 12:37 UTC, maintainer pawel-kow, every bot check green (schema, lint, filename, PR description, 2 editor links, template coverage).

## Decisions locked
- **DEC61. LOCKED 2026-10-02 v2. Domain Connect signed template is the automatic-DNS route for the sending identity.** The tenant clicks "Set up automatically with <provider>", is sent to their DNS provider's Domain Connect consent page (same tab, OAuth-style round trip, never a new tab because the provider redirects back to our callback), logs in there, approves, the provider writes our Postmark records, our callback re-verifies. **Rejected alternatives:** Entri (the commercial widget; $249/mo starter for 600 connections/yr, built for high-signup SaaS); a browser extension driving the registrar UI with an LLM (edit-the-wrong-record blast radius on a tenant's live website/email, Chrome Web Store review per update, no mobile, 70-85% reliability still needing a human). **Shape:** template signed (RS256, `syncPubKeyDomain` moversapp.app, key id `_dck1`, public key as two TXT records in the spec's `p=1,a=RS256,d=` format), `syncRedirectDomain` my.moversapp.app, records `%dkimselector%._domainkey` TXT `k=rsa; p=%dkimkey%` + `pm-bounces` CNAME `pm.mtasv.net`, modelled on the merged wavelinker.io Postmark template. Button appears only when: identity unverified, snapshot is Postmark-shaped (old Resend snapshots such as S146 get nothing), discovery finds a provider that returns 200 for our template, both signing env vars present. Manual records, F20 guide and the rest of the help stack (S437-S440) remain as fallbacks. **Rationale:** open standard, free, IETF draft, supported by IONOS, GoDaddy, Cloudflare, Plesk, WordPress.com, NameSilo; the DNS step is the visible stall point for non-technical movers and the mailbox step is the real one (F15 adapters 2 and 3, Gmail/Microsoft OAuth, are the mailbox-side equivalent of this decision).

## New items 2026-10-02 v2
- **S433. NEW 2026-10-02 v2. F69 follow-through once providers ingest the template. OPEN. Do ASAP when the button appears; first real test of the signature.** Day 0 after the upstream merge IONOS, Cloudflare and GoDaddy all returned 404 for `providers/moversapp.app/services/email-sending`. Steps: (1) watch for pickup: prod Sending identity card on Testing Mover (`mail.richardstowell.com`, IONOS zone) shows "Set up automatically with IONOS" with no deploy needed (discovery caches 10 min); or have Codex GET the template-support URL at `api.domainconnect.ionos.com`. (2) Real run on Production: click, log in at IONOS, approve, callback lands on the card with the success message, Check verification goes green, `verified_at` set. (3) Remove `DOMAIN_CONNECT_SKIP_TEMPLATE_CHECK` from Vercel Preview so preview behaves like prod. (4) If Cloudflare still 404s after IONOS is live, follow Cloudflare's Domain Connect template onboarding page (developers.cloudflare.com, DNS reference, Domain Connect) to request enablement; GoDaddy: wait. (5) Record the outcome in quirks + session summary. If the signature is rejected, the fix is in `lib/domain-connect/signing.ts` against the spec's signing section; the public key at `_dck1.moversapp.app` has already been verified against the private key by `scripts/domain-connect-keygen.ts --check`. **AMENDED 2026-10-09: pickup is not automatic.** Probe on 10-08: our template 404 at IONOS, Cloudflare and GoDaddy six days after merge, and a sample of 35 templates merged upstream May to October is served by none of them. domainconnect.org says service providers must contact each DNS provider. Onboarding emails sent 10-08 from admin@moversapp.app to domain_connect_admin@domain.ionos.com, domain-connect@cloudflare.com (with the SVG logo, `_dck1.moversapp.app`, CNAME DNS-only, `dc-template-linter -cloudflare` passes with two info notes) and domainconnect@godaddy.com. Steps (1) and (4) are superseded by the emails; (2), (3), (5) stand once a provider confirms.
- **S434. NEW 2026-10-02 v2. Domain Connect callback lands on the active company, not the tenant in `state`. OPEN. Small, edge.** The callback verifies the signed-in user can edit settings for the tenant named in `state` and re-verifies that tenant, but the redirect lands on whichever company is currently active in the app. If the user switched companies mid-flow the success message shows against the wrong company. Fix: set the active-tenant cookie to the `state` tenant before redirecting (reuse the switcher's set path), or show the tenant name in the message. Comment left in `route.ts` (`0b5df19`).
- **S435. NEW 2026-10-02 v2. iOS PWA: Domain Connect round trip lands in Safari, not the app. OPEN. Small.** An installed iOS PWA hands external links to Safari, which does not share login cookies with the PWA. IONOS approval still writes the records, but the return to `/api/sending-identity/domain-connect/callback` hits the auth check unauthenticated and redirects to login. Fix: when the callback has a valid `state` but no session, run no re-verify and render a plain "Records added at <provider>. Go back to the Movers App and tap Check verification." page instead of the login redirect. Android/Chrome in-app browser sheet is fine.
- **S436. NEW 2026-10-02 v2. GitHub organisation for the LTD; move the movers-app repo and the Templates fork into it. OPEN. Housekeeping.** Everything on GitHub is under the operator's personal account; the Domain Connect template PR #2108 was opened from it (contact in the PR body is admin@moversapp.app, so the business identity is carried). Create a Future Apps LTD org, transfer `movers-app-2` (Vercel re-link, Codex remotes), transfer or re-fork `Templates`. Not urgent.
- **S437. NEW 2026-10-02 v2. "Search how to do this at <registrar>" link + registrar hint on the Sending identity card and DNS guide. OPEN. Small. Folds S215 (registrar auto-detect, 08-26, archived).** Layer 3 of the DNS help stack. Text box for the registrar name (free text), prefilled search URL per record type the tenant actually has ("how to add a TXT record on IONOS", "how to add a CNAME record on IONOS"), opens in the device's default browser, zero maintenance, covers the long tail beyond F20's ten registrars. Pair with S215's nameserver lookup reframed as a hint only ("Looks like IONOS, change if wrong") next to the box, never a hard pre-select, which removes the wrong-guess risk that deferred S215. Operator pushed for this over per-registrar videos (rejected: stale within months). **AMENDED 2026-10-09:** the nameserver hint is DONE inside F75 as the DNS-host line ("Your DNS is managed at X", never a registrar guess). S437 keeps only the search link.
- **S438. NEW 2026-10-02 v2. "Send this to your web person" button, DNS and mailbox variants. OPEN. Small.** Layer 4. # 2026-10-02 v3 SESSION: PUSH LIFECYCLE (S441) + LOGOUT SCOPE (S444)

## Completed 2026-10-02 v3
- S441, S444. Detail in movers_app_backlog_archive_APPEND_2026-10-02_v3.md and the 2026-10-02 v3 session summary.

## Decisions 2026-10-02 v3
- **DEC55 AMENDED** (see the DEC55 entry above). No new decision locked.

## New items 2026-10-02 v3
- **S442. NEW 2026-10-02 v3. Tenant name in the push payload. OPEN. Small.** Multi-tenant owners (the operator today, any franchise or group later) receive pushes from every tenant they own on one device (DEC55) and cannot tell which business an item belongs to. Add the tenant name to the title or body line 1 of all four triggers (`lib/push/send.ts` body builder, 14 unit tests to extend). iOS adds "from MoversApp" itself, so never the app name. Keep under the existing 50-char excerpt budget.
- **S443. NEW 2026-10-02 v3. Deep link when the notification's tenant is not the active tenant. OPEN. Small, multi-tenant only.** `notificationclick` focuses or opens `data.url` (a review-queue or escalation row). If the device's active company is a different tenant, the target row is not visible (unknown whether that is a 404, an empty inbox, or a redirect; check first). Fix: carry `tenant_id` in the payload and set the active-tenant cookie to it before routing (reuse the switcher's set path, same shape as S434). The operator will hit this on richjstowell@gmail.com (owner of Testing Mover + S199 Movers).
- **S445. NEW 2026-10-02 v3. Rename `push_subscriptions.last_used_at` to `last_registered_at`. OPEN. Low. Migration by hand.** Discovery under S441: only the subscribe POST writes it; `send.ts` never does. With the shell re-POSTing on every app open (S441 + S444 follow-up) it now means "last time the app was opened on this device with push enabled". Rename so nobody reads it as delivery evidence; if delivery evidence is ever wanted, add a separate `last_sent_at` written by `send.ts`.
- **S446. NEW 2026-10-02 v3. Offline logout hangs on "Logging out" with no exit. OPEN. Low. Pre-existing.** Found while trying S441 verification step 6: with wifi off, Sign out spins until the network returns, then reloads the app still logged in. Supabase `signOut` needs the server and `/login` needs the network, so offline logout cannot complete; it should fail fast with "You are offline, try again when connected" instead of spinning. S441's own DELETE step already caps at 1.5 s and is not the cause.
- **S447. NEW 2026-10-02 v3. F57 customer-PDF snapshot test fails on main. OPEN. Low but noisy: every full-suite run reports 1 failure.** Pre-existing before S441; confirmed unrelated by two sessions (F69 and S441 both noted it). Refresh the snapshot after checking the diff is intentional (likely the S341-S344 / VAT / override work changed the rendered PDF). Clean-main baseline is currently "N pass, 1 fail (F57)" plus the two intermittent timing failures noted in quirks section 21; make it 0.

## Amendments to existing entries 2026-10-02 v3
- **S314 (F14 leftovers) note:** nothing changed in `sw.js` this session; `getRegistration()` is now used client-side instead of `serviceWorker.ready` because `ready` never resolves on a browser with no service worker (would have hung every desktop logout for the full 1.5 s cap).
- **Working rules, verification (process):** a verification step that depends on being offline cannot include an auth round trip; design the offline case as "server call fails" and prove recovery on the next online load instead (this session's step 6 was unrunnable as written and was replaced by step 3 + unit tests).
- **Working rules, Codex deviations (process):** two Codex deviations from the S441 brief were accepted on report (`getRegistration` over `ready`; Disable swallows a DELETE failure and unsubscribes the browser anyway, leaving the dead endpoint to be cleaned by the send path's 404/410 handling). Both were improvements. One Codex addition (clearing sessionStorage on logout) turned out to be the wrong layer and was removed by the S444 follow-up: a client-side "already did this" marker cannot see server-side session changes.
- **Test data:** rjstowell@hotmail.co.uk accepted its pending crew invite on Testing Mover during S441 step 3 (login routed through `/profile/complete`). It is now active crew there as well as owner of Cornwall Movers Ltd; it has a company switcher. Left in place by choice.

## Decisions locked
- **DEC62. LOCKED 2026-10-06. Customer email and job calendar source of truth.** (1) A customer email leaves the app only via an explicit tenant action: a button, a modal Yes, or a named per-automation toggle the tenant switched on in settings. Never from a drag, an edit or a save alone. "Always do this" remember-choices that would turn a gesture into an email are not offered (the F70 drag remember-choice was cut for this reason). (2) Job calendar events are a projection of the card. Every control on a job event (Deposit received, Undo, Change date, Delete = Cancel job) acts on the card and the event redraws from the card's list and dates; the event's own icon, date and status are never edited directly. Future automations (move-day reminders, deposit chasers, review requests) inherit both rules. Origin: F70 design chat 2026-10-02.

## New items 2026-10-06
- **S450. NEW 2026-10-06. Jobs page tour + first-time explainers. OPEN. Medium. Build after F70 settles.** Two layers: (a) a one-off "when you do this, this happens" card the first time a card lands on a role'd list (Awaiting Deposit / Upcoming Jobs), with "Don't show again" per user; (b) a "Take a tour" button on the Jobs page, auto-opens on first visit, walking lists, lock popover, drag, drawer (Book job, Deposit received, pills), calendar link. Reuse the pattern for Calendar, Agents, Settings later. Raised because a tenant who drags a card and suddenly sees it on the calendar has no idea why.

Medium (every click, every save).** Found by S454 measurement: 5 serial trips in middleware before any action starts (auth getUser, memberships, tenant_config onboarding, billing gate, isReadOnly re-reading tenant_billing). One billing read + parallel onboarding/billing checks saves 2 to 3 trips on every request.

Low until a bar touches the home indicator.** S455's phone save bar has safe-area padding that resolves to zero because the viewport meta does not opt in. Checked on the operator's phone: not touching today. App-wide change, every fixed-bottom element must be checked when it lands.

## New items 2026-10-07
- **F74. NEW 2026-10-07. Editable booking confirmation + optional lines. OPEN. Design chat first, then one session.** Found verifying F72: the Book job gate blocks a send on ANY blank tag the template uses (vehicles, team, delivery_date), and a tenant cannot add a one-off line to a confirmation without editing the template. Agreed shape, no LLM: (a) the Book job preview becomes editable in place, tags already resolved, edits apply to this send only, template untouched, the edited body is what the activity row records; (b) blank-tag rule: a line whose tags all resolve empty is dropped from the render, the gate becomes a warning "These lines will be left out: ..." with Send anyway / Add details; a line mixing a blank tag with real content still blocks (dropping it would lose real content). Not a Cornwall gate (the manager types the values); a stranger would hit it on day one.

The preview (S147) renders the signature region only when the marker exists, so a tenant can see it is missing but is not told.

## Candidate decisions raised 2026-10-07
- **Candidate DEC63 (NOT locked). One card per job.** A multi-day job is one card with an optional delivery day (`delivery_date` + delivery arrival) and a second job event derived from it; never a second card. Two cards would split deposit, quote link, history and the confirmation. Raised and built as F72 on 2026-10-07; lock if the Cornwall seed and first real two-day booking confirm the shape.

## New items 2026-10-08

- **S482. NEW 2026-10-08. iOS shows no scroll indicator on the Jobs card stack. OPEN. Low, S479 residual.** After S479 the cards scroll inside `.card-stack` and Add card stays pinned, but on the operator's iPhone no overlay indicator appears while scrolling, so there is no feel for how far a long list goes. `scrollbar-width:thin` is desktop-only; iOS ignores it. A custom indicator means JS (measure scrollHeight, draw a track, fade on scroll). Decide whether it is worth it once Cornwall has real list lengths; do not build on a guess.
- **S483. NEW 2026-10-08. Quote Engine: create a job card from the quote. OPEN. Small to moderate.** Sid: "turn into job card" at the bottom of the quote engine. S372 gave the reverse (Load from job card). Needed: a button on the Quote summary that creates a card on the tenant's Jobs board (which list: ask, or the first non-locked list), links the quote (`cards.quote_id`), copies customer name, addresses, move date and quoted total; if a card already holds this quote, open it instead of creating a second. Check first whether any quote-to-card path exists (F71 creates cards from enquiries, not quotes). Pair with S423 (address edits after save) since both touch the saved quote.
- **F76. NEW 2026-10-08. Extra collection and delivery days per job. OPEN. Design chat first (DEC63 revisit), then one session.** Sid's note read as a "multi-day" label and checkbox but the intention is jobs spanning three or more days with additional collection or delivery days, not only the one `delivery_date` F72 added. Shape to design: a dated list of job days on the card (type collection | delivery | other, date, arrival window) replacing `delivery_date` + `delivery_arrival_*` or layering on top of it; one calendar event per day (today F72 derives two from the card, `source_subtype` job / job_delivery); booking-confirmation tags per day and how the template lists N days (F74 interacts: editable preview could absorb odd cases); the Change date and rebooked prompts per day; drawer UI on phone. Candidate DEC63 (one card per job) holds if the days live on the card. Cornwall today: two-day jobs only, so F72 covers the live need; F76 is for the general case.
- **F77. NEW 2026-10-08. Escalated reply: draft with AI, or send to the Review Queue. OPEN. Design chat first.** Sid: the Escalated tab reply box (F34) is a blank composer; he wants either an LLM-drafted reply to edit, or to push the enquiry to the Review Queue for a draft. Two routes, flagged now: (a) "Draft with AI" inside the composer, one LLM call from the enquiry body + voice + signature, nothing stored until Send, same transport as today; simple and safe. (b) Send to Review Queue creates an `agent_review_queue` row with `send_after`, which the auto-send cron will fire unless the row is marked manual-only; DEC62-class risk (customer email leaving without explicit action) and a second draft path through computeDraft on an already-escalated run. Lean (a); (b) only with an explicit never-auto-send flag on the row. Thread replies (S202) are the common escalation, so the draft prompt needs the thread context F11 already stores.

## Decisions locked 2026-10-09
- **DEC64. LOCKED 2026-10-09 (F78). Enquiry routes: business email first, mailbox decides the door, BCC off by default.** Two onboarding questions. Q1 "Do you have a business email address?" Yes means connect that mailbox (IMAP today, OAuth adapters later); No means customers use the tenant's Movers App address and nothing is connected. Q2 "Where do you want to manage enquiries?" In the app (recommended) or In your email inbox; inbox needs a business email. A healthy connected mailbox is read in BOTH modes and is the door; the Movers App address is the door only when no healthy mailbox exists (reverses DEC40, restores DEC37's rule without the toggle trap). Sent-folder writeback and filing apply in both modes, booking confirmations included. BCC copies of sent mail are OFF by default; opt-in setting "Email me a copy of everything the app sends" with an optional different address that must confirm by link; never to the generic sender. The setup checklist, the test enquiry address and detection, and the Agents Setup cards all follow the two answers. **Rejected:** forwarding the business email to the Movers App address as a route (one-way, an extra step for a non-technical tenant, no filing, Gmail confirmation code lands in the app); built on the morning of 10-09 and removed the same day.

Changes only the apex MX; `inbound.` and `send.` records untouched.
- **S487. NEW 2026-10-09. Onboarding Quoting step on resume: options locked and "Routes and survey rules can't be changed here yet" shown to the tenant. OPEN. Small, cosmetic.** Seen 10-09 re-entering guided setup with `?resume=1`. The note means nothing to a tenant and the boxes cannot be selected, so they Skip. Either make the step editable on resume or hide it with a plain line. Fold into the next onboarding review.
- **F79. NEW 2026-10-09. Inbound mailbox connect guide, per provider, like F20 for DNS. OPEN. Feature. Launch-relevant for trial conversion.** F78 makes connecting the mailbox the path for every tenant with a business email, in both modes, and the Receiving enquiries form has no instructions at all. Per-provider steps for the stall point, "how to get an app password" (Hostinger, IONOS, Gmail, Outlook/Microsoft 365, 123-reg, Fasthosts, cPanel), host/port autodetect (S439), "send this to your web person" (S438) and the Gmail/Microsoft sign-in buttons (F15 adapters 2/3) all hang off it. Own design session first.

## Amendments to existing entries 2026-10-09
- **F9 (setup checklist):** F78 adds "Choose how enquiries reach you" (counted, done when both answers exist) and "Connect your inbox" (counted, business email Yes and no healthy mailbox); "Send yourself a test enquiry" targets the address customers will use and only ticks for a test that arrived that way (old mismatched ticks do not count); "Set up email sending" is non-counted "(optional)" without a business email. Cornwall's checklist vanished on 10-08 because every counted row was done while no mailbox was connected; F78 is the fix.
- **F12(b) / S369:** address and detection now follow the F78 route (business email Yes: contact email, mailbox-sourced; No: Movers App address, resend_inbound).
- **F20 (DNS guide):** F75 pre-selects the detected DNS host, replaces "registrar" wording with "where your domain's DNS is managed", adds Back to Agents top and bottom, and the pending copy tells the tenant they can leave the page.
- **S438 / S439 / S440:** now hang off F79.
- **S221 / DEC42 (Postmark):** S478 adds `deletePostmarkDomain` and the one-domain-per-tenant rule; Postmark domains are still looked up by name but never reused unless the same tenant owns the row.
- **Quirks:** section 27 added (sending identity, DNS hosts, Domain Connect onboarding, preview protection, Resend bounce on self-BCC, owner-only guided setup).
